  ~17:23Z).** The live proof on s2: a real manager question produced a streamed reply and the
  guard logged `hook:reply_payload_sending, chars:3296, flags:[]` — full content on the exact
  path 2026.5.27 was deaf to (the previous lines in the same log are 22-char stubs). The canary
  cleared all three risks the 6.x changelog named for our topology: `inference.local` model
  calls (real GLM turn), doorman MCP over `host.openshell.internal` (badge-authenticated
  spine round-trip), and `openshell:resolve:env` secret resolution (Telegram polling resumed);
  deny lists intact both layers, all 10 plugins load, `doctor --fix` clean. **Upgrade recipe**
  (sandbox egress blocks npm registry; the HOST has node but no npm): curl the tarball on the
  host and verify the registry shasum → bootstrap npm-cli itself from a tarball → stage
  `npm i -g --prefix /tmp/oc-stage` on the host (linux-x64 = correct platform for native deps)
  → `mv` the container's install to `openclaw-<old>.bak` (instant rollback) → `docker cp` the
  staged tree in → `nemoclaw <s> doctor --fix` → `gateway restart` → verify the startup plugins
  line has NO "unknown typed hook" warning. Pre-upgrade state tarballs live in
  `~/upgrade-backups/` on the box. **FLEET UNIFORM as of 2026-08-12 ~18:10Z (owner call: "just
  roll out"):** all six sandboxes on 2026.6.34 + guard v0.3.0, verified per-box (version, guard
  in the startup plugin list, polling, no unknown-hook warning since upgrade). s3 got the guard
  for the FIRST time — it was skipped in the v0.1.0 deploy. s7 re-hit its known perms trap
  during install (`/sandbox/.openclaw` 700 vs 2770, `openclaw.json` 600 vs 660 →
  GATEWAY_UNSAFE_CONFIG_PATH, gateway briefly DOWN) — third occurrence on s7; the chmod
  2770/660 + chown + doctor --fix + restart recipe cleared it, but whatever keeps resetting
  s7's perms is still unexplained. Remaining hole: the image still ships 2026.5.27, so a
  recycled container silently re-deafens — PR #125 (bootstrap ocpin/wire/verify pin) closes it;
  deploy the merged bootstrap to the box (incl. /usr/local/sbin copy) and run verify (expect
  6× ok). The flag-rate clock runs from the 17:23Z verified-live line — measure it with
  `node scripts/guard-rate/report.mjs` (PR #127): dup-less lines only, `corrected` flags split
  out (self-fixes never trigger act-mode), and a DEAF-GUARD alarm (exit 2) when a box has
  outbound traffic but no guard lines in 24h, because a rate over a deaf window is no number.
  Checkpoints — the owner delegated remembering this playbook; whoever is here on these dates
  executes it without asking them to re-explain:
  - **~2026-08-19, mid-window liveness — protect the evidence, decide nothing.** Healthy →
    the back half of the window is trustworthy, stop. DEAF → fix the wiring (startup plugins
    line, perms 2770/660), restart the clock; one week lost instead of two — which is the whole
    point of checking mid-window.
  - **~2026-08-26, the act-mode decision — spend the evidence.** WHETHER is settled by the
    never-a-human-verification-layer doctrine above: any uncorrected flag gets
    cancelled-and-retried, not delivered. The rate decides HOW: low (a flag every few days) →
    retry suffices, build phase-2 as designed; high (several/day) → retry would paper over an
    upstream regression — find and fix that first; zero on healthy coverage → upstream holds,
    ship act-mode anyway as cheap insurance. The corrected-flag count is its own series
    (self-corrections) — reported, never acted on.
  Ops note from a false
  alarm during verification: a streamed reply writes its guard line at delivery END — after
  ~20+ `editMessageText` calls — so check the log file's mtime against the edit burst before
  declaring the hook dead.
- **Guard v0.3.0 fixes both eval-found detector defects (2026-08-12, PR #124).** D17, the one
  miss in 94 adversarial replies: "Saturday (August 16th)" — parentheses split chrono's parse so
  the pair was never checked; a pre-pass unwraps `Weekday (DATE)` to the comma form ONLY when
  the whole parenthetical looks like a date (month+day, ordinal, or "the N" — bare digits like
  "Friday (2)" are counts and stay untouched). T02, the one act-mode false-fire: a wrong pair
  QUOTED inside a correct correction («If we said "Friday August 20," that's a mistake — the
  20th is Thursday») — cancel-and-retry would cancel correct behavior; when the same message
  also states the CORRECT pair for the same date, its flags get `corrected: true` — logged for
  the rate, but **act-mode must trigger only on flags without `corrected`** (and rate analysis
  should track corrected flags as their own series: self-corrections, not fabrications).
  Regression replay of all 94 eval replies old-vs-new: exactly two changed outcomes (D17 gains
  its flag, T02's becomes corrected), the other 92 byte-identical. **DEPLOYED s2+s6 2026-08-12
  17:53Z and liveness-verified on a real streamed reply** (`v:0.3.0 hook:reply_payload_sending
  chars:1148 flags:[]`, stub dedupe marking `dup:true` as designed); s3/s5/s7/s8 get v0.3.0
  bundled with their 2026.6.34 upgrade so each box restarts once. **v0.4.0 (2026-08-14) adds
  the LINK guard**: `scanLinks` flags any absolute URL in an outbound reply whose host is
  outside the tool-sourced allowlist (board host from `BOARD_PUBLIC_URL`, `t.me`, Google Maps
  deep links; widen via `TRADES_LINK_GUARD_ALLOW`) — observe-only (`linkFlags` in the same
  jsonl record), motivated by spine-eval S04 inventing `dispatch.evalmain.com`. NOT YET
  DEPLOYED to any sandbox — code + mirror tests in-repo only; deploy = the standard
  plugins-install → chmod → doctor → restart sequence, per sandbox.
- **The weekday fabrication is ENDEMIC, not episodic — 23 mismatches in 419 historical texts
  (2026-08-06).** Built the outbound weekday guard (observe-only `message_sending` plugin,
  chrono-node, see nemoclaw-tech-agent.md §4c) and replayed it over every assistant text in the
  fleet's transcripts before deploying. Expected to confirm the 3 failures found by hand; found
  23, **all verified true positives, 0 false positives** — ~4% of assistant texts state a
  (weekday, date) pair that is internally inconsistent. Reach: a slot-proposal list with FIVE
  consecutive wrong labels (one wrong anchor, derived down the list), a freeze proposal
  ("Sat Aug 9" — a Sunday), a customer-facing date change ("Friday August 17" — a Monday), and
  the ORIGINAL July 17/18 "this Friday" booking incident, rediscovered in the transcripts by the
  detector. Two conclusions worth carrying: (a) "resolve_date worked fine before query_spine" was
  survivorship bias — the derivation failures were always there, in lists and confirmations nobody
  re-checked; a detector, not more instructions, is what made them visible. (b) A GitHub survey
  first (Guardrails hub's 40 validators, NeMo-Guardrails, code search) found NO existing
  temporal-consistency validator — the primitive that makes ours cheap is chrono-node's
  stated-vs-inferred (`isCertain`) distinction, which is what holds false positives at zero:
  flag only when the text states BOTH halves of the pair. The concurrent-feature
  workflow puts whole extra checkouts at `.claude/worktrees/<name>`, each with its own
  `tsconfig.json`. typescript-eslint then finds multiple candidate `tsconfigRootDir`s and refuses to
  parse ANY file: 289 errors, none real, on files the worktree has nothing to do with. Fixed by
  ignoring `.claude/` and pinning `tsconfigRootDir: import.meta.dirname` in `eslint.config.js`.
  Worth knowing because the failure looks catastrophic and is purely environmental — and because it
  was masking a genuine `no-unused-vars` error in the suite.
- **The date eval is phase-attributed, and the four phases are the unit of diagnosis
  (2026-08-11, `scripts/date-eval/`, README there).** "Is the agent good at dates?" is not
  answerable as one number: a date passes through RESOLVE (call `resolve_date`, don't do the math
  in-head) → USE (carry it into the query window intact) → SPEAK (state it back with its weekday,
  the two agreeing) → GUARD (catch a bad pair on the way out), and each fails differently and
  needs a different fix. The harness tags every question with the phases it exercises, reads the
  session **trajectory** (tool calls, arguments, results, per-message timestamps — at
  `/sandbox/.openclaw/agents/main/sessions/<sid>.jsonl` inside the container) rather than the
  final text alone, and attributes each failure to the EARLIEST failing phase so an upstream bad
  date is not also counted as three downstream failures. The load-bearing split: `resolve` is
  reported as **machinery** (tool asked correctly, answered wrongly — the signal a new date
  implementation is broken) vs **model** (asked for the wrong day, or called nobody), decided by
  recomputing every call independently. That independent date math must never import
  `dateResolve.ts` — sharing it makes the check agree with the machinery exactly when the
  machinery is wrong. Truth is derived from the live DB at plan time, so a schedule shift moves
  the answers with it. Same trajectory timestamps give the latency split (measured on 3 probes:
  resolve ~4.6s, use ~2.9s, speak ~5.3s of a ~12.8s full-pipeline turn, plus ~10s of CLI boot
  that is harness overhead, not the agent). Gates: `--yes` required to spend, model verified
  before the first question, and the inference route re-read after EVERY question so a mid-run
  failover **aborts** instead of silently mixing two models into one number.
- **`resolve_date` has NO month / quarter / year mode, and the doctrine tells the agent to use it
  anyway (found 2026-08-11).** The tool takes `dayShift` (±366), `weekday`+`weeksAhead` (0–52,
  FORWARD only), `explicitDate`, and `week` (0–52, FORWARD only — so "last week" has no path
  either). Nothing expresses a calendar month, quarter or year. Yet `query_spine`'s own tool
  description instructs: *for "the last 30 days", "since May", "so far this month" — call
  resolve_date FIRST and paste the dates it returns.* For every period above a week that
  instruction is **unsatisfiable**, and the agent does the only thing left: it invents the
  boundary. Live probes on s2 — *"how many jobs did we complete last month?"* → **zero
  resolve_date calls**, `'2026-07-01'`/`'2026-08-01'` written straight into the SQL; *"so far this
  month"* → resolved today via `dayShift:0`, then hand-wrote `'2026-08-01'`. Both answers were
  correct, which is the trap: August is an easy month. A February, a 31-day month, or a January
  (where "last month" is December of the PREVIOUS year) are not, and **the weekday guard cannot
  catch this class at all** — a wrong boundary yields a plausible number carrying no weekday claim
  to contradict. Rolling windows are fine ("the last 30 days" is exactly `dayShift:-30`); it is
  CALENDAR periods that have no path. Fix shape (not yet decided): period modes mirroring `week`,
  with negatives allowed for past periods. Until then treat any month/quarter/year answer as
  unverified, and see the `period` questions (M01–M11) in `scripts/date-eval/`, which mark these
  `periodBoundary` so hand-written boundaries are counted as a machinery gap rather than charged
  to the model — prompting cannot close it. Related testability limit: the pilot holds ~2 months
  of visits, so six-month aggregation is only testable for range honesty until history is seeded.
  **CLOSED 2026-08-12 (PR #122):** `resolve_date` gained `month`/`quarter`/`year` modes
  (calendar-anchored, 0 = current, negative = past — "last month" in January correctly lands in
  December of the PREVIOUS year), plus negative `week` ("last week" = -1) and negative
  `weeksAhead` ("last Friday" = -1, anchored one whole week before the coming occurrence). All
  three periods return one `resolved_period` shape: `periodStart`/`periodEnd` with weekdays, a
  human `label`, and a quotable `phrase`. The eval harness recomputes the new modes independently
  (`corpus.mjs`, still importing nothing from `dateResolve.ts`), so the next run grades them
  machinery-vs-model instead of tagging `periodBoundary`.
- **v_schedule counted cancelled visits as booked work — every agent count was inflated by a
  constant (found by the 2026-08-12 eval, fixed in PR #121).** The eval's count questions came
  back August 234 vs truth 144 (+90), this-week 82 vs 57 (+25), last-30-days 57 vs 27 (+30) — and
  the offsets are EXACTLY the cancelled rows in each window (verified per-status against the
  pilot). Root cause: the view's only predicate was the tenant GUC; no status filter, so
  `count(*)` counted every assignment ever made. The agent's SQL was clean — the VIEW lied, which
  is the worst variant: `get_team_schedule` filters to confirmed, so two tools gave two different
  answers to the same question. This class is guard-proof (a plausible number, no weekday claim)
  and compounds forever — cancel-and-rebook creates cancelled rows continuously (94 on the pilot,
  grown organically Jul 15–Aug 4). Fix: `v_schedule` now means the schedule
  (`status in ('confirmed','completed')`); cancelled rows move to `v_cancelled_visits` (same
  shape + `reason`, `updated_at`) so cancellation questions stay answerable explicitly;
  `proposed` is visible in NO view (staged workflow state, not bookings). Lesson, same family as
  local_day/local_weekday: when the agent must compose the query itself, the DEFAULT reading of
  the surface has to be the correct one — semantics the agent needs must live in the view, not in
  an instruction to remember a WHERE clause.
- **`get_customer_context` answered "no visits scheduled" over four confirmed bookings — the
  2026-08-13 eval's whole `specifics` category, 8 of its 11 real failures, one blind spot.** The
  context aggregate read only the `visits` table, which is L2 evidence of PAST work; bookings
  live in `assignments`. So "when is X's next visit?" answered "none" for any customer whose next
  work was booked but not yet done — wrong in the dangerous direction, and on every surface that
  briefs from the shared context (`get_customer_context`, `brief_customer`,
  `answer_customer_question`). Scheduling windows sitting on UNPLACED rows (plan 4b-2) were
  equally invisible: four "what's the scheduling window?" questions answered "can't compute, no
  duration" while the window sat in `assignments.earliest_date/latest_date` the whole time. The
  trajectory pair that pinned it: S05 answered correctly ONLY because the model chose a third
  call (`query_spine`); S06 stopped at `get_customer_context` and relayed the empty read —
  whether a manager heard the truth was a coin flip on an optional tool call. Fix (same PR):
  `CustomerContext.upcomingVisits` — placed rows from local-today forward plus live unplaced
  windows, tenant-scoped via `listUpcomingAssignmentsForCustomer` with a two-tenant leak test,
  rendered in the naive-local display convention (slices, never tz conversion). Lesson, the
  v_schedule family again: **a read surface that silently omits a category the question is about
  is worse than an error — the model relays the empty result with full confidence.**
- **The model hand-counts rows and gets it wrong — `get_team_schedule` now returns
  `counts.byDay`/`counts.total`, server-computed (2026-08-13 eval, W13).** Asked "more visits
  this week or next?", the model tallied the stop rows by eye: 58/70 against the served 57/71 —
  and gave the SAME Wednesday two different counts in two adjacent answers (16 in W10, 15 in
  W13). Counting is arithmetic; the resolve_date doctrine ("you are unreliable at calendar math")
  applies to tallying too. The response now carries counts computed from the same rows it lists,
  the description orders the model to use them verbatim, and timeTbd stops are LISTED but never
  COUNTED (a windowed visit is not ON a day until placed — the same distinction that made the
  eval's T07 "free weekday" question ambiguous until its phrasing pinned it).
- **GLM passed the date eval 100% (2026-08-13, v3 run): 94/94, zero stalls, zero delivered
  weekday-date contradictions — the arc was 86% (morning) → 97% (post-#133) → 100% (post-#134),
  and every point of it came from mechanism, not prompting.** What closed the last three: served
  `phrase` labels on upcomingVisits (the model mislabeled a served date exactly once — never
  again once it had a phrase to say verbatim), and the YOUR-CLOCK-IS-WRONG warning atop
  resolve_date/query_spine — the ONLY server-side surface a turn sees before its first tool
  call. Standing lessons: **the evening (post-5pm PDT) is the adversarial regime** — message
  timestamps are UTC and name tomorrow, so mid-day eval runs structurally cannot see the
  UTC-today failure class; run evals in the evening. **Same-day re-runs contaminate** unless the
  session id carries a per-run nonce (run.mjs .runid — replies otherwise CONTINUE the earlier
  conversation). `scripts/date-eval/adjudicate-db.mjs` makes the adjudication mechanical.
  PARKED: the v2 run delivered three weekday-date mismatches with ZERO guard flags — real
  weekday-guard misses, not yet investigated.
  **Midnight-straddle trap (2026-08-15 Nebius run):** the stale-manifest guard checks only at
  START — a run that CROSSES firm-local midnight grades its tail against yesterday's truth
  (59/93 void; the agent's "Nope — it's Saturday the 15th" was scored wrong while being right
  on the wall clock). Never start a run within ~1h of firm-local midnight; real fix =
  per-question day check in run.mjs. Sibling gap, same run: run.mjs has NO per-question ssh
  timeout — D11's ssh hung 71 min AFTER the agent answered in 9s (exit 255; reply salvaged
  from trajectory as designed), and that hang is what pushed the run over midnight. Provider
  comparison mechanics: custom-typed provider profiles CANNOT serve cluster inference — use
  `--type openai --config OPENAI_BASE_URL=<url>`; pause/restore the medic around the window.
  Nebius fleet numbers (valid): agent-turn median 7.8s vs 14.1s (v3 DeepInfra); the ~14s
  CLI+ssh overhead is provider-independent.
  **Fireworks fleet run (2026-08-15 mid-day Saturday, first on the production route;
  artifacts + adjudication.md in date-eval-runs/run-2026-08-15-fireworks): 85/91 strict, 0
  machinery failures / 0 fabrications; agent-turn traj median 6.3s — fastest measured (those
  published medians are trajMs, wall ≈ +14s ssh — never cross-compare the two). First-ever
  Saturday anchor: all 6 misses are weekend-ambiguity UX, split 2 real over-clarification
  (agent asked "which week?" past an explicit disambiguator — W02 "Monday through Sunday",
  W06 "starting tomorrow"), 3 clarify-without-data (doctrine wants both readings WITH numbers,
  the shape that passed in W07-W09/W11/W12), 1 bucket-label (M09). Fleet cacheRead observed
  ~99% on turns despite no gateway prompt_cache_key — replica-luck holds for serial
  single-session traffic.**
- **Eval truth-set conventions (2026-08-13 adjudication, five grader bugs):** (1) stored times
  are NAIVE-LOCAL with a lying '+00' (see the 2026-07-22 relabeling incident) — the truth now
  renders raw wall-clock slices like every product surface; it had graded "first job at 01:30"
  against an agent correctly showing 8:30. (2) A count-0 day question graded by number-extraction
  marks HONEST answers wrong on the digits inside an address — D21 is now `kind:'empty-day'`
  (says-nothing-scheduled OR relays a window visit = correct; naming a clock time = wrong).
  (3) "Completely free day" must say whether unplaced windows count — the question now says
  "not even the flexible-window ones" and the truth skips window-covered days. (4) M09's
  current-month bucket accepts full-month AND through-today readings. (5) Two `LLM idle timeout`
  turns had CLI chrome captured as their "reply" — a stalled turn's stdout is not an answer;
  grade from the trajectory's prompt-error record instead.
  (verified 2026-08-11).** Made the agent emit "The meeting is Monday 2026-08-12" — Aug 12 is a
  Wednesday, exactly the fabricated pair the guard exists to catch — and `weekday-guard.jsonl`
  did not move. So **no CLI-driven test can measure the guard**; a run that tried would report a
  0% catch rate that is an artifact of the harness. The eval instead replays the plugin's own
  exported `scanMessage` over every collected reply, which measures the detector's LOGIC exactly
  (it caught the planted mismatch) but proves nothing about the hook being wired up in
  production — that stays a one-off Telegram check per install. **Open at time of writing:** the
  guard log has three lines, all from 2026-08-06, despite a real Telegram session on s2 on
  2026-08-11 — so the "~2 weeks of measured rate" the phase-2 cancel-retry decision waits on may
  not be accumulating at all. The plugin IS loaded (gateway startup line lists it among 10).
  Worth 30 seconds from a phone before trusting any rate derived from that log.
- **The inference failover is broken — it turns a blip into an outage and reports success
  (2026-08-06).** A ~4-minute DeepInfra unhealthy window failed the fleet over to `nvidia-prod /
  z-ai/glm-5.2`; the medic logged "FAILOVER active; will fail back automatically", but NVIDIA
  returns `400 (no body)` on a real completion, so turns died in 0.8s with
  `terminalError: non_deliverable_terminal_turn`. **Failover therefore provides NO protection** —
  it converts a recoverable provider blip into a hard outage while reporting success. Same blind
  spot as the 2026-07-28 stall: `openshell inference set` doubles as probe and switch, and it
  validates the **endpoint registration**, not a completion. The differing model ids
  (`zai-org/GLM-5.2` deepinfra vs `z-ai/glm-5.2` nvidia) are deliberate and NOT the bug — NVIDIA
  accepts the registration, then 400s the request. Suspect the rotated NVIDIA key (the old one
  lingers in `/root/s4-nvidia.env`) or model entitlement. **medic v3 — a 1-token real-completion
  probe, run against the FALLBACK before declaring failover successful — is now urgent rather than
  a nice-to-have.** *(Built 2026-08-07 — see the medic v3 entry below.)*
  Debugging path: `journalctl -u nemoclaw-inference-medic` gives the failover timestamp; the real
  error is only in the sandbox's `/sandbox/.openclaw/logs/gateway-persistent.log`
  (`embedded run agent end: isError=true … error=400`). The trajectory says only
  `non_deliverable_terminal_turn`, which is useless alone. Route changes apply WITHOUT a container
  restart (the 19:52:53 turn used the route set at 19:52:14) — unlike credentials, which need one.
- **medic v3 SHIPPED (2026-08-07): no route is healthy, and no failover is successful, without a
  completion actually coming back.** v2's probe (`openshell inference set`) validates endpoint
  REGISTRATION only — the blind spot behind both the 07-28 "route OK" mid-stall and the 08-06
  "FAILOVER active" onto a 400-ing NVIDIA. v3 keeps the registration gate and adds a **1-token
  completion through the managed route** (`https://inference.local/v1/chat/completions`, curl'd
  from inside a running sandbox container): the exact path agent turns take, gateway-injected
  credentials, so **no provider key ever leaves the store**. The gateway forces the model id per
  request, so the probe follows whatever route was just set — which is how the FALLBACK gets
  completion-verified before failover is declared. Decision table locked by
  `src/control/inferenceMedicScript.test.ts` (PATH shims for openshell/docker; both live
  incidents are named test cases). Accepted trade-offs, documented in the script: a ~30s flap
  window per tick during a registration-ok/completions-dead deepinfra stall (v2 parked on the
  dead primary permanently, so strictly better); and with NO sandbox container running the
  completion gate degrades to v2 semantics with a loud WARN (a box mid-maintenance must not mark
  healthy providers dead). Bash traps hit while building, worth remembering: `$?` after a bare
  `if cmd; then …; fi` with no else is the IF's status (0), not cmd's; and re-enabling `set -e`
  inside a function overrides the caller's `set +e` wrapper, turning `return 1` into a script
  exit — use `cmd && rc=0 || rc=$?`.
  **Deploy postscript (2026-08-11): the probe MUST run `docker exec -u sandbox <cid> sh -lc`.**
  The sandbox's egress environment (proxy/DNS for the container's policy rig) is assembled in the
  sandbox user's LOGIN profile; a bare root `docker exec curl` gets an EMPTY reply from a healthy
  provider. v3's first live run declared a false BOTH-DEAD exactly this way — pre-deploy testing
  had used `nemoclaw exec` (which logs in as sandbox), so the script's bare `docker exec` was
  never the tested path. The containment branch worked as designed (route restored to primary,
  exit 1 loud, zero agent harm, and no timer tick ever ran the broken build). The test shim now
  refuses to answer a probe not invoked as `-u sandbox` + `sh -lc`, so the invocation shape fails
  the suite, not the fleet. General rule: `nemoclaw exec` ≠ `docker exec` — anything needing the
  sandbox's network posture needs the sandbox user's login shell.
- **medic v4+v5 are DEPLOYED as of 2026-08-13 19:54:23Z — the NVIDIA failover can no longer fire.**
  Verified the way this repo requires (md5 of the path the systemd unit actually runs, never a
  sibling copy): `/usr/local/sbin/nemoclaw-inference-medic.sh` = `ae3787ed0ccf08485de26cde8f114b3f`,
  244 lines, byte-identical to `Box_Setup_Stuff/`. Every surviving `FALLBACK`/`nvidia-prod` string is
  a COMMENT; no executable line references either. The first tick 3s later identified the new build
  by its wording — `route OK on deepinfra-prod / zai-org/GLM-5.2 — **serving verified (tool call
  returned)**`, where v3 said "completion verified". **The last two windows the old failover ever
  caused were 2026-08-13 09:11:56 and 11:48:12**, each routing the whole fleet to a provider that
  400s real turns while logging "COMPLETION-VERIFIED". Worth noting how close this came to being
  recorded wrongly: a check at 19:45 found the Aug-11 build and reported "not deployed", the deploy
  landed at 19:54, and only re-checking on the user's say-so caught it — **a deploy claim is only
  true at the moment it is measured.** **The one remaining gap is ALERTING**: the medic now tells the
  truth into a journal nobody reads.
- **medic v4 (2026-08-12): a probe that doesn't look like a TURN can't tell you a provider serves
  turns.** v3 closed "is it registered?" but its probe was `{"content":"hi"}, max_tokens:1` — no
  tools, no streaming, one token. `nvidia-prod` served that happily while returning `400 (no body)`
  on every real agent turn, so the medic logged **"FAILOVER active — COMPLETION-VERIFIED" over a
  hard outage**: 10 failovers 08-10→08-12 and **6 events on 08-12 alone** (05:19, 05:24, 20:58
  failover; 14:04, 16:48, 18:00 both-dead). Each is a ~4-min window where every user message
  returns "LLM request failed" while the journal says success. The user hit one at 20:59/21:00 and
  reasonably blamed `--deliver`; it was the route. **Silence is the damage — the one signal said
  the opposite of the truth.** v4's probe carries real spine tool schemas (flat + nested +
  enum-heavy, 864 prompt tokens), a system prompt, streaming on, and `max_tokens:512`; the gate is
  the stream closing (`data: [DONE]`) **with `finish_reason:"tool_calls"`** — an actual tool call,
  the thing every real turn depends on. Measured before shipping on healthy deepinfra: **12/12
  emitted a tool call, 1.9–9.9s, ~$0.0003/probe (~$2.70/mo at the 5-min cadence)** — so the strong
  probe was affordable and there was no reason to settle for a weaker one. Per-run usage, measured:
  **864 prompt + 43–66 completion ≈ 920 tokens** (768 of the prompt cache-hit), ~264k tokens/day at
  288 runs; a FAILING tick costs up to 4 probes (retry + fallback) ≈ 3.7k. The DeepInfra prefix
  cache was still hitting at a 150s gap, which does NOT match the "TTL ~1min" recorded in
  [[glm-cache-key-trial]] — treat that TTL as unverified.
  Calibration results worth keeping:
  - **`tool_choice:"required"` does NOT rescue a small token budget.** GLM-5.2 reasons by default
    and spent all 32 tokens on `reasoning_content`, returning `tool_calls: []` +
    `finish_reason:"length"`. Token *headroom* is what makes tool emission observable, not
    `tool_choice`. (Consistent with the standing "GLM thinks by default" finding.)
  - **Retry once before failing over.** The v4 probe is heavier, so one slow response on a HEALTHY
    provider is plausible; flapping the fleet for that would be the same bug pointed the other way.
    Two consecutive misses is evidence, one is noise. In-tick only — no state file, keeping v2's
    "flaps are visible in the journal" rule.
  - **Payload SIZE is the untested axis.** Three tools = 864 prompt tokens vs the real ~39-tool
    surface. That exercises schema *shape*, not size; if a provider ever fails on size, that's the
    knob. Also: the NVIDIA-as-oracle test (route the fleet at it and confirm the new gate fails)
    was **never run** — it needs a live outage window. The gate was proven instead against a
    synthesized prose-no-tool-call response, which v3's gate passes and v4's rejects.
  - **The tool-emission gate is an assumption about the MODEL, and it is the acceptance test for
    swapping the fallback.** `nvidia-prod` was a testing stopgap, never a product decision, and is
    due to be replaced. Point `FALLBACK_PROVIDER` at something new **only after** probing it
    directly and confirming `finish_reason:"tool_calls"` — a model that answers the date in prose
    would be marked dead while perfectly healthy.
  Both stale "every 15 min" mentions in `doorman-architecture-memory.md` / the setup guide were
  corrected to the real cadence (5 min) in the same change.
- **medic v5 (2026-08-12, user decision): the fallback is DELETED, and the real lesson is that
  nobody ever decided to have one.** Asked why the probe needed tools at all if the medic's job is
  just "is DeepInfra set", the honest answer was: a health probe only earns its cost if something
  ACTS on the verdict, and the only actor was a failover to a provider being retired. Tracing it
  back: nvidia-prod was the ORIGINAL provider (2026-07-02); the 07-06 DeepInfra swap kept it as a
  **manual rollback**; medic v2 on 07-11 wired that manual rollback up as an **automatic failover
  target** — no decision, no verification, and the NVIDIA key was rotated that same day. By 08-06
  it 400'd every real turn. **A break-glass escape hatch was promoted to a live safety net by
  accident, and an unverified fallback is worse than none: it fails exactly when you depend on it
  and reports success while doing it.** v5 asserts the route and reports; it never reroutes.
  - **Deleting the fallback gets most of v4's benefit for free.** v4's value was "stop steering
    traffic onto a dead provider" — with no failover branch, that is structural rather than
    probe-dependent. What v4 still buys is an honest signal.
  - **The turn-shaped probe is KEPT anyway, and the reasoning inverts.** With nothing to reroute
    to, the probe's worst case is crying wolf in a log, so the false-failover risk that would
    argue for a gentler gate is gone. What remains is the signal — and that is the point, because
    it is what an alert should fire on. The retry now exists to prevent false ALARMS (an alert
    that cries wolf gets ignored), not false failovers.
  - **On a probe failure the route is still ASSERTED, not abandoned** — DeepInfra outages have
    self-healed every time, so holding the route means recovery is picked up by live turns
    immediately instead of at the next tick.
  - Pinned by a new invariant test: **"NEVER routes anywhere but the primary, whatever fails"**,
    plus one asserting the output never says FAILOVER or names a fallback. Re-adding a fallback is
    then a deliberate change that must also change those tests — it cannot quietly reappear.
  - **The remaining gap was ALERTING** — the medic told the truth into a journal nobody reads,
    which is precisely why six outage windows on 08-12 went unnoticed. An honest red light in an
    empty room is barely better than a green one. Closed by the entry below.
- **Ops alerting: the verdict now leaves the box (2026-08-12).** `POST /control/ops` on Render →
  `ops_events`, fed by `ops-report.sh` wired to the medic unit's `OnFailure=`/`OnSuccess=`.
  Design points that carry beyond this feature:
  - **ONE ROW PER INCIDENT, NOT PER TICK.** The medic runs every 5 min, so a 30-minute outage is
    six identical observations. The reporter is STATELESS — it says what is true now, every tick —
    and the SERVER folds those into transitions (open on the first bad report, close on recovery).
    Storing every observation would make it a log rather than an incident list, and any notifier
    built on it would fire six times and get muted. **Suppression is the feature, not an
    optimisation.**
  - **The state lives on the server precisely BECAUSE the medic must stay stateless.** v2's
    no-state-files rule exists so flaps stay visible in the journal; dedup needs state to compare
    against; the database is where state belongs. Bonus: a dropped report is harmless and the box
    needs no retry logic.
  - **A partial unique index (`where closed_at is null`) is the real guarantee**, not the
    read-then-insert. Two reports racing on a flapping provider would otherwise both see "nothing
    open" and both insert — double-counting exactly during the noisiest moment. The insert catches
    23505 and reports the existing incident rather than erroring.
  - **Alerting must never break what it watches.** The reporter is a SEPARATE systemd unit (not a
    call inside the medic), ALWAYS exits 0, and caps curl at 5s. Verified with the collector down:
    fails in 0.13s, exit 0.
  - **`host_id` comes from the authenticated TOKEN, never the body** — otherwise one host could
    file incidents against another. Pinned by a test.
  - **The body is CAPPED (64 KiB) where the sibling control routes are not**: it carries a journal
    tail whose size is set by however much a failing unit logged, and `readBody` is uncapped. Note
    the cap destroys the socket, so the client sees a reset rather than a 413 — that is
    `readRawBody`'s existing behaviour, shared with the phone webhook paths.
  - **Credential trap:** it reuses the badge pull's `HOST_PULL_TOKEN`/`PULL_URL` from
    `/etc/doorman/badge-pull.env` (no new secret). That file is `0600 root:root` and the unit runs
    as `ubuntu`, so systemd injects it via `EnvironmentFile=`. **Do NOT chmod the file, and do NOT
    run the reporter as root** — `openshell` is ubuntu's install, so root would get a different
    HOME/config and the route lookup would come back silently empty (same class as the medic's
    `-u sandbox` login-shell requirement).
  - **STILL A STORE, NOT AN ALARM.** Nothing notifies yet — deferred by user decision until a bot
    is paired. It makes an outage reconstructable afterwards, not noticed as it happens. Do not
    mistake the table existing for the problem being solved.
- **Sandbox chat sessions NEVER auto-reset — and the verbatim memory is the transcript file, not
  the model (verified 2026-08-15: vendor docs + live s2 probe).** Our sandboxes set no
  `session.reset` (`openclaw config get session.reset` → path not found on s2) and the vendor
  default is `none`, so a person's Telegram session persists until a manual `/new` or maintenance
  pruning (stale sessions pruned after ~30 days). Long sessions **compact** in the live context —
  past ~50k recent tokens older turns become summaries — but the on-disk transcript
  (`/sandbox/.openclaw/agents/main/sessions/<sid>.jsonl`) stays verbatim and complete.
  Consequences: an agent can "know" things that exist nowhere but its session (the
  intelligent-spine direction's bonus class — harvest by reading transcripts, never by asking the
  agent to remember), and anything un-harvested has a ~30-day shelf life. Docs:
  https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/config-agents. Direction doc:
  `thoughts/shared/plans/2026-08-15-intelligent-spine-direction.md`.
- **The lifecycle cron has a step 8 — the Stage-0 watcher — and a cadence gate (2026-08-16,
  plan `2026-08-15-feature-A-intelligent-spine-stage0-watchers.md`).** The Render schedule is
  meant to run `*/5` (change it at rollout — the schedule setting, not code): steps 1–7 run only
  on quarter-hour ticks (`getUTCMinutes() % 15 < 5`, stateless, tolerant of delayed firings —
  off-cadence ticks report `{skipped:'off-cadence tick'}` per step), step 8 runs every tick.
  Step 8 = `runWatchers` (src/jobs/watchers.ts): deterministic overdue-confirmed-visit gap
  detection, gated on **`WATCHERS_MODE`** — unset = skipped clean (deploy-before-decide safe),
  `shadow` = counts into the JSON summary only, `live` = review items (`overdue_visit_gap`,
  once per assignment EVER) + one batched count-only manager push (needs `BOARD_BOT_TOKEN` in
  the cron env; absent → `pushSkipped`, never red), any other value = LOUD stepError. ROLLOUT
  STATE: **DEPLOYED IN SHADOW 2026-08-16** (PR #147 merged `a0a9fd0`; schedule `*/5` + cadence
  gate + shadow mode all verified from a live off-cadence log line the same day; first reading:
  `tenants:1, gapsFound:35, freshGaps:35` — tenant identity of those gaps unconfirmed, likely
  the demo world's active WA-seed schedule; identify before any live flip). Note: in pure
  shadow, `freshGaps` ALWAYS equals `gapsFound` (nothing is ever flagged, so nothing is ever
  stale) — the shadow observables are the LEVEL and its daily churn, and freshGaps-at-flip is
  the size of the first-hour burn. **Live stays a deliberate flip after volume review** — the
  archetype firing on 67/67 of the pilot's historical overdue visits, which is why the 72h
  look-back exists (the backlog must never surface) and why live is a deliberate flip, not a
  default. Escalation ladder (ratified 2026-08-16): today's manager review item is rung 2 of 2 —
  Stage 1's voice inserts the technician-first rung when it lands. Money-inference Phase 3 note:
  its quiet-open-jobs nudge must become this framework's SECOND detector (shared tenant loop,
  same step) — never a parallel copy.
- **The ladder cannot see the MONEY LEDGER — and a money-only answer leaves the nudge open**
  (defect found live 2026-08-19, NOT fixed). `hasActivitySince` builds its per-job activity map
  from `raw_captures` + `visits` only. So when an operator answers a nudge with a fact the agent
  records as MONEY (`payment_received`, `material_cost`), nothing lands in either table, the nudge
  never closes, and at `asked_at + 48h` it escalates a manager item asserting the person "hasn't
  heard back" — a FALSE statement about a named human. Same root cause in two more places: a
  manager recording the cost does not close a `job_cost_gap` (its `filled` never re-reads the
  ledger, though the DETECTOR checks it before creating), and `job_cost_gap` review items never
  auto-resolve (the open-item sweep reads `findOpenGaps(tenantId, 'overdue_visit_gap')` only).
  All three are one design problem — fix them together, not as three patches. Research:
  `thoughts/shared/research/2026-08-19-nudge-money-answer-blindspot.md`. **The live specimen FIRED
  2026-08-20 19:55:41Z** (a day early — `NUDGE_ESCALATE_HOURS` moved 48 → 24 that afternoon and the
  row was already 24.8h old). It proved BOTH halves. The escalation path is correct: nudge →
  `escalated`, review item `5927d0f8-…` opened with `escalatedFromNudge` linking back, weekday and
  date fused and firm-local. And the copy is false on both counts — *"hasn't heard back"* and
  *"nothing is on the job's record yet"* — about a person who answered and a job carrying a
  `payment_received` row. **The lesson is that nothing about that message LOOKS broken:** it is
  confident, specific and well written, and only the escape-hatch line ("If it's already handled,
  just dismiss this") keeps it recoverable. The item is open on the pilot and is safe to dismiss;
  its evidentiary job is done. The 2026-08-20 planner work added a `basis: 'record'` close for the
  PLANNER on `job_cost_gap` only (authorised by `listJobIdsWithCostEvents`, never by the model
  asserting it) — it deliberately does NOT touch the sweep's `filled`, and it does not help this
  row: a visit gap's hole is the missing write-up, which is genuinely still missing.** Separately fixed in PR #156 (they did NOT share this root cause): the
  cost detector skipped `paid` jobs entirely — `deriveStatusTarget` auto-walks a job to `paid`
  once payment clears, and the detector only looks 3h after the visit ends, so a
  paid-at-the-door job was past the filter before it ever ran — and a CORRECTED `material_cost`
  still suppressed the gap forever, so fixing a typo buried that job (`subject_ref` is the job
  and `uq_nudges_subject_ever` is any-status).

- **Stage 1 — the voice — is BUILT (2026-08-16, branch `feat/spine-voice`, plan
  `2026-08-15-feature-B-intelligent-spine-stage1-voice.md`; NOT yet deployed).** The ladder lives
  inside step 8: a fresh gap with a reachable technician (active + Telegram + sandbox + host,
  under 2 asks/person/rolling-24h — NO firm-wide cap, per-tick cap 5 is the surge brake) becomes
  a `nudges` row instead of a manager item; **`NUDGES_MODE`** gates it independently of
  WATCHERS_MODE (unset='off' = Stage-0 byte-for-byte, `shadow` counts `nudgesWouldCreate`,
  `live` runs the ladder; typo = LOUD stepError). Step 9 (`teamLeadCompose`) is the ONE model
  call: writes the question from code-built context only — time words are SERVED
  (`relativeDayPhrase`: "earlier today"/"yesterday"/"a couple of days ago"; user ruling: the
  model may say "today" only when the code's clock said so). **Ask window — RETIRED 2026-08-29,
  and compose's model call itself went in the 2026-08-24 collapse; kept for the reasoning.** (user
  ruling 2026-08-17): compose ran only 7 AM–7 PM firm-local, seven days (`insideAskWindow`) — an
  evening gap holds pending (`held` in the step summary) and composes fresh next morning. The
  gate sits on COMPOSE, not the outbox, precisely because of the served phrases: a question
  written at night and delivered at 7 AM would say "earlier today" about yesterday. Paired
  constraint: the 24h never-asked deadline absorbs the ≤12h overnight hold — a window that
  ever excludes whole DAYS must lengthen that deadline with it, or weekend-held rows expire
  to the manager unasked. Known v1 limit: a multi-hour box outage can still deliver an
  already-composed question with a stale day phrase (bounded by the 24h expiry; accepted).
  Identity `spine:team-lead` is
  dispatch-capped to read/ask (deny-by-default namespace gate + DB CHECK `ref not like
  'spine:%'` — the Composio relay routes around dispatch and keys on roster-row existence).
  Transport: `GET/POST /control/nudges` (host token) + `nudge-runner.timer` on the box (5-min,
  reuses badge-pull.env, fresh session `nudge-<id>` per ask, delivery judged by assistant TEXT
  in the trajectory, pre-check re-acks instead of re-pinging on a lost ack). **THE ORPHAN
  DOCTRINE (four adversarial reviews converged on it): any path that kills a nudge before the
  ask lands must hand the gap to the manager FIRST** — model decline, thin context, compose ×3,
  serve cap 10, delivery ×3, and the watcher's 24h never-asked deadline (covers offboarded/
  recycled/host-unbound techs, invisible to every pull) — because an ever-nudged assignment is
  never fresh again and the once-ever index blocks re-asks; a silent death = gap on NEITHER
  surface forever. Never-asked handoffs use the STAGE-0 copy (never "their assistant asked").
  Corollary: a row too tampered even to FLAG is FROZEN (left open, logged), never closed —
  closing it would be the orphan again. Rollback reality: unsetting NUDGES_MODE stops
  creation+compose only — queued rows keep serving until `systemctl disable --now
  nudge-runner.timer`; pull BOTH levers. And a WATCHERS_MODE rollback freezes ALL nudge
  sweeps (they live in step 8) while the un-gated outbox keeps serving — rolling back the
  watcher with nudges in flight requires pulling the box timer too. DEPLOY
  PREREQUISITES: watchers live-flip decision, pilot migrations (`20260817090000_nudges` +
  `20260817100000_reserve_spine_namespace`), Render deploy, runner install, and the Phase-5
  a WORKING inference key on the CRON service (presence-only check — a suspended key runs and fails 3x per nudge instead of skipping; pilot shows NO model usage since 2026-08-15 18:13, consistent with the parked Fireworks suspension), and the Phase-5 MUST-PIN: what `--deliver` reports on a FAILED Telegram send (dead chat leaves CLI success +
  trajectory text per the date-eval record — `delivered` is unearned for never-Started
  recipients until probed). Replies take the ordinary record_field_note path — that capture is
  what closes the loop.
- **Stage-1 shadow CONFIRMED on the pilot (2026-08-18T00:05Z).** Scheduled-run log:
  `"mode":"live","freshGaps":1,"itemsOpened":1,"nudgeMode":"shadow","nudgesWouldCreate":1,
  "nudgesCreated":0`, `teamLeadCompose:{"skipped":"NUDGES_MODE not 'live'"}`, ok:true — the
  ladder identified a reachable person, decided it would ask, wrote nothing, and still filed the
  manager card.
  **HOW TO READ SHADOW: `nudgesWouldCreate` is an EVENT counter, not a level.** A gap is
  surfaced once EVER, so with ~1 gap/person/day the signal is ONE non-zero tick among ~288
  zeros a day. Reading a random recent log and seeing 0 proves nothing (we nearly called it
  broken). Either catch the tick, or reconstruct from the DB: flagged `overdue_visit_gap` items
  whose `detail->>'technicianRef'` is a reachable roster row.
  **ORDERING TRAP for any synthetic test gap: create it only AFTER `NUDGES_MODE=live`.** A gap
  created while shadow is on is consumed by the manager fallback on the next tick and, by the
  once-ever rule, can never become a nudge — the test visit is burned.
- **TRAP — `nemoclaw <name> agent -m` CANNOT carry a multi-line message; every Stage-1 delivery
  failed on it (found live 2026-08-18, first real end-to-end).** `nemoclaw ... agent` forwards
  argv through `openshell sandbox exec`, which rejects any argument containing a newline:
  `command argument N (229 characters spanning 5 lines) contains a newline or carriage return,
  which OpenShell exec does not accept` — **exit 2 in ~1 second**. Our deliverable is multi-line
  by construction (question / evidence line / footer), so the CLI never even started a turn.
  **FIX (PR #150, merged as `adac0d5`; deployed to the box 2026-08-18): stage the body
  INSIDE the sandbox over stdin
  (`printf '%s' "$msg" | nemoclaw <ref> exec --no-tty -- bash -c "cat > /tmp/nudge-<sid>.txt"`)
  then pass `--message-file`** — exit 0 in 39s and the message landed in Telegram.
  **Why six review rounds and 1789 tests missed it:** nothing exercised the runner with a REAL
  message; every probe (mine included) used a single-line ASCII string, which passes. When
  testing a CLI path, reproduce the actual payload shape — newlines, unicode, length — not a
  convenient stand-in. Diagnostic note: the failing branch is invisible in the box journal
  (the script sends output to /dev/null); the reason surfaces in the nudge row's `resolution`
  (`delivery failed 3x: agent CLI exited non-zero or timed out`), and a ~1s sudo session in
  `journalctl -u nudge-runner.service` is the tell that the CLI rejected the call rather than
  ran it. **The orphan doctrine held throughout: after 3 attempts the gap was handed to the
  manager rather than lost.**
- **USER DECISION 2026-08-18 — DeepInfra is being RETIRED; Fireworks replaces its inference key.**
  NOT kept as a warm rollback, and no replacement lane (Nebius or otherwise) is being stood up:
  the fleet becomes SINGLE-PROVIDER with no fallback. The user was told that the 2026-08-15
  Fireworks spend-cap suspension (HTTP 412) was recovered in ~5 min precisely BY rolling back to
  DeepInfra, and chose it anyway — do not re-litigate. The owed work is a written, TESTED recovery
  procedure for "Fireworks is down and every technician's assistant is dead". Nothing executed yet.
  State verified on the box the same day: route still `deepinfra-prod`/`zai-org/GLM-5.2`;
  **`fireworks-prod` is ALREADY a registered provider** (`openshell provider list -g nemoclaw`,
  type openai) so this is REPOINTING the fleet, not editing a key inside DeepInfra's registration;
  the deployed medic and `Box_Setup_Stuff/nemoclaw-inference-medic.sh` differ in EXACTLY the two
  `WANT_*` lines (diff-verified — installing the repo copy loses nothing); **Render's Fireworks key
  and the box's `fireworks-prod` credential are SEPARATE**, and the box's has not been exercised
  since the suspension, so its first use is a live test, not a formality. Reminder: the medic timer
  re-asserts `WANT_*` every 5 min, so a hand-run `openshell inference set` is reverted before you
  finish reading its output.
- **Stage-1 box runner is INSTALLED on trades-box-1 (2026-08-17; FIXED BUILD REDEPLOYED
  2026-08-18).** `/usr/local/sbin/nudge-runner.sh` — md5 **`a84f1a37c83caf06932ed4003d4de97c`**,
  byte-identical to `Box_Setup_Stuff/nudge-runner.sh` at `adac0d5` (the superseded
  `a429eea420342c3de2bc76bdaac99668` is the newline-bug build; see the `-m` trap above) — plus
  both units; `nudge-runner.timer` enabled + active on the 5-min cadence. Install verification is
  `sudo md5sum` of the path `ExecStart` NAMES: the file is mode 700 root, so a plain `md5sum` as
  `ubuntu` fails silently-looking (permission denied), which reads like "not installed". Verified end to end from the box: the derived URL
  `https://tradesdatabase.onrender.com/control/nudges` returns **HTTP 200 `{"nudges":[]}`** with
  the badge-pull token — which also proves the web service is serving the Stage-1 routes. It is
  INERT until `NUDGES_MODE=live` queues something. Kill switch:
  `systemctl disable --now nudge-runner.timer`. Note a silent, instant `systemctl start` is NOT
  proof of a working pull (the script always exits 0) — curl the endpoint to actually confirm.
- **Stage 1 is PROVEN END TO END IN PRODUCTION (2026-08-18 23:10-23:17Z, first clean run).**
  One synthetic overdue visit for manager-sam walked the whole ladder unattended: cron tick
  `watchers{mode:live, freshGaps:1, nudgesCreated:1, itemsOpened:0}` — the `itemsOpened:0` beside
  `nudgesCreated:1` is the LADDER ASSERTION (the gap went to the technician INSTEAD of the
  manager queue, not in addition to it) — then `teamLeadCompose{composed:1, held:0}` on
  Fireworks, then the box runner at the next tick: trajectory pre-check (no prior text) →
  stage-to-sandbox (1s) → `agent --message-file --deliver` (117s) → trajectory re-read finds
  assistant text → `asked` at 23:17:12 with **serve_count 1, attempts 0** (first attempt, retry
  machinery never engaged). Read the run in the DB, not the box journal: systemd prints an
  identical clean "Finished" whether the send worked or not.
  Timing arithmetic for anyone reproducing: end-to-end is TWO independent 5-min pulses (spine
  cron composes, box timer delivers) plus a ~40-120s agent turn, so worst case ~11 min from
  overdue to Telegram. A synthetic gap created seconds AFTER a cron tick waits for the next one.
  RETURN PATH PROVEN in the same run: reply 23:18:56 -> `record_field_note` wrote L1 + L2 ->
  nudge `answered` 23:20:33 ("a field report arrived - the gap filled itself"), no manager item
  ever opened; it picked the RIGHT job of the customer's two. **Read the evidence on the VISIT,
  not the capture**: `raw_captures.job_id` is NULL by design (L1 attaches to the CUSTOMER), the
  job link lives on the L2 visit via `source_capture_id` + `attributes.workPerformed`. A
  captures-per-job count reads 0 and looks like a failure; `hasActivitySince` is satisfied via
  `listVisitTimesForJobs`, which is why auto-resolve fired correctly.
  **D6 RESOLVED 2026-08-18 — continuity is by CHAT, closure is by DATABASE.** The ask and the
  reply land in DIFFERENT sessions: the question goes out in the isolated `nudge-<id>` session,
  the reply arrives in the person's main Telegram session, and that session's message log holds
  NO trace of the question. It works anyway because the gateway compiles a `Conversation context
  (untrusted, chronological, selected for current message)` block of TELEGRAM CHAT HISTORY into
  every turn's model input (proven in the reply turn's `context.compiled` + `prompt.submitted`
  events, tagged by originating session) — a bare "all done" resolved to the right customer and
  job unaided. Two INDEPENDENT mechanisms carry the loop and neither needs session threading.
  The nudge footer appears NOWHERE in that context: it lives and dies inside the nudge session,
  reaching neither the human nor the replying agent. Caveat for any future BATCHING: that block
  is *selected*, not complete. **TRAP while reading trajectories: `systemPrompt.truncated:true`
  (50344 -> 32768) is a LOGGING cap — `reason: trajectory-field-size-limit` — NOT a model-input
  truncation. Read the reason field before raising an alarm** (this project has had one false
  AGENTS.md-truncation scare already, and nearly had a second here).
  **STAGE-1 PLAN CLOSED 2026-08-19** (`status: complete`, commit 3af90b3): escalation regression
  passed (`asked_at` 49h back -> `nudgesEscalated:1, itemsOpened:1`, audit thread via
  `escalatedFromNudge`, exactly one item = no double-flag) — but it moved a TIMESTAMP rather than
  waiting out 48h, so it proves the BRANCH, not the CLOCK; the `9:34 PM` ask time in the copy is
  the fingerprint of the plant (a real ask can never fall outside the 7am-7pm window). Note
  `review_items.suggestion` is its OWN COLUMN, not a `detail` key — a `detail->>'suggestion'`
  read returns null and looks like missing copy.
  STILL UNPINNED: a FAILED Telegram send (a technician who never pressed Start on the bot) —
  what `--deliver` reports in that case is unknown, and it is the silent-by-nature failure mode.
  Answer it before enrolling any real technician.
- **TRAP — RENDER DEPLOYS FROM A FORK, NOT FROM `origin` (root-caused 2026-08-17 after ~1h of
  misdiagnosis).** The Render services are wired to **`Yu-C1/TradesDatabase`**, which
  `gh api` confirms is a FORK whose `parent` is `hongyi42/TradesDatabase` — the repo we push to.
  **Merging a PR on origin does NOT deploy anything.** The fork only advances when someone syncs
  it, and Auto-Deploy ("On Commit", genuinely enabled) fires off the FORK's commits. Live proof:
  fork `pushed_at` 20:25:34Z at `da1a7f2`, so the running cron image carried the env captured at
  20:25 — before the 20:46 `WATCHERS_MODE=live` flip — and every scheduled run reported a healthy
  `"mode":"shadow"`, while a dashboard Trigger Run (current env) reported `live`. Two pushes of
  mine to origin at 20:30 and 21:21 triggered NOTHING. Syncing the fork to `8f01d34` at 21:42:58Z
  deployed within ~3 min and the next scheduled tick opened items normally.
  **Checks before believing a deploy happened:** `gh api repos/Yu-C1/TradesDatabase/commits/main`
  vs `git log origin/main` — if they differ, production is running older code than `main`, and
  the Render dashboard's repo line (`Yu-C1 / TradesDatabase`) is the authority on what deploys.
  Corollary: the earlier note "the deploy log's Yu-C1 URL is presumably the same repo under a
  rename" was WRONG — the SHAs matched only because the fork happened to be synced at that moment.
  Second corollary (2026-08-24): `origin` **was** separately renamed `hhesecured-web/TradesDatabase`
  → `hongyi42/TradesDatabase` (a push to the old URL now answers `This repository moved`). That
  rename is real and is a *different fact* from the fork — do not let confirming one look like
  evidence about the other, which is how the wrong note above got written twice. GitHub's redirect
  keeps stale remotes working, so nothing fails loudly; repoint them anyway, since the redirect
  dies the moment anyone creates a repo at the old owner/name.
  **The deploy procedure this implies — the durable part, unlike any state table:** merge to
  `origin/main` → **sync the `Yu-C1` fork** (this is the step that actually deploys) → confirm the
  running SHA at **`GET /health`** on the web service (`docs/control-plane.md`), because that is
  the one check that reads the live system rather than a written record. For the two services with
  Auto-Deploy OFF (see the Render estate table in §4) even a fork sync ships nothing — deploy them
  by hand. Do NOT keep a separate provenance doc: a hand-maintained table of "what is live" rots
  silently and gets trusted; the estate table in §4 is dated and re-verified in bulk, `/health` and
  the `gh api` check above cannot go stale, and that is the whole answer.
- **TRAP — a Render CRON env change does NOT reach SCHEDULED runs until you DEPLOY
  (2026-08-17, cost ~30 min of misdiagnosis during the watchers live-flip).** Scheduled runs
  execute the last *deployed* version with the env captured at deploy time; saving the variable
  does not itself deploy, and a restart is not enough (render.com/docs/deploys: "if you've
  recently updated your service's environment variables but haven't redeployed since then,
  restarting does _not_ incorporate those changes"). The deceptive part: the dashboard's
  **Trigger Run** DOES use current env, so a manual test passes while every scheduled run keeps
  the old value. Live symptom we chased: `WATCHERS_MODE=live` set correctly, a manual run at
  20:49Z opened the expected 5 items, then every scheduled run logged a perfectly HEALTHY
  `"watchers":{"mode":"shadow","freshGaps":19,"tenantErrors":0}` and wrote nothing. **Diagnose by
  the SCHEDULED run's log line, never the dashboard field or a Trigger Run; after any cron env
  change click Manual Deploy → "Deploy latest commit".** Applies to WATCHERS_MODE, NUDGES_MODE (RETIRED in build 5 — delete the var at deploy time)
  and the inference key alike — the Stage-1 flip would have hit this exact wall.
- **TRAP — MERGING Stage 1 makes its migrations MANDATORY IMMEDIATELY, not at flip time
  (hit live 2026-08-17, PR #148 merged as 8ef1e68 and deployed before the pilot had the
  tables).** The nudges pre-filter read (`listNudgedSubjectRefs`, watchers.ts) is deliberately
  NOT gated by `NUDGES_MODE` and sits ABOVE the `if (mode === 'shadow') continue` — that is by
  design (it is how `freshGaps`, the number the flip decision needs, is computed in shadow).
  So on a pilot without the `nudges` table it throws for every tenant with gaps → the tenant
  loop catches → `tenantErrors` → lifecycle's systemic detector
  (`tenantErrors === tenants` ⇒ `ok=false`) → **exit 1, every 5-minute cron run marked FAILED**,
  while `freshGaps` silently stops being recorded (`gapsFound` still counts — it increments
  BEFORE the throw, so the log looks half-alive). One bound tenant means one error is already
  "all tenants". **Rule: land the migration on the pilot in the same change window as the merge,
  never at rollout time.** RESOLVED 2026-08-17 — both migrations applied to the pilot via MCP
  `apply_migration` + `notify pgrst, 'reload schema'`; verified by re-running the deployed
  watcher against the pilot in shadow (write-free by construction): `tenantErrors: 0`,
  gapsFound/freshGaps 22/22. Note the pilot's `supabase_migrations` versions are MCP
  auto-stamped and deliberately differ from the repo filenames (`fireworks_rates` = repo
  20260815120000 vs pilot 20260815175254) — this DB is not `db push`-managed; match the
  precedent, don't "fix" the version drift.
- **PR #146's migrations (`money_events`, `tenant_profiles`) were missing from the pilot until
  2026-08-17 — same class as the trap above: a merged PR's migrations are not self-applying.**
  APPLIED 2026-08-17 (MCP `apply_migration` + pgrst reload), verified: both tables + both views
  (`v_money_events`, `v_firm_profile`), RLS on, ZERO anon/authenticated grants, `spine_reader`
  select on both views, append-only + updated-at triggers live, 12 enum values, `anon` has no
  sequence usage, and both readable through PostgREST from the app path.
- **TRAP — `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` is NOT a reliable inventory of the pilot
  (2026-08-17).** Comparing repo migration filenames against recorded names showed SEVEN
  "missing"; checking actual OBJECTS showed only TWO were genuinely missing. Five
  (`phone_call_ingest`, `query_spine_reader`, `v_schedule_local_weekday`,
  `nvidia_failover_rates`, `ops_events`) had been applied directly via `execute_sql`, which
  writes no migration record — their tables/roles/views exist fine. **Always audit the pilot by
  object existence (`information_schema` / `pg_roles` / `pg_proc`), never by the migration
  table**, or you will "re-apply" a migration that is already live and get a spurious failure.
  Recorded versions are also MCP auto-stamped and differ from repo filenames by design.
- **`npm audit fix` is a NO-OP in this repo (verified 2026-08-17); plain `npm update` is a
  124-package sweep including a MAJOR `@hono/node-server` 1→2 jump.** All 7 advisories are
  transitive under `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` (hono + @hono/node-server, fast-uri via ajv,
  ip-address via express-rate-limit) plus dev-only nanoid/postcss; none of the vulnerable code
  paths are ones we invoke (no hono JSX/CORS/proxy middleware, Linux so no Windows static
  traversal, code-authored JSON schemas, rate-limit keying not access control). The clean fix is
  a TARGETED lockfile bump (LANDED as PR #149, 2026-08-17) — `npm update --package-lock-only hono @hono/node-server fast-uri
  ip-address nanoid postcss brace-expansion` → 8 patch/minor packages, `package.json` and the
  SDK version untouched, audit clean.
- **`nemoclaw list` shows the ONBOARDING RECORD, not the live inference route (2026-08-06).** Read
  off that list, s2/s5/s6/s7 all say `provider: nvidia-prod` — which reads exactly like "the fleet
  has been stuck on the fallback for six days", and was reported as such before being checked. It
  is wrong. `nemoclaw list` prints each sandbox's onboarded model/provider from `sandboxes.json`;
  the medic switches the **global gateway route** (`openshell inference set -g nemoclaw`) and never
  rewrites those per-sandbox fields. A failover stales them permanently and a failback never clears
  them — the same cosmetic-drift class as s3's nemotron ref. s8 is the most misleading: it keeps its
  true onboarded value (`deepinfra-prod`) and prints a "live OpenShell gateway differs" note that is
  itself stale.
  **Authoritative:** `openshell inference get` for the route, plus
  `journalctl -u nemoclaw-inference-medic.service` for whether the 5-minute assertion is passing now.
  **For proof the model actually SERVED** — which `inference set` can never give, see the blind-spot
  entries above — query the pilot's `cost_events` for `provider='deepinfra'` rows with skus
  `glm-5.2-{input,output,cached-input}`. Those are billed completions, and they need no API key.
- **The cost tracker was blind to the failover provider — FIXED 2026-08-07, with one claim
  corrected.** `cost_rates` held `deepinfra` and `anthropic` rows only, and `MODEL_SKUS` had no
  `nvidia` entry — two layers deep: `recordModelUsage` throws on an unmapped vendor (into a
  swallowed catch, so events were never written), and the Phase-3 rollup INNER joins `cost_rates`,
  so even a written event would have vanished from the dashboard. The `ledgerVendor()` seam was
  BUILT for this (it derives `nvidia` from the resolved `INFERENCE_BASE_URL` host, precisely so a
  failover is not priced against a DeepInfra invoice that never arrives) — but the vocabulary was
  never seeded, so its loud path was a silent one. Fixed: `MODEL_SKUS.nvidia` + $0 rate rows
  (free evaluation tier; the value is token counts and provider attribution, not dollars —
  migration 20260806230000).
  **Correction to the 2026-08-06 entry above-dated claims:** "a stretch of zero spend may mean
  failed over" OVERSTATED the mechanism. Spine model billing is keyed off the SPINE's own
  `INFERENCE_BASE_URL` on Render, which the box medic's gateway failover never touches — the
  07-31/08-04 zero-model-event days were tool calls that simply don't invoke models
  (`tool_call` events exist; most spine tools are pure reads/writes). The blindness was REAL but
  latent: it would have bitten when the *spine* fails over (the provider-readiness path), not
  when the *box* does. Box-agent NVIDIA usage is a separate story — it is invoice-allocated, and
  NVIDIA's free tier produces no invoice, so it remains unmetered by design.
- **`revoke ... from public` does NOT revoke anon/authenticated on a new function.** Supabase's
  DEFAULT PRIVILEGES grant EXECUTE on a newly created function in `public` *directly* to `anon` and
  `authenticated`; those are separate grants, so revoking PUBLIC leaves them. Hit live on the pilot
  2026-08-06 with `query_spine`, whose ACL read
  `spine_reader=X | anon=X | authenticated=X | service_role=X` after a migration that "revoked from
  public". It mattered because query_spine takes the TENANT AS A PARAMETER — anyone with the
  publishable anon key could have called it for ANY tenant. Fixed by
  `revoke execute … from public, anon, authenticated`, and `src/db/querySpine.test.ts` now asserts
  the ACL (verified to fail when the grant is restored). **Always name anon and authenticated
  explicitly; compare `tenant_cost_detail`, which is correctly `postgres|service_role` only.**
- **anon-readable tables on the pilot are a DELIBERATE posture (owner, 2026-08-06) — do not
  "fix" them.** Measured that day: with RLS off and `anon` granted SELECT, `customers`, `jobs`,
  `assignments`, `technicians`, `tenants`, `visits` and `raw_captures` *(⚠ rung 9b, 2026-09-05:
  `assignments` and `visits` are no longer two tables — the schedule was renamed to `visits` and
  the compat view named `assignments` is dropped by `20260906140100`; re-check this posture list
  after that migration)* are readable with the
  publishable key; `cost_events`, `call_ingest` and `query_audit` are not (RLS on). The owner
  confirms RLS-off is intentional here, and the mitigation is that the anon key appears nowhere in
  this project — so this is exposure-by-key-disclosure, not open data. **Treat the anon key as a
  secret on this project**, because it is load-bearing in a way it is not on a typical Supabase
  app. Raise it again only if the key is ever published (a web front end, a client bundle, a doc).
  What the **2026-10-30** date actually means: Supabase removes the `api.auto_expose_new_tables`
  option, after which NEW entities stop being auto-granted to anon. **Existing grants are
  unaffected** — it is a change in the default for future tables, not a remediation deadline.
  Not affected either way: the `v_*` query_spine views. They are granted to anon but fail closed
  (SQLSTATE 42704, `unrecognized configuration parameter "spine.tenant_id"`) because anon cannot
  set the GUC — verified by setting `role anon` on the pilot.
- PostgREST silently caps result sets at **1000 rows** (`db-max-rows`) — any scan that can
  exceed it must paginate (see `src/db/foldImpact.ts` for the pattern).
- `googleapis` is GONE (rung 10, 2026-09-07). It survived the schedule sheet's retirement only
  because `src/email/ingest.ts` imported it; that puller had no production caller and was deleted
  with it. Email arrives by Composio webhook (`src/inbound/webhook.ts`), push not poll.
- Schedule times are stored **naive-local** (wall-clock written as-if-UTC) — deliberate
  (see [thoughts/shared/plans/2026-07-14-schedule-core-on-assignments.md](../thoughts/shared/plans/2026-07-14-schedule-core-on-assignments.md)).
  Availability checks pass timezone `'UTC'`. Revisit if a tenant spans timezones.
- ⚠ **RETIRED by rung 9b (2026-09-05).** It read: *"`rescheduleAssignment` creates the new row as
  `'proposed'` by default while every reader filters `['confirmed']` — moves MUST pass
  `status:'confirmed'`."* There is no `rescheduleAssignment` and a move creates no row: it is one
  UPDATE through `moveVisitRow` (`src/db/visits.ts`), and the visit keeps its status and its id.

## 4. Live-state snapshot (as of 2026-08-24 — verify before relying)

> **How to read this section.** *"Where the system stands"* below was re-verified on 2026-08-24
> against the pilot database, the Render API and the repo, and it is the part to trust. Under it
> the tenant and sandbox tables are maintained; **"Feature status" is a build log** written
> forward from 2026-07-20 — read it for the reasoning, never as a status board. The newest month
> of work has its own home in §6. Everything here rots: re-verify before you act on it.

### Where the system stands (2026-08-24)

- **The ladder is complete through ⑤ — all five stages merged AND deployed (2026-08-24).**
  ① the eyes see the whole record (money trio, PR #162) · ② the mouth stops lying (manager copy
  + one-shot field notes, PR #163; ②b the planner writes the technician's question) · ③ the
  brain decides (attribution, closing moved from the sweep to the planner, continuity — PR #164,
  first clean tick 2026-08-23 01:01Z) · ④ the brain can look (read surface, PR #167) · ④b
  delivery costs nothing (runner v2, PRs #171/#172/#174) · ⑤ memory architecture (PRs
  #175/#176). Blow-by-blow in §6. **The agentic planner eval (PR #178) is now the yardstick:**
  re-judged baseline **Opus 63/65 vs GLM 58/65** — measure any planner change against those
  numbers, never the withdrawn Haiku-judged ones.
- **Model routing differs BY RENDER SERVICE (verified 2026-08-24).** The most surprising live
  fact in this file. `activeProvider()` (`src/model/forcedTool.ts:91`) is one process-wide
  switch reading `MODEL_PROVIDER`, and **anything other than the exact string
  `anthropic` means Fireworks**. The two services are configured differently, so the same code
  runs two different models:

  | Surface | Where it runs | Model today | Selected by |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Planner + Reflector | `lifecycle-heart` cron (`crn-d93iq7a8qa3s73ap6260`, `*/5`) | **`z-ai/glm-5.3` via OpenRouter** (since 2026-08-29 17:16 UTC; **Opus 5** before that) | `MODEL_PROVIDER=openai` + `INFERENCE_MODEL=z-ai/glm-5.3`, set on the service. `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` + `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` stay set as the one-variable ROLLBACK (`MODEL_PROVIDER=anthropic`) — and a rollback that forgets `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` silently gets Sonnet 4.6, the code default |
  | Capture + synthesis forced-tool calls — email classify, extract, brief, answer, phone-call classify | web service (`srv-d8uqmdegvqtc73bfn83g`) | **GLM-5.2 via Fireworks Fast** | **neither** `MODEL_PROVIDER` nor `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` is set there, so the code default (`accounts/fireworks/routers/glm-5p2-fast`) wins |
  | Seat agents (six sandboxes) | trades-box-1 gateways | GLM-5.2 via Fireworks Fast | box inference route; the medic asserts it |

  **THE SWITCH ITSELF IS THE LESSON (2026-08-29).** The OpenRouter code shipped and went live at
  04:56 UTC, and the planner kept running Opus for another twelve hours because the env cutover is
  a SEPARATE step that nobody had performed. Seven successful ticks in that window all billed
  `anthropic`. Attributing a tick to a model by its DATE — "the code is deployed, so it must be
  GLM" — produced a false reading in this repo, on this exact question. The reliable join is
  `cost_events.provider`/`sku` inside the tick's `[started_at, finished_at]`, plus `list_deploys`
  for what was live at that instant.

  Two further consequences worth carrying. **Billing:** `MODEL_SKUS` (`src/db/costs.ts`) is keyed per
  VENDOR, not per model — every Anthropic call is priced as Opus 5, so a rollback to Sonnet must
  re-edit that map or the ledger silently lies. **Caching:** the Anthropic path of both
  transports now sets `cache_control` with a 1h TTL (`toolLoop.ts:233`, `forcedTool.ts:245`,
  PR #177 + `cae37c4`) — the "production planner runs uncached" debt of 2026-08-23 is **PAID**.

- **43 manager tools, a 4-tool team-lead surface, and 12 views (2026-08-24; surface widened
  2026-08-30).** `MANAGER_TOOLS` (`src/mcp/server.ts`, pinned by `server.test.ts`) is 16 base
  (17 minus `get_schedule`) + 25 admin = **41** since rung 9a (2026-09-05) deleted `set_promise`,
  on top of rung 8 (2026-09-04) taking the four solver tools; it read 13 base + 30 admin = 43 when
  this entry was written. `spine_read`, `recall_decisions`, `read_memory` and `write_memory` are a
  **team-lead-only surface that REPLACES rather than extends**, gated to the `spine:` namespace.
  The last two arrived with build 3 (2026-08-30) and `write_memory` is **the identity's first
  write verb** — it passed the gate's then-rule ("nothing irreversible or externally visible")
  because every overwrite is versioned and no person receives anything, but it is the first model
  call in this codebase that mutates durable state from INSIDE the tool loop, with no post-loop
  validation stage the way `applyDecision` has.
  **That rule is RETIRED (rung 10b, 2026-09-08, user ruling 2026-09-07: the planner writes L2.)**
  It was a v1 posture, not a design — it described a planner nobody had yet decided should write
  the record. `record_caller` is the first verb that writes the customer record itself. What
  replaces the rule: the allowlist is still the CEILING, every write it permits cites the capture
  it came from, ask-first is doctrine rather than mechanism (linking a known caller needs nobody's
  permission; creating, opening a job and booking a trip are put to a manager), and nothing on the
  surface reaches a person — a `send` is still proposed by the planner and performed after the loop
  by `applyDecision`, where the rails live. The reasoning lives beside the allowlist in
  `src/teamLead/identity.ts`; a test pins that a name on the allowlist and a schema in
  `TEAM_LEAD_TOOLS` are added together. `spine_read` now exposes
  **twelve** views — the four ④ additions (`v_captures`, `v_visits`, `v_nudges`,
  `v_review_items`) joined the original eight, so any "eight views" claim elsewhere in this file
  is pre-④ history. *(⚠ rung 9b, 2026-09-05: `v_visits` was replaced by `v_visit_notes`. Still
  twelve; the name in any pre-9b text is the only thing that changed.)*

rollout was ordered that way, why a copy line reads as it does) and re-check any claim you would
act on. Known corrections since it was written: the model line below is wrong twice over
(DeepInfra is retired and the spine's forced-tool calls are more than four — see "Model routing"
above), and the demo schedule window it describes has expired.

  on his own laptop, not through this pilot.** **Device-pass finding
  (2026-07-25, owner): verb-follows-noun gap** — vague "cancel Tom …" correctly triggers the
  which-kind ask, but "cancel <person>'s visit <day>" (noun = visit) silently resolves to
  unschedule with no cancel_job offer. Mitigations that held: promise-gated confirm + the
  preview's "work stays open → loose bucket" disclosure before acting; edge = promise-none
  one-shots disclose only after the fact (reversible). Candidate fix, recorded not built: a
  result-embedded self-heal line on unschedule preview/one-shot replies — "if the WORK itself
  is dead, say so and I'll cancel the job instead" (the book_visit promiseLevel-line pattern;
  server result strings need only a deploy, no fleet restart). C10 rerun (owner, later same
  day): the agent DID ask which-kind for a visit-noun phrase — the gap is GLM-variance, not
  deterministic, which strengthens the self-heal-line case. Second copy nit from C10: the
  unschedule option says the job "goes back to the loose bucket" even when the visit is
  UNASSIGNED (already tray-visible — it never left); candidate conditional phrasing when
  technician_id is null, same deploy-only result-string class.
  **"next <weekday>" both-and-ask rule VIOLATED live 2026-07-25 (trajectory-diagnosed,
  same day as the week fix):** "is there any job next thursday" (asked on a Saturday) →
  agent called `resolve_date {weekday: thursday, weeksAhead: 1}` ONCE (→ Aug 6), silently
  picking one reading despite the description's explicit resolve-both-and-ask order; the
  manager meant Jul 30 (weeksAhead 0). The rule exists only as prompt-layer steering (tool
  description + §0f item 3) — the documented GLM class. Candidate mechanism (week-mode's
  sibling, recorded not built): a `saidNext: true` flag on weekday mode — the server returns
  BOTH candidate dates + a result-embedded ask note, shrinking the agent's job from
  "resolve twice and compose the question" to "relay the result". Also verified same session:
  NO date is injected into agent context — today's date appears ONLY in resolve_date results
  (by §0f design), confirmed by a full-session grep (1 hit, the tool result).
  **Temporal grounding riders SHIPPED 2026-07-25 (user decision after the eval re-run —
  A 66.3% / B 80.6% / C 99.0%, re-run recorded in the date-reliability research doc):** every
  successful object tool result carries `now` (the condition-B line: date + weekday + tz,
  firm-local, 10-min-memoized tenant tz) via the ONE decoration seam in `src/mcp/server.ts`
  (same seam as boardLink). Arrays keep their shape. Self-refreshing across midnight — the
  reason injection-at-session-start was rejected; OpenClaw has no per-prompt injection hook
  (config scan + vendor docs check, github.com/openclaw/openclaw issue #53404 still open).
  resolve_date STAYS the arithmetic authority (the eval's weekday-walking tier: 41% with
  injection alone, 98%+ with the tool). Rollout: deploy-only — result fields are per-call,
  NO fleet gateway restart needed (tools/list unchanged). Rider is BEST-EFFORT (review
  finding): it decorates after dispatch has committed, so a grounding failure omits `now`
  rather than erroring a committed result (which would invite a retry double-book).
  **Deliberate decision (2026-07-25): NO AGENTS.md/bootstrap line for the rider** — condition
  B works by mere presence in context, unlike boardLink which needed a fused instruction to
  make the model ACT. Watch item for the next device pass: does the agent parrot "Right now
  it is…" into replies? If yes, a one-line "never quote the `now` field" rides the NEXT wire
