- **2026-07-31 — cron was dead fleet-wide, for TWO stacked reasons. Both now fixed on s2, s3, s5,
  s6, s8 (s7 held back — in use for testing).**
  1. **Device pairing.** Every tool routed through `callGatewayTool` (`cron`, `nodes`, `gateway`,
     `wake`) reconnects to the Gateway as a *client* (`clientId=gateway-client`,
     `clientMode=backend`). OpenClaw waives pairing only for shared-secret clients on a **loopback**
     address, but NemoClaw points `OPENCLAW_GATEWAY_URL` at the bridge IP — so every call was
     refused with `pairing required`. NemoClaw's auto-approval allowlist
     (`openclaw_device_approval_policy.py`) covers `openclaw-control-ui` + `webchat`/`cli` only, so
     the agent's own client is *never* auto-approved; and `doctor --fix` deadlocks because its first
     step is a gateway-pinned `devices list`, which needs the approval it is trying to grant.
     Contributing: `openclaw.json` `gateway.port` had drifted (18790/18793) from the real port
     (**18789**), so OpenClaw's local loopback fallback — the documented escape hatch — dialled a
     dead port. **Fix per sandbox:** correct `gateway.port` (gateway-mediated recipe), then
     `nemoclaw <s> exec --no-tty -- bash -lc "openclaw cron status"` to mint the request and
     `... "openclaw devices approve <id>"` to approve it (prints *"using local fallback"*), twice —
     once for read, once for the cron-mutation scope. Verify with `doctor` → `[ok] Tool-call device
     scope`. Must be `nemoclaw exec` (gateway netns); `docker exec` lands elsewhere and loopback
     fails. Full write-up: `thoughts/shared/research/2026-07-31-nemoclaw-device-scope-deadlock.md`.
     **Note `cron.add/update/remove/run` require `operator.admin`, not `operator.write`** — and
     `operator.admin` is a universal allow (`if (scopes.includes("operator.admin")) return
     {allowed:true}`), so scheduling costs config-write/agent-delete/channel-control too.
     **That approve-based recipe only works on a sandbox that was part-paired during its startup
     window (s2). A COLD sandbox cannot be repaired that way** — every `nemoclaw exec` mints a new
     pending request that supersedes the one being approved, so it never converges. What works
     there: **seed both halves of the token pair by hand.** OpenClaw authenticates a paired device
     by *plain token comparison* (`verifyPairingToken` → `safeEqualSecret`); the stored `publicKey`
     is never used to verify a connection. The same secret lives in two files and a real approval
     writes both — `devices/paired.json` (server) and `identity/device-auth.json` (client).
     **Seeding only `paired.json` fails closed with `token-mismatch`** — the client can't present a
     token it never received. No gateway restart needed (`loadState` re-reads per auth attempt), so
     it's zero-downtime on live boxes; clear `devices/pending.json` to `{}` afterwards or `doctor`
     keeps reporting `[fail]` on an inert stale request. Script + full derivation in the research
     doc. Used on s3/s5/s6/s8 on 2026-07-31.
  2. **Main-session cron cannot fire here.** A `sessionTarget: "main"` + `systemEvent` job does not
     run itself — it wakes the heartbeat, and we set `agents.defaults.heartbeat.every: "0m"` on
     purpose. `heartbeat-runner` returns `{status:"skipped", reason:"disabled"}`, so cron records
     `status: skipped, error: "disabled"` **and flips the job to `enabled:false`** — it looks
     healthy at creation and silently never fires. **Isolated `agentTurn` jobs are unaffected** and
     were verified end-to-end: fired on time, ran a real turn, `delivered: true` to Telegram.
     The §0g AGENTS.md block now mandates `sessionTarget:"isolated"` + `payload.kind:"agentTurn"`
     and is REFRESHED per wire run (delete-then-append) instead of append-once.
- **AGENTS.md is ~26.6k chars against a 12k injected-context budget, so most of it never reaches
  the model.** Truncation keeps head 45% + a policy digest 35% + tail 15%
  (`trimAgentsBootstrapContent`). The digest scans the whole file but caps lines at **240 chars**
  and fills its 4,200-char budget in **file order**: s2 has 56 high-priority (`must`/`never`/`do
  not`) lines totalling 4,900 chars, so **135 policy lines are dropped** and anything late in the
  file loses. The new cron rule survives only because its block is currently last (tail) and its
  imperative is a single ≤240-char line — deliberately written that way. **Any operator guidance
  added late in AGENTS.md is probably invisible; verify with the head/digest/tail model before
  trusting it.** Trimming AGENTS.md is the real fix and is still owed.

- **Money's structure in the spine is load-bearing for FUTURE BUSINESS ANALYTICS** (user
  constraint 2026-08-19). `money_events` is not L1/L2/L3 — it is a ledger ALONGSIDE L2, FK'd into
  it, with evidence links back to L1. **L2 = nouns (what exists now); the ledger = verbs (what
  happened, in order)** — state is folded, never stored, and the `completed → invoiced → paid`
  status hops are derived by `syncJobStatusForMoney`, never hand-advanced. The two are
  conceptually separate but must be **read together**, and that coupling is already enforced
  structurally, not by convention: `job_id not null references jobs(id) on delete restrict` (no
  orphan either way); `v_money_events` joins job + customer onto every row so agent SQL cannot
  select a bare amount; and every accessor is job-keyed (there is deliberately no "list all money
  events"). **THE RULE for any analytics work: aggregate the SUMMARIES (`summarizeMoney` per job),
  never the rows.** Hand-rolled SQL over `money_events` must re-implement four things the fold
  already handles, and silently lies if it misses one: direction is by `kind` and never sign
  (`amount_cents >= 0` is a DB check, so cross-kind `sum(amount_cents)` is nonsense — **cost kinds
  newly enable this mistake**); negated rows (voids/corrections) stay in the table forever;
  `currency` is a defaulted column, not a guarantee; and `lastEventAt` scans ALL events including
  negated ones. Consequence: **costs belong as new KINDS inside `money_events`, never a separate
  cost table** — new kinds are provably inert in existing revenue math (the fold is kind-additive)
  and inherit the FK, the joined view, the accessors and negation semantics for free.

## 5. The walls — traps by the time you'd hit them

The box-side details live in `Box_Setup_Stuff/` (setup guide §8 gotchas, architecture-memory
"Gotchas") — this is the index of what exists, so you know to go look.

**FIVE copies of `bootstrap-box.sh` live on trades-box-1, and the obvious path was the STALEST —
this is what produced fleet-wide doctrine drift (found + fixed 2026-08-20).** An audit of the six
live seats found **five distinct `AGENTS.md` md5s**: the confirmation-doctrine block was at
`2026-07-26` on ALL SIX (repo: `2026-08-14`), so **rule 6 — assent-takes-recommendation — was
deployed on zero seats** even though spine-eval M09 had verified it 3/3. The dates block sat at
three different versions (s2 `08-15`, s5–s8 `08-04`, s3 `07-15`).

Root cause: the copy at `~/bootstrap-box.sh` — the path you would naturally type — was from
**2026-08-06** and contains exactly `Confirmations … 2026-07-26` + `Dates … 2026-08-04`, i.e. the
state four seats were running. **Seat doctrine is a fossil record of which copy was used, and
when.** The inventory at the time:

| path | state | note |
|---|---|---|
| `~/bootstrap-box.sh` | 2026-08-06, stale | **now overwritten with the repo copy**; old kept as `.stale-20260806.bak` |
| `~/Box_Setup_Stuff/` | 2026-08-15 | no `plugins/` dir — cannot run `wire` end to end |
| `~/stage-deepinfra-retire/` | matches repo | only 2 files — also no `plugins/` |
| `~/deploy-recycler/` | 2026-07-12 | |
| `/usr/local/sbin/bootstrap-box.sh` | matches repo | **the one the recycler actually runs** (`recycle-sandbox.sh` → `$BOOTSTRAP wire`); its bundled guard plugin was stale at 0.2.0 and was updated to 0.4.0 the same day |

Rules this leaves behind:
- **md5 a box-local `bootstrap-box.sh` against the repo before running it** — the same discipline
  the doorman/medic entries already demand ("the path the unit actually runs, never a sibling copy").
  A first `find` sweep missed `/usr/local/sbin/` entirely; search system dirs too.
- **`wire` needs the WHOLE `Box_Setup_Stuff/` tree**, not just the script — the weekday-guard step
  reads `$SCRIPT_DIR/plugins/trades-weekday-guard`, so a script-only copy half-applies.
- **4 of the 9 AGENTS.md blocks are append-once** (`accuracy-rules`, `permanent-record`,
  `batching`, `web-content` are skip-if-present); `wire` can NEVER update them. Changing their text
  requires an explicit removal step first, or the fleet silently keeps the old wording.
- **s2 is the canary and must stay the most up-to-date seat** (owner: "s2 is the testing box for
  the repo"). Corollary: s2 being freshest is *how this hid* — audit a NON-s2 seat, or all of them.
- **A per-seat exemption exempts that seat from every LATER fix, not just the one it was written
  for.** s3's tool-deny carve-out meant nothing ever re-ran `wire` there, so it sat on July doctrine
  for six weeks. Retired 2026-08-20.

**Re-inventoried 2026-09-04 for the rung-8 doctrine rollout: there are now SEVEN copies, and not
one matched the repo.** Two weeks after the identical 2026-08-22 finding. The count itself drifts,
which is why the rule is "re-inventory", not "consult the table":

| md5 | paths |
|---|---|
| `746911971373f48569056ee9428b9b63` | `/usr/local/sbin/` **(the copy the recycler runs)**, `~/bootstrap-box.sh`, `~/Box_Setup_Stuff/` |
| `973afe2f19a1d2bade8fb3b334444db0` | `~/stage-deepinfra-retire/`, `~/bss-repo/` |
| `82751ab6dbf39ce84283f148b0ce9a35` | `~/Box_Setup_Stuff.prev-20260825/` |
| `dadbf315abd9717e02d1b9c9d8c7d436` | `~/deploy-recycler/` |

Fixed by `rsync`-ing the whole tree to `~/Box_Setup_Stuff/` and `install`-ing over
`/usr/local/sbin/bootstrap-box.sh` (backup kept as `.pre-rung8.bak`), then `wire` on all six seats,
**s2 first**. Verified per seat rather than from the script's own success line: `freeze=0` on all
six, the only surviving "pinning" match being the Confirmations header recording that the clause was
removed. Every container stayed `(healthy)`; none restarted.

Two mechanics that bite every time: **`wire` needs the whole tree** (the weekday-guard step reads
`$SCRIPT_DIR/plugins/...`), and **non-interactive ssh lacks `~/.local/bin`**, so prefix
`PATH="$PATH:/home/ubuntu/.local/bin"` or `wire` dies at its bare `nemoclaw` call.


**Working in a second worktree (local Supabase stacks):**
- **`npx supabase stop` targets the `project_id` in the CURRENT directory's
  `supabase/config.toml` — so running it from a worktree that never had
  `scripts/second-stack.sh` applied STOPS THE PRIMARY STACK, not that worktree's.**
  `second-stack.sh` leaves the rewritten `project_id`/ports *uncommitted*, so any worktree where
  that edit was reverted (or never made) reads `project_id = "TradesDatabase"` and the CLI
  cheerfully stops the main checkout's stack out from under whoever is using it. Hit twice on
  2026-08-19, once while a concurrent session was mid-`npm test`. **Always
  `grep -m1 '^project_id' supabase/config.toml` before `supabase stop|start`**, and read the
  CLI's closing line — it names the project it acted on
  (`Local data are backed up to docker volume ... project=<id>`), which is the cheapest
  confirmation you stopped what you meant to. Recovery is `npx supabase start` from the main
  checkout; the volume-backed data survives a plain `stop` (only `--no-backup` discards it).
- **Concurrent local stacks are the top cause of phantom test failures on this machine.** Four
  stacks = 46 containers and the Docker VM pinned near 1200% CPU on a 14-core Mac; DB-backed
  suites then blow their 10s `beforeAll` / `pg.connect()` hook timeouts and fail in a different
  combination on every run. The tell is **failures that move between runs** and a suite time that
  balloons (33s → 135s for the same tree). Before believing any red suite, check
  `docker ps -q | wc -l` and per-stack CPU
  (`docker stats --no-stream`); a comments-only diff cannot break 15 tests. Stop stacks belonging
  to merged/abandoned worktrees — verify with
  `git merge-base --is-ancestor $(git -C <wt> rev-parse HEAD) origin/main` first.

**Tuning a constant (the nudge ladder, 2026-08-20):**
- `NUDGE_ESCALATE_HOURS` **48 → 24** and `NUDGE_DELIVERY_DEADLINE_HOURS` **24 → 18**. These ask
  DIFFERENT questions and had collided by accident: escalation is *how long do we wait for a
  PERSON*; the delivery deadline is *how long before we admit the MACHINERY is broken* (offboarded
  tech, recycled sandbox with a cleared `host_id`, rollback-frozen row). Nobody is ignoring us in
  the second case, so there is no patience to extend — **a gap nobody can even receive should reach
  the manager SOONER than one somebody is sitting on.** ~~The deadline's floor is ~14h, not zero: the
  7am-7pm ask window holds an evening gap overnight (12h; 13h on a DST fall-back night)… A window
  that ever excludes whole DAYS must move that number FIRST.~~ **The floor argument died with the
  window (2026-08-29):** nothing holds a gap overnight any more, so 18h is now a plain broken-pipe
  timeout with no derivation. The number was NOT re-tuned when its rationale was removed —
  deliberately, so the two decisions stay separately reviewable. Treat 18 as inherited.
- The evidence for 24h came from the PLANNER, before the code changed: across three shadow ticks on
  one real pilot gap it chose to escalate at 24h and argued it from job size ("a routine 60-min
  visit follow-up should not sit unanswered for a full day"). 48 was never reasoned about.
- **Retuning a constant is retroactive.** The pilot row was already 24.8h old, so the first sweep on
  the new code escalated it 14 seconds after the container came up — a day earlier than its own
  clock would have. Check what is already past the NEW threshold before shipping a shortened one.
- **TEST ROT — hit three times in one day.** Fixtures that hardcode hours (`49h`, `25h`, `22h`) were
  each chosen to sit on one side of a boundary; retune the constant and the boundary slides out from
  under them, and a test named for a case silently stops testing it. Derive fixture timestamps FROM
  the constant. Same shape as the weekday-guard bomb below. The check that catches it: flip the
  constant and confirm the suite is still green *for the right reason* — then break the behaviour
  and confirm it fails.

**Calling the model from the spine:**
- **GLM-5.2 thinks BY DEFAULT, so a small `maxTokens` truncates the FIRST turn — not the answer,
  the whole call.** Measured 2026-08-20: the team-lead planner at `maxTokens: 4096` with
  `reasoningEffort` unset returned `finish_reason=length` on turn one and produced no plan at all.
  The truncation guard caught it (that is what it is for); the caller simply could not run. Fixed
  with `reasoningEffort: 'low'` + 8192 — deliberately NOT `'none'`, which is right for a
  forced-JSON extraction and wrong for a call whose entire job is judgment.
  **Three shipped callers carry the same latent risk, unmeasured:** `src/calls/classifyCall.ts`
  (maxTokens **256**), `src/email/classify.ts` (**256**), `src/writePath/extract.ts` (1024). They
  work today because their prompts are short; a long email or transcript is the input that trips
  them, and the failure is a thrown error, not a degraded answer.

**Working on the agent boxes:**
- **A tool DESCRIPTION change is not deploy-only; a result-side field is.** Gateways cache
  `tools/list` per process, and descriptions are part of that payload — so editing one needs
  `nemoclaw <s> gateway restart` before any agent sees it, while adding a field to a tool's
  *result* (PR #83's grounding riders; the 2026-07-31 per-stop `mapsUrl`) goes live on deploy
  alone. A full box wire re-run is only needed when the change also touches AGENTS.md contract
  text or the pinned tool NAMES — a pure description edit touches neither. **And the restart is
  per TIER, not fleet-wide:** a tool filtered off one surface only forces restarts on the tier
  that carries it. Worked example (2026-07-31) — the `get_schedule` rewrite changed that tool's
  description, but `get_schedule` is filtered out of `MANAGER_TOOLS`, so *technician* sandboxes
  needed the restart and the manager side, whose change was result-only, was live the moment
  Render deployed. Getting this backwards costs either a stale fleet or needless restarts.
  **Amended 2026-08-04, after the tool-search rollout (PR #99):** under `tools.toolSearch` a
  description is no longer only the parameter surface — it is the **discovery surface**. The
  model's loaded tool list is just `tool_search`/`tool_describe`/`tool_call`; every spine tool is
  a catalog entry it has to *find*, by matching against that description text. So a description
  edit now changes both what the agent can learn about a tool and whether it locates it at all,
  and the gateway's per-process `tools/list` cache still gates when either takes effect. The
  matching failure is silent and total — that is exactly how `resolve_date` stopped being called
  (§0j in `bootstrap-box.sh` teaches the route explicitly as the fix). **Any tool whose
  description you rewrite inherits that exposure**; `get_schedule` was rewritten on 2026-07-31
  and has no §0-style block of its own, so "the agent never called it" is a live failure mode to
  rule out before blaming the tool.
- **A sandbox can hold a badge the doorman no longer knows — and nothing says so.** Found on s5
  2026-08-04: every spine call returned the doorman's flat `badge not authorized`, while
  `badges.json` held a live, non-revoked entry for that very person (`ben-shelton`). The sandbox
  was simply carrying an OLDER badge — the pull regenerates `badges.json` wholesale from the
  roster, so a rotation orphans whatever the sandbox still has. There is no error surfaced to the
  operator, no unhealthy status, and the container/gateway both report healthy; the bot just
  cannot reach any tool. **Diagnostic (verified, fingerprints only — never print plaintext):**
  fingerprint the badge from BOTH `/sandbox/.openclaw/openclaw.json`
  (`.mcp.servers["trades-spine"].headers.Authorization`, strip the `Bearer ` prefix) and the
  running gateway's `/proc/<pid>/environ` `TRADES_SPINE_BADGE`, then test
  `jq --arg f "$FP" 'has($f)' /etc/doorman/badges.json`. Both fingerprints matching each other but
  absent from `badges.json` = orphaned badge, and it also proves a gateway restart did not cause
  it (the file and the live process agree). Fix is server-side `rotate-badge` → next pull mints
  into `/root/pending-badges.jsonl` → install as the provider credential → **container** restart
  (a gateway restart is not enough; creds inject at container launch). Check this BEFORE
  debugging any "the agent isn't using tool X" report on a specific sandbox.
- **The doorman is ONE shared process — an unhandled stream `error` anywhere in its request
  flow kills every sandbox's in-flight MCP call at once.** Bitten 2026-08-04: an upstream TLS
  reset mid-SSE fired `upReq`'s error handler *after* the response head had been relayed; its
  unconditional `res.writeHead(502)` threw `ERR_HTTP_HEADERS_SENT` and the whole process exited.
  `Restart=always` brings it back in ~5s, so the only visible symptom is a climbing
  `restart counter` in `journalctl -u doorman.service` — check that counter before trusting "the
  doorman is fine". Fixed 2026-08-11 (headersSent guard, `stream.pipeline` instead of bare
  `pipe`, client-disconnect teardown of the upstream leg); `src/control/doorman.test.ts` now runs
  the REAL `Box_Setup_Stuff/doorman.mjs` against a stub upstream and replays every one of these
  deaths. Two docs embed verbatim copies of the file (`doorman-runbook.md`,
  `doorman-nemoclaw-setup-guide.md`) — they must stay byte-identical to it; the runbook's copy
  drifted once (2026-07-06→08-11) and kept handing out the crash-prone pre-relay code.
- **Credentials inject into a sandbox's process env at container launch ONLY.** Any provider
  credential write (badge install/rotation, token change) needs a container restart to take
  effect. This exact mechanism silently killed Judy's spine MCP for 4 days (badge in store,
  absent from env).
- **A manual `docker restart` of a long-running sandbox can crash-loop on
  `invalid token: ExpiredSignature` (hit 2026-08-12, s2 token handover).** The gateway mints each
  sandbox's JWT ONCE at create (`~/.local/state/openshell/docker-sandbox-tokens/…/sandbox.jwt`,
  TTL ~30d from `openshell-gateway.toml`); the running supervisor refreshes it in memory only and
  never rewrites the file. Restart the container after the on-disk token expires and it boots
  into `Policy fetch failed … ExpiredSignature` with "static token sources cannot rebootstrap
  automatically" — the revive unit then flaps in `activating auto-restart`. Fix:
  `sudo systemctl restart sandbox-token-medic.service` (re-mints from the gateway's signing key
  and restarts the container; script header at `/usr/local/sbin/sandbox-token-medic.mjs` has the
  full story — it exists because a 2026-07-05 box reboot hit the same class). Side effect: run
  without `NO_RESTART=1` and it also bounces every OTHER sandbox whose on-disk token is expired,
  healthy or not (s3 and s5 took ~10s blips as bystanders on 2026-08-12). So: before any manual
  container restart, run the medic (or check the token's `exp`) — the credential-restart rule
  above still applies, it just needs a valid sandbox token to survive the restart.
  **Hit again 2026-09-05 00:58 PDT — a GATEWAY restart is a container restart for every seat.**
  The committed systemd adoption (`pkill openshell-gateway` → `systemctl enable --now`,
  25d15ee/5ca763d) had the new gateway "resume" all six sandboxes and immediately re-provision
  them (docker: SIGTERM ×6, then SIGKILL ×6 ten seconds later). s2/s3/s5 came back on their
  own; s6/s7/s8 looped on `ExpiredSignature` (revive counter reached 20) until the medic ran
  eight minutes later. `sandbox-token-medic.service` is `Before=nemoclaw-boot.service`, so it
  only protects a REBOOT — a hand-run gateway restart gets no re-mint. So: **run the token
  medic BEFORE restarting the gateway**, and expect every seat to blip regardless.
- **NEVER change a sandbox's `openclaw.json` with `openclaw config patch`.** It writes the file
  but does NOT update nemoclaw's `.config-hash`, so the next `gateway restart` fails
  `GATEWAY_UNSAFE_CONFIG_PATH` and leaves the gateway DOWN (3 bots went down learning this on
  2026-07-30). Two extra teeth: run as root via `docker exec` and the file ends up `root:root`,
  which the gateway also refuses (`chown sandbox:sandbox` + `chmod 660` to fix); and without a
  login shell `OPENCLAW_HOME` is unset, so the patch silently lands in `/root/.openclaw/` and
  changes NOTHING — it still prints "Applied N config update(s)". **Always verify by reading
  `/sandbox/.openclaw/openclaw.json` back, never trust the success message.**
  Use the gateway-mediated recipe instead (already in `bootstrap-box.sh`, heartbeat + tool-trim
  blocks): `nemoclaw <s> download` → edit with `jq` → `upload` → write `.config-hash` →
  `chmod 660` → `gateway restart`. If a gateway is already stuck this way,
  `nemoclaw <s> doctor --fix` then restart clears it.
  **The ownership tooth bites specifically on ATOMIC REPLACE (learned 2026-08-03 — s5/s6/s7/s8 all
  went down for ~2 min).** A root-side write *in place* (`open(path,"w")`) truncates the existing
  inode and **keeps** `sandbox:sandbox`, so it works. Rewriting the same script to be "safer" —
  write `path.tmp`, then `os.replace()` — creates a **new inode owned by root**, and
  `shutil.copymode()` copies the mode but **not the owner**, so `ls` still shows `-rw-rw----` and
  nothing looks wrong. `chmod` is not a substitute for `chown`: after ANY root-side write, run
  `chown sandbox:sandbox` and check the **owner** column, not just the permission bits, before
  restarting. The failure is at least loud and instant — every restart returns
  `GATEWAY_UNSAFE_CONFIG_PATH`, and chown + restart is the whole recovery.
- **FOURTH tooth, SOLVED 2026-08-12 — the "something keeps resetting s7's perms" mystery was
  our own deploys plus a silent lost race.** Evidence: s7's `config-audit.jsonl` shows EVERY
  openclaw config write (`config patch`, `plugins install`) creating a fresh `openclaw.json`
  inode at **0o600** — vendor secure-default, six for six entries (`previousMode 660 →
  nextMode 600`; the 2026-07-31 one also left it `root:root`), and every one of those writes
  was our own rollout tooling. nemoclaw anticipates this: `normalize_mutable_config_perms.py`
  restores 660/2770 after each command — but it **fail-closes by design** (`UnsafeTree`; its
  stability key includes `st_mtime_ns`) when ANY file in the tree changes mid-walk, and the
  failure is a swallowed "cleanup exit 1" in stderr. A busy gateway loses that race; s7 (the
  busiest test box) lost it three times (2026-08-06, 2026-08-12 ×2), each ending in
  `GATEWAY_UNSAFE_CONFIG_PATH`. Fault-injection on s6 (set 700/600, run the normalizer) proved
  the repair path itself sound — the failures were the race, not the script. Fix (PR #126):
  the bootstrap's guard-install block now restores `chmod 2770` + `chmod 660` +
  `chown sandbox:sandbox` DETERMINISTICALLY between `plugins install` and `doctor --fix` (a
  root chmod on two named paths cannot lose a race), and `verify` gained a per-sandbox perms
  tripwire so a lost race can never again be silent. Upstream-worthy: a fail-closed security
  check whose failure is invisible is half a mechanism.
- **`nemoclaw rebuild` can destroy the sandbox's device private key** (vendor bug: the
  state-snapshot sanitizer replaces `privateKeyPem` with `[STRIPPED_BY_MIGRATION]` in the
  LIVE identity). Symptom: endless device-pairing loop, no Telegram polling, no MCP. Detect:
  `grep STRIPPED_BY_MIGRATION .../identity/device.json`. Back up identity files around any
  rebuild; prefer `gateway restart` (safe) over rebuild wherever possible.
- **The placeholder versioning trap:** the stock start script freezes credential placeholders
  to a versioned form; a later provider write strands them → dead Telegram bridge after
  restart. The boot self-heal never runs on the new-generation stack. After any provider
  credential write, confirm placeholder forms are canonical before restarting agents.
- **`nemoclaw onboard`/rebuild resets the gateway inference route** to the NVIDIA free-tier
  default (rate-limited, wrong model). The medic timer re-asserts DeepInfra within 5 min; if
  agents act "dumb"/throttled right after an onboard, that's why.
- **Tool VOCABULARY lives in three places, and one of them self-reapplies.** Renaming or
  adding an agent-facing tool means: (1) the MCP tool defs (gateway-cached `tools/list` →
  fleet restarts), (2) the living docs (`docs/nemoclaw-tech-agent.md` + the Box Setup Stuff
  sync rule), and (3) **the heredoc template blocks in `Box_Setup_Stuff/bootstrap-box.sh`**
  (doctrine + boardLink blocks, ~:594/:619), which are delete-then-appended into every
  agent's AGENTS.md **on every wire run** — miss (3) and the fleet's instructions re-acquire
  the dead name forever, and no code gate notices. Grep gates must include
  `Box_Setup_Stuff`. (Caught in the 2026-07-25 cancel-vocabulary plan review.)
- **There is NO per-tool allowlist file** in the sandbox configs, whatever older docs imply.
  "Pinning" = the gateway's cached MCP `tools/list` (refresh: `nemoclaw <s> gateway restart`;
  MCP starts lazily, so verify behaviorally — logs stay quiet) + the AGENTS.md contract
  lines. **MCP tool descriptions are fetched once per process** — after deploying description
  changes, restart the relevant sandbox containers.
- **The Telegram sender allowlist lives in TWO places** (rendered `openclaw.json` +
  `.config-hash`, and source `sandboxes.json` `allowedIds`) — a durable change writes both,
  then `gateway restart`. `channels status --json` shows drift. File perms must be 660.
- **Never health-check via `nemoclaw <s> agent`** — it injects into the person's LIVE
  Telegram conversation. Use `doctor`, `channels status`, logs, or a live message from an
  authorized phone.
- **Trust box evidence over vendor status pages** (DeepInfra stayed green through two real
  outages). Both-providers-dead alarm → check the DeepInfra balance (HTTP 402) first.
- **DeepInfra balance runs out roughly monthly and NOTHING alerts on it** — 2026-07-11,
  2026-07-20, and again **2026-07-31** (last healthy probe 07:15:53Z, failover 07:20:57Z, still
  failed over 8h24m later). Each time medic v2 failed the fleet to `nvidia-prod` within one tick
  and the bots kept working (0 model errors on s2 across 9h), which is exactly why it goes
  unnoticed. Surface it with the medic's own probe run LOUD:
  `openshell inference set -g nemoclaw --provider deepinfra-prod --model zai-org/GLM-5.2` —
  the 402 body names the cause. Auto-top-up / a balance alarm is still not configured.
- **`inference.local` silently follows the failover, so a provider flip INVALIDATES any latency
  measurement taken across it.** A `cron_job` A/B on 2026-07-31 was ruined this way: samples
  before 07:20 hit DeepInfra (~1.5–10s), samples after hit NVIDIA (5.9–45s, then HTTP 429s on
  the measurement traffic itself). Run `openshell inference get` **before and after** any timing
  run and discard the run if it changed. NVIDIA rate-limits ad-hoc measurement traffic well
  before it affects the bots — a 429 on your own probe is not evidence the agents are degraded.
- **A deactivated seat's bot goes silent but alive** — deactivation revokes the *badge*
  (instantly — doorman re-reads badges.json per request), not the container or Telegram bot.
  A "zombie chat" answers small talk but every spine call 403s, until recycled. Related mask:
  sandboxes have OpenClaw's native `web.fetch` enabled, so a bot with a dead spine MCP can
  still "answer things" — don't let that fool a diagnosis.
- **Don't verify badges by extracting them from `/proc/<pid>/environ`** — that method
  produced false 403s (never diagnosed). Use the runbook's minted-badge gate or a live agent
  call.
- Small but sharp: non-interactive ssh lacks `nemoclaw` on PATH
  (`export PATH=$HOME/.local/bin:$PATH`); `nemoclaw upload` writes 640 but the gateway needs
  660 (`doctor --fix`); `nemoclaw exec` eats stdin in heredocs (`</dev/null`).
- **A crashed `gateway restart` leaves a stale shields-transition lock that blocks every later
  restart** (observed 2026-07-25 on s8, from a restart that died 07-24): the next restart waits
  30s then aborts with "Timed out … waiting for shields transition lock … recorded owner PID
  <n> is not running". NemoClaw refuses to clear it itself; the fix is exactly what the error
  says — confirm the owner PID is dead and no transition process is running (`ps`), then remove
  `~/.nemoclaw/state/shields-transition-lock-<s>.json` on the box and retry. Side effect of the
  original crash: the sandbox keeps running its PRE-restart config (s8 showed
  "live gateway differs from onboarded" drift for a full day).
- **CLI chat probes run on the EMBEDDED agent, not the gateway** (observed 2026-07-23,
  4a.5 detach probe): `nemoclaw <s> agent --agent main --session-id <fresh> -m "…"` gets
  "gateway closed (1008): pairing required" (the box CLI device isn't gateway-paired) and
  falls back to an embedded agent reading the same `openclaw.json` — so a CLI probe DOES
  exercise deployed Render code + doorman + badge + real tool choice, but NOT the gateway's
  cached tools/list, and it TRUNCATES AGENTS.md at 12,000 chars (`[agent/embedded] …
  truncating`) — never judge AGENTS.md-dependent behavior (board-link lines, batching,
  contract parentheticals) from a CLI probe; the Telegram path is the authority. Use a
  fresh `--session-id` so probes never pollute the user-facing chat context.
  > **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-12 — that fallback was CONDITIONAL, and the condition is gone.**
  > The embedded agent is opt-in: `openclaw agent --local` selects it. Without `--local` the
  > CLI drives the **gateway** over its WebSocket, and once device pairing works that is what
  > happens. Verified the same day on both s2 and s6: the gateway log shows
  > `[ws] ⇄ res ✓ agent … runId=…` followed by `[agents/tool-policy] tool policy removed 4
  > tool(s) via tools.deny`, **no** `[agent/embedded]` line and **no** truncation warning — so
  > full AGENTS.md and the gateway's cached tools/list are both in play. The 2026-07-23
  > observation was a *pairing failure* being described as CLI behaviour, and reading it as the
  > latter cost this project several hours and three wrong conclusions.
  > **How to tell which agent answered, every time:** look for `[ws] ⇄ res ✓ agent` in
  > `/sandbox/.openclaw/logs/gateway-persistent.log` (gateway) versus `[agent/embedded]` +
  > `truncating` in the CLI's own stdout (embedded). Never infer it from the command alone.
  > **Consequence for date-eval:** if its runs fell back to embedded, they measured a doctrine
  > short by ~6,477 chars — a different prompt than production. Re-check before trusting its
  > phase attribution. **Useful flags** (`openclaw agent --help`): `--deliver` sends the reply
  > out through a channel (this is what makes the outbound plugin hooks fire — the weekday guard
  > logged a flagged mismatch on exactly such a run), `--channel/--reply-channel/--reply-to/
  > --reply-account` steer delivery, `--session-id`/`--session-key` make the transcript
  > addressable, `--message-file` avoids shell quoting entirely.
- **`/hooks/agent` (the gateway's HTTP webhook) CANNOT drive an agent turn on this box.** It
  admits the request (`200 {"ok":true,"runId":…}` in ~30-60ms) and then **skips the run**:
  hook runs execute through the **cron** runner, which preflights any provider whose `baseUrl`
  is loopback / private-network / `.local`
  ([cron docs](https://docs.openclaw.ai/automation/cron-jobs)). OpenShell's endpoint is
  `https://inference.local/v1`, so it is probed — and the probe does a **direct `getaddrinfo`**
  in a namespace with **no public DNS at all** (`api.deepinfra.com` does not resolve either;
  everything egresses via the proxy at `10.200.0.1:3128`, which does the resolving). Real model
  calls work precisely because they go through that proxy, so the preflight is a **false
  negative on a working provider**:
  `Agent cron job uses inference/z-ai/glm-5.2 but the local provider endpoint is not reachable
  at https://inference.local/v1. Skipping this cron run … getaddrinfo EAI_AGAIN inference.local`
  Tested with `deliver:false` **and** `deliver:true` — identical skip, 16 minutes apart (so the
  5-minute reachability cache had expired; `EAI_AGAIN` is nominally "temporary" but is permanent
  here). `deliver` cannot help: the preflight is the entrance, `deliver` is the exit. The docs
  state the preflight **cannot be disabled** (`--fallbacks ""` only narrows the walk to the
  primary, which is the failing candidate). **Use the CLI gateway path instead.**
  **Latent production risk from the same root cause:** any *isolated agent cron job* a manager
  schedules would be silently skipped — and skipped runs do not increment execution-error
  backoff by default, so nobody is told. Nothing is broken today (s2 has no cron jobs and zero
  occurrences), but §0h doctrine actively invites recurring work ("run the freeze every Friday
  morning" is named as the canonical legitimate recurring job). Mitigations:
  `failureAlert.includeSkipped` to surface repeated skips, or give the inference provider a
  `baseUrl` that is not loopback/private/`.local` so it stops being preflighted at all.

**Reading our own docs:**
- **A status gate outlives its truth — date it, or delete it.** The onboarding runbook carried
  "Phone-call capture … **is not merged to `main` and not deployed**. Until it lands, Phase 5
  cannot run and the firm onboards without the phone channel" from 2026-08-06 until 2026-08-17.
  It stopped being true the moment `7f59a54` landed, and for eleven days the runbook instructed
  operators to skip provisioning a paying firm's phone number for a channel that was live. Nobody
  updated it because merging the branch and updating the doc that *gates on* the branch are
  different actions, and only one was in the PR. **When a doc asserts the state of the world,
  stamp it with a date and how to re-verify; when you read one, re-verify before acting.** The
  same day, a growth analysis repeated the stale claim twice to the founder because it trusted the
  doc over the code.
- **Resolve merge state with `merge-base`, never by eyeballing `git log` on a feature branch.**
  The 2026-08-06 call-classifier research recorded a contradiction it could not settle — the
  working tree was on `feat/phone-call-capture` and `git log` showed no merge commit, while
  project memory said "merged to main + redeployed". Memory was right; the branch simply had not
  fetched. `git merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> origin/main` after a fresh `git fetch` answers this
  in one command. Pair it with a read-back for *deployed* (here: the pilot's `call_ingest` had 7
  rows across 1 number) — merged and deployed remain separate questions.

**Working on the spine / Composio:**
- **When inbound email "stops working", check the webhook subscription FIRST** — it's
  SDK-only config (no dashboard page; `GET /api/v3/webhook_subscriptions`). It once pointed
  at a dead dev tunnel for days with zero errors surfaced anywhere.
- The Composio **master API key never goes in a sandbox** — the auth header the SDK returns
  for session MCP is the master key (act-as-anyone). Sandbox access is doorman-relayed
  through Render with per-person session pinning.
- Composio **Connect Links expire** (~hours) — treat as perishable, re-mint on demand.
- Session tool restrictions enforce with HTTP 400 code **4310** (docs wrongly say 403).
- Known race: Composio retries a webhook ~5s in while classify+extract still run → duplicate
  visit (L1 dedup holds). Owned by the async-ingestion work
  (`thoughts/shared/research/2026-06-29-async-webhook-ingestion.md`).
- A badge's tier comes from an ACTIVE roster row matching its ref — a manager badge without
  an active manager roster row silently acts as a plain technician.
- **Google Routes `optimizeWaypointOrder` over ONE intermediate returns the undocumented
  sentinel `optimizedIntermediateWaypointIndex: [-1]`** (docs imply `[0]`; observed live
  2026-07-20). Our permutation guard rightly rejects it, so every 3-stop day silently
  degraded to unmeasured until `optimizeStopOrder` learned to skip the flag when there is
  nothing to reorder (fix + pinning test in `src/routes/googleRoutes.ts`). Moral: the
  fake-Google tests can't catch what only the live API does — run the key-gated live tests
  whenever `GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` is available.

- **Fleet rollouts must enumerate live sandboxes from the box, never from a remembered or
  written-down map** (observed 2026-07-22: the set_promise wire+restart pass covered s2/s3/s5
  from the recorded map and missed s7 — a live, bound manager sandbox added since — leaving it
  on the stale cached tool list until asked). Ground truth: `nemoclaw list` for the container
  set, then `nemoclaw <s> channels status` per sandbox for bindings. Do NOT trust
  `~/.nemoclaw/sandboxes.json` top-level fields for this — botToken/allowedIds read as
  null/absent there even for live, bound sandboxes; `channels status` is the only honest
  binding probe (it reads the rendered runtime config).

**Designing agent behavior (the meta-lesson):** GLM-5.2 does not reliably obey standing
instructions — proven repeatedly (skipped `endTime` despite rules → PR #53 made it required;
skipped the board link despite "offer it" → PR #59 fused it into results; resolved "next
week" ambiguity despite a mandate to ask). **When a behavior matters, move it into the
mechanism** (required params, result-embedded directives, server-side resolution like
`resolve_date`) — prompt rules are steering, not enforcement. Same family: an
append-once AGENTS.md block drifts stale as the product moves — refreshable blocks
(delete-then-append per `wire` run) fixed that class; and long/bulk tasks need the batching
mechanism in [long-running-task-batching.md](long-running-task-batching.md).

- **`npm test` truncates the local stack, and that wipes the spine-eval world.** The suite's
  own cleanup (`src/test.setup.ts`) empties the spine tables but leaves `tenants` alone, so an
  eval world built before a test run comes back as tenant rows with **zero** customers, jobs and
  visits. Every read then returns `[]` and the tier-1 report showed 34 confident, meaningless
  failures. Rebuild with `npm run spine-eval:world` after running the suite; both eval drivers
  now check for this and refuse with one line instead of grading a ghost.
- **An eval's own fixtures collide unless each scenario owns its rows AND its people.** Two
  rounds of this in one afternoon (2026-08-12). First, tier 1 cancelled job `rs-04` and tier 2
  then asked the agent to cancel the same visit — already gone — which reported as an agent
  failure. Per-tier customer/job pools fixed that, and then both tiers booked onto
  `cfg-tech-06` on the same day and the `no_confirmed_overlap` exclusion refused the second.
  **Whatever a scenario writes to — a customer, a job, a visit, or a technician — it has to
  own.** Pools are now split `t1`/`t2` for both.
- **Fixture data must look like customer data.** The first fixture naming reused a shared name
  pool and told rows apart with a `(rs-01)` suffix. That produced two customers called "Aisha
  Nguyen" (the agent could not tell which it had been asked about) and taught the agent that
  `rs-05` was an identifier — it duly passed the literal string `"rs-05"` to a tool expecting a
  uuid. Unique realistic names, no harness markers.
- **A missing `BOARD_BOT_HANDLE` makes the agent invent board links.** With the variable unset
  no tool attaches a `boardNote`, and the tool descriptions say "no boardNote → say nothing
  about the board". On the first tier-2 run the agent instead fabricated
  `https://board.evalmain.com/schedule?date=…` from the tenant name. Same class as the
  2026-07-22 live finding (`board.example.com/v=<scheduleVersion>`): **instructed to always
  produce X, given no material for X, it produces one anyway.** Worth checking on Render, since
  the failure is silent and people tap the links.
- **`resolve_date` now has calendar-PERIOD modes** (`month`, `quarter`, `year`, all accepting
  negatives, commit `84e5ba7`, 2026-08-12). This closes the gap `scripts/date-eval/README.md`
  still documents as open — that README's "Known coverage gap: month, quarter, year" section
  and its `M01–M11` period questions predate the modes by one day and will grade differently
  now. The eval's 366-case date matrix covers all seven modes and passes 100%.
- **The evals and the production fleet spend from ONE DeepInfra balance — a heavy eval day can
  take the pilot's agents down.** Learned 2026-08-14 (the third balance-out): ~$10 of spine/date
  eval runs accelerated a balance already near zero, completions went 402 mid-run, and the
  medic correctly held the route ("AGENTS ARE DOWN") while ops_events recorded three outage
  windows — silently, because the Phase-4 notifier doesn't exist yet. Before any multi-dollar
  eval run: check the balance first (a 1-token completion probe answers in one call — 402 =
  out), and treat auto-top-up as the structural fix. The 402 is instant and non-retryable by
  design in every harness, so a balance-out mid-run poisons the tail of the run rather than
  hanging it.
- **"This job has no recorded type" from `find_slots` almost never means a data problem — it
  means the agent went in by `customerId`.** Solved 2026-08-14 (spine-eval PR #136, args now
  recorded per call): GLM's recurring habit is skipping the context read and calling
  `find_slots`/`book_visit` with `customerId` — which describes a HYPOTHETICAL new job, hence
  "no recorded type" — even when the manager's message names the job already on file. Seven of
  nine failures in the first 59-scenario pass shared this one signature, and it mints DUPLICATE
  jobs whose booking replies look perfect (only the job-scoped end-state checks catch it). B05
  passes because its script forces the context read first; M08 shows the model self-corrects
  after `get_customer_context`. Candidate product fix (a customerId-path guard that surfaces the
  customer's matching open jobs) is a pending user decision — cite this entry rather than
  re-deriving the diagnosis.
- **"The model is 94% of a turn" is a HARNESS number, not a production one — don't quote it as
  the latter.** The spine-eval's tier 2 measured every spine tool's p95 under 51ms and concluded
  the model dominates. True of that path, misleading about production, because tier 2 hands the
  model a **fresh** 42-tool list and **only** the spine's tools. Production gives it the gateway's
  **cached** `tools/list`, openclaw's **native** tools (all but the 10 denied) and openclaw's own
  system prompt on top, `thinkingDefault: adaptive`, the doorman round trip, and — **on manager
  seats only** — a second `composio` MCP server whose per-request `sessions.use` costs +2.28s/turn
  (§5 above). Tool-schema depth alone once moved a turn from 67s to 3s. Any latency claim has to
  name which path *and which seat shape* it measured.
- **`tools.toolSearch` is `false` on ALL SIX sandboxes (verified in-container 2026-08-12) — the
  §0j doctrine block now describes a surface that no longer exists.** The 2026-08-04 finding
  (tool search hid `resolve_date`, so a doctrine rule naming a tool silently broke) was fixed two
  ways, and the *config* fix outlived the memory of it: every seat now shows the model its tools
  directly. Consequences: (a) any claim that "tool search hides X" is stale — re-check
  `tools.toolSearch` before repeating it; (b) the §0j block appended to s2/s5–s8 is now **dead
  prompt text** describing a `tool_search → tool_describe → tool_call` route the model no longer
  has, which costs tokens on every turn and could actively mislead — worth a deliberate decision
  to keep or remove, not silent retention; (c) an eval that shows the model all tools directly is
  therefore **faithful** on this axis, not optimistic. **Also verified: `tools.deny` was the same
  10 native tools on s2/s5–s8 but `0` on s3** (untrimmed until the carve-out was retired 2026-08-20 —
  all six seats now carry the identical deny), and the
  `composio` MCP server exists **only** on the manager seats s2/s3 — s5–s8 are spine-only. Seat
  shape is not uniform; never generalise one seat's timing to the fleet.
- **The gateway HAS an authenticated HTTP entry — `POST /hooks/agent` — so a gateway-inclusive
  test needs NO Telegram account.** Recorded because we nearly built the expensive thing: the
  first draft of the tier-3 plan concluded "a real Telegram message is the only entry", which is
  **wrong**, and the in-repo evidence genuinely looks like it isn't: ingest is `getUpdates`
  **polling** (`bootstrap-box.sh:264`) so there is no *inbound Telegram* webhook to forge; the
  gateway listens on **18789 in a nested network namespace**, so it is unreachable from the host
  (reach it via ssh → `exec` inside the container); and the CLI path runs the **embedded** agent,
  skipping the cached `tools/list` *and* truncating AGENTS.md at 12,000 chars (§5 above) — against
  an 18,477-char doctrine that silently discards 6,477 characters including the board-link and
  batching rules, so **a CLI-driven number looks gateway-inclusive and is false**. What closes the
  gap is openclaw's own `hooks` config (`hooks.{enabled,token,path,mappings}`, `path` defaults to
  `/hooks`, **header-only** auth via `Authorization: Bearer` or `x-openclaw-token`, dedicated token
  — never a gateway auth secret):
  [configuration](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/configuration),
  [reference#hooks](https://docs.openclaw.ai/gateway/configuration-reference#hooks).
  `POST /hooks/agent` takes `message`/`agentId`/`sessionKey`/`sessionMode`/`deliver`/`channel`/`to`/
  `timeoutSeconds`; **`sessionMode:"isolated"` (the default) gives a fresh session per request**,
  which is how a gateway-path eval becomes repeatable at all — a real Telegram chat accumulates
  context between scenarios. It returns on **admission, not completion** (15s deadline; `200`
  admitted, `400` bad delivery coords, `409` session conflict, `502` prep failure, `503` deadline).
  `deliver:true` sends the reply out through a channel; `deliver:false` completes silently.
  **Unresolved and worth testing before relying on it:** whether the outbound plugin hooks fire on
  a webhook-triggered run. `reply_payload_sending` is documented as running "after payload
  normalization and before channel delivery, including replies routed back to the originating
  channel" ([plugins/hooks](https://docs.openclaw.ai/plugins/hooks)), which makes it *likely* — but
  hook behaviour varies by execution context (`agent_end` is fire-and-forget on gateway paths,
  awaited on one-shot CLI), and upstream
  [issue #29203](https://github.com/openclaw/openclaw/issues/29203) is exactly "internal hooks not
  triggered in auto-reply path". Settle it with the **proven 2026-08-11 probe**: make the agent
  emit a fabricated weekday pair and see whether `weekday-guard.jsonl` moves.
  **Lesson, the CLAUDE.md case study again:** "the vendor has no HTTP entry" was inferred from our
  own deployment configuring only `channelId:"telegram"`. Absence of a configured feature is not
  absence of the feature — check the vendor docs before designing around a missing capability.
- **Design consequence worth reusing: make the cheap tier the control group.** Tier 3 runs the
  *same* corpus and graders over the Telegram transport, so `tier3 − tier2` per scenario **is**
  the gateway's cost, attributed rather than guessed. Sharing the corpus is what makes the
  subtraction meaningful — a second eval with its own scenarios could not produce this.
- **The pilot DB is an accepted test-write target (user decision, 2026-08-12)** — *"You can touch
  the pilot. The live DB is used for testing anyways."* This reverses the earlier same-day
  "don't touch the pilot" constraint, and it is lifted **per path, not globally**: tiers 1/2 stay
  local-only and `src/eval/targetGuard.ts` (which refuses the pilot by ref) is **not** weakened to
  unlock a pilot path — a pilot path gets its own separate fail-closed gate. Never truncate the
  pilot; write inside a dedicated tenant and clean up by tenant-scoped delete. And note
  `raw_captures` still has **no delete path**, so every pilot test run appends **permanent** L1
  rows — stamp them in source metadata and report the count rather than letting the growth be
  discovered later. Prod stays read-only regardless.

- **L1 PINS L2: a stamped capture makes its customer permanently undeletable.** Found 2026-08-12
  building the eval world into the pilot. `raw_captures.customer_id/job_id/visit_id` are
  `on delete set null`, so deleting a customer *updates* the referencing capture — and the
  append-only trigger is `before update OR delete`. The delete therefore fails with the bare
  string `raw_captures is append-only`, from a statement that never mentions raw_captures.
  (The context_spine migration's own comment about "surviving the append-only trigger" refers to
  the OTHER direction — `customers.source_capture_id` — which is on the mutable side and is fine.)
  **Consequence: any world built in a database you cannot truncate is BUILD-ONCE.** Dropping the
  stamps to regain deletability is not a fix — `src/db/context.ts` reads them as a customer's
  evidence, so `get_customer_context` would go blind. To re-anchor an existing world, **shift it
  rather than rebuild it**: visit dates are the only anchor-dependent part and the schedule table
  is mutable (named `assignments` when this was written; `visits` since rung 9b, 2026-09-05). Whole-week shifts only (weekday alignment), in two passes (`no_confirmed_overlap` is a
  non-deferrable GiST exclusion, so a single-pass uniform shift transiently collides).
- **The pilot's migration LEDGER is incomplete — don't diagnose its schema from
  `list_migrations`.** On 2026-08-12 the ledger was missing four migrations that exist locally
  (`query_spine_reader`, `v_schedule_local_weekday`, `phone_call_ingest`,
  `nvidia_failover_rates`), which reads as "the pilot is four behind" and nearly got reported as
  broken production. Two of them were then found APPLIED — `v_customers`/`v_jobs`/`v_schedule`/
  `v_team`/`v_time_off`/`v_cancelled_visits` all exist, and `v_schedule` has `local_day` and
  `local_weekday`. They were applied out-of-band, so the ledger under-reports. **Check
  `information_schema` for the actual object, not the migration list.**
- **A new tenant in the pilot silently enlists in the lifecycle cron.** `src/jobs/lifecycle.ts`
  step 3 ensures a tier-scoped Composio session for **every** technician whose state is `active`,
  every 15 minutes — and eval technicians must be active or the schedule readers treat the roster
  as empty. So a 20-person synthetic tenant would mint **20 real Composio sessions** on the
  production Composio account within a quarter of an hour. Fix used: pre-fill
  `technicians.composio_session_id` with a deliberately-invalid sentinel
  (`eval-no-composio-session`), because `ensurePersonSession` short-circuits when the column is
  set. Generalises: **before adding a tenant to a live database, read what the cron sweeps over
  every tenant.** The other steps are safe by shape (pairing advancement, offboarding expiry and
  log ingestion all need Composio state a synthetic tenant does not have).

**A date label built from an offset-less string is a wrong sentence the date rail cannot catch
(guarded 2026-08-25).** Every human-facing time in the ladder comes from one of two formatters —
`clockLabel` (`src/teamLead/planner.ts`) and `wallClockLabel` (`src/jobs/watchers.ts`) — which
parse a string and format the result in a named zone. Per ECMA-262 a date-time **with** an offset
is absolute, and one **without** is read on the host's clock, so an offset-less input silently
means a different instant on a dev laptop than on Render: `2026-08-24T07:00:00` renders 7:00 AM in
UTC, 2:00 PM from an LA host, and **Sunday 10:00 PM** from Tokyo. Nothing in the repo produced that
shape — `naiveLocalISO` appends `.000Z` for exactly this reason, and PostgREST returns `+00:00` —
so the convention held while being invisible at every call site. The names pointed the other way:
the parameter was literally `naiveIso` and the header said "a naive-local string", which is
precisely the input that shifts; a hand-built test harness reproduced the shift by believing them.

Why it outranks a formatting nit: **these labels are the planner's date-safety mechanism.**
`buildGapView` hands them to the model as the only date vocabulary it may use, and
`cleanPlannerText` refuses any note containing a date outside that list. The rail therefore proves
the model **copied** the string — never that the string was **right**. A shifted label is laundered
into a confident, rail-approved sentence to a technician or a manager, and a one-day rollover is
invisible to every existing check. This is the same shape as the humans-never-check-dates rule:
date safety has to be machine-side on **both** ends.

The rule is now explicit rather than conventional: `parseAsUtc` (`src/schedule/availability.ts`)
reads an offset-less date-time as UTC and passes everything else — offset-stamped, date-only,
unparseable — to `Date.parse` untouched. Both formatters and all three zone helpers
(`localDateISO`, `localDayOfWeek`, `localTimeHHMM`) route through it, and the tests flip
`process.env.TZ` across UTC / LA / Tokyo so the invariant fails on a UTC CI box too, not just on a
non-UTC laptop. **Explicitly not affected, and left alone:** the naive-vs-naive comparisons in
`watchers.ts` and `nudgeKinds.ts` parse both sides the same way so any offset cancels, and
`MAX_UTC_OFFSET_MS` already absorbs ±14h of slop.

**Dead-looking pilot tables are not an outage — read the cron's tick summary first (2026-08-25).**
Every write surface in the pilot stopped within four minutes of each other on 2026-08-24
(`nudge_decisions` and `cost_events` at 07:31 local, `review_items` at 07:35) and stayed silent for
20 hours. Reasoned from the database alone that looked exactly like a dead cron, and it was called
one. It was not: `lifecycle-heart` was firing every 5 minutes throughout, `ok: true`, last
successful run confirmed live. **A healthy tick and a dead tick both write nothing** — the lanes
just skip, and only the per-tick JSON says why.

The real state, from that JSON: `watchers` reports `gapsFound: 13, freshGaps: 0, itemsOpened: 0,
nudgesWouldCreate: 0` and `teamLeadPlanner` reports `skippedNoGaps: 1`. Two structural stalls, not
a failure:
- **No reachable technician, so rung 1 can never mint a nudge.** Reachability is
  `active` + Telegram-bound + sandbox + host. Ben Shelton has the last three but has been
  `invited` since 2026-08-19; every other technician row has no Telegram binding. `nudgesWouldCreate: 0`
  is the tell. With no nudges the planner hits its empty-queue gate every tick and never calls the
  model — which is why 2026-08-24 cost $0.08 across 2 ticks where 2026-08-23 cost $0.58 across the
  full 24.
- **Nothing is fresh any more, and nothing new is arriving.** `fresh = gaps.filter(g =>
  !everSurfaced.has(g.id))` (watchers.ts:526) excludes any gap already flagged OR nudged, and all
  47 open `overdue_visit_gap` review items have never been drained. Meanwhile `gapsFound` decays
  tick by tick — 28 → 26 → 24 → 21 → 18 → 15 → **13** across 2026-08-24 — because the 72h lookback
  slides forward and old gaps age out with nothing entering behind them. The pilot tenant's demo
  schedule simply ran out after 08-23.
  *(Corrected 2026-08-25, same day it was written: this was first recorded as an UNEXPLAINED
  possible detector bug — "confirmed visits with technicians ended 08-24 09:00/12:00/14:00/15:45
  and were never flagged". They are not the pilot's. The sweep covers only tenants with Telegram
  bindings — **one**, `Default Tenant` `82bbc6f8-…`, which had ZERO confirmed visits ending 08-24.
  All four belong to `spine-eval-main`. **Scope every pilot query by tenant_id before calling
  something a bug** — an unscoped count silently mixes the eval tenants in.)*

Rule: **`list_logs` on `crn-d93iq7a8qa3s73ap6260` before diagnosing anything from table
timestamps.** The tick summary names every lane's skip reason in one read; table timestamps cannot
distinguish "not running" from "running and correctly idle".

**The manager heads-up push has never fired — `BOARD_BOT_TOKEN` is unset on `lifecycle-heart`
(found 2026-08-25).** `src/jobs/watchers.ts:321` gates the push on `!!process.env.BOARD_BOT_TOKEN`.
Runtime proof rather than a config read: every tick that opened an item logs
`"itemsOpened":1,"pushed":0,"pushSkipped":true`, and `"pushed":1` appears **zero times** across the
full 30-day Render log window. The pilot currently holds **94 open `overdue_visit_gap` review
items** and one resolved.

Setting the variable does not clear the backlog, because **the push is once-only by design**:
`if (opened > 0)` (watchers.ts:1274) fires only when something NEW opens, and the comment states the
intent — "a quiet queue never re-pings on any cadence." A fix notifies about future items and stays
silent about the 94 already sitting there.

The deeper shape, and the reason this reads as a design gap rather than a missing env var: **rung 2
has no ladder.** A technician gets ask → wait → escalate, re-examined every 30 minutes. A manager
gets one count-only line with no names, and nothing ever follows up. The planner is the component
that follows up, and it reads ONLY the `nudges` table — `grep review_items src/teamLead/planner.ts`
returns nothing. (It can query `v_review_items` through `spine_read` once it is already running on
some other gap, but a review item can never wake it.) So a gap routed to rung 2 — which is every
gap, while no technician is reachable — has **no owner in software at all**. This is the mechanism
behind "the manager's bucket has never drained" (§2, 2026-08-21); the batched end-of-day "record
all" recorded there as the intended fix is still not built.

## 6a. The build order — the harness rebuild (2026-08-28 → )

**Why there is a second ladder.** The first one (§6) built the nudge ladder INSIDE a fixed
structure: two gap kinds, a doctrine-enforced order of operations, ~16 global constants encoding one
firm archetype. The user's argument on 2026-08-28 was that the structure is the thing that is wrong:

> "we've basically defined all the nouns and verbs that the planner can do and we've defined the
> order of those verbs according to our own view of a gap's lifecycle… our imagination can almost
> never absorb the complexity of the world… today, we are building a harness for yesterday's
> models already."

Plus two rulings that follow from it: **the planner is non-negotiable** (a fallback for its absence
is contradictory — the software runs *under* a planner), and **lessons learned inside a rigid gap
structure are not worth much**. The audit is
`thoughts/shared/research/2026-08-28-the-harness-audit.md`.

**The shape being built toward:** the harness provides PLACES to put things, OBSERVATIONS about the
firm, the DATA the firm generates (the spine), and VERBS. The planner decides what to do, period —
one technician or two, escalate, close by going and finding out. Nothing else in the harness decides
what to do. Attention curation is a WEIGHTING, never a gate (user, 2026-08-28).

```
1  the planner never goes down    OpenRouter — one model id, ~10 providers   [MERGED #186, 8354733]
                                  planner Opus 5 → z-ai/glm-5.3              [deployed 2026-08-29]
2  the firm is data               tenant_settings; the send window DELETED   [MERGED #187, 24210a2]
3  the planner's memory           .md files it organises itself, firm-scoped  [BUILT 2026-08-30]
4  a gap is not an ask            detection stops implying a question         [MERGED #193, 245378b]
5  the sweep stops deciding       it observes; every judgment is the planner's [BUILT 2026-08-31; §5 below]
5.5 the planner's inbox           agent<->planner comms: desk, bucket, verbs    [BUILT 2026-08-31; §5.5 below]
6  intent verbs + the doctrine    the list becomes a SUGGESTION, not a state machine [BUILT 2026-09-02; §6 below]
6.5 one send verb                  escalate DIES; send anyone, gap-less   [BUILT 2026-09-02]
7  the record has authors        the planner opens its own; the seats are an eye  [BUILT 2026-09-02]
8  the schedule is the manager's  DEMOLISH the solver ritual + pinning   [BUILT 2026-09-04; §8b below]
9a the rails come out            promise ladder + stored window DELETED  [BUILT 2026-09-05; §8 below]
9b the visit is one object        committed→placed→happened→reported; absorbs old 8  [DEPLOYED 2026-09-06; §9b]
9c a visit carries a crew         visit_participants; the guard moves with the person; NO lead  [DEPLOYED 2026-09-06; §9c]
10 inbound ASKS before it creates CALL **AND EMAIL** → observation → focused planner pass → the manager's agent opens/books, cited  [DEPLOYED 2026-09-07; §10]
10b the new caller               inbound_unmatched → planner asks a PAIR → record_caller cites the call  [DEPLOYED 2026-09-08; live e2e: the ask PASSED, the answer FAILED — §10b-live below]
10b.2 the citing door on the seat  record_caller shared with ACTIVE MANAGERS (the manager's own seat is where a caller is settled); book_visit untimed EXPLICIT; address≠name; sender≠own mailbox  [plan 2026-09-08-the-citing-door-on-the-seat; NEXT]
10c an inform wakes the planner  unread to_planner message PINGS, like a call does — its own claim column  [user 2026-09-08; BLOCKED on notes doctrine — see ORDER below, now measured as a coin flip]
```

**Hard dependencies:** 1 < 5 · 4 < 6 · 6 < 7 · 2 < 3 · 6.5 < 7 · 7 < 9 · 8 < 9 · 9a < 9b ·
9b < 9c · 9b < 10 · 9b < 12 (9b changes what a gap's SUBJECT is — a visit id — so every detector
after it reads one visit). **9c and 10 are INDEPENDENT of each other** — both only need 9b.
**10b, 10c and 10d all need 10** and are independent of each other: 10b appends a KIND to the
existing ping loop (the helpers are already kind-blind), 10c adds a second WAKE SOURCE to it, and
10d changes who may WRITE. *(Superseded 2026-09-08: "10b and 10d are ONE PLAN" — 10b shipped ALONE
as PR #214 with `record_caller` as 10d's first slice.)* **The order now is 10b.2 → 10d** (user,
2026-09-09): the seat gets the citing door first and the chained e2e proves the loop closes; then
10d as ONE plan covering both sides — the planner gains the five verbs the door gives up, and the
door is reduced to L1 + identity + links. Do NOT split 10d's door side from its planner side:
`updateVisitStatus→completed` has exactly one call site in the codebase (the door's happened-flip),
so deleting the door's mint without handing the planner the verb strands every unbooked trip as
`confirmed` forever. 10c is separate and independent of both.

**Why an inform waking the planner is its own rung (10c), placed here.** It belongs to 5.5's
territory — the planner's inbox — but it cannot be built until 10 exists, because what it extends
is 10's ping loop. Three things make it more than a one-line filter change, all learned the hard
way and none of them optional:
- **It needs its own claim column.** Observations have `pinged_at`, separate from `status`. Office
  messages have only `status` (unread/read), and claiming by flipping to `read` means a crash
  mid-pass destroys the message — worse than the observation case, because nothing ever re-asks a
  message. A message is seen ONCE.
- **The scoping INVERTS.** The focused pass runs `listOfficeMessages: []` on purpose, so a ping
  about one call cannot swallow the inbox. A message-triggered pass needs the opposite: scoped to
  that one message.
- **The gate goes before the claim.** `focusedPassBlockedBy()` first, exactly as rung 10 phase 2's
  review demanded — a worker whose credentials are rejected would otherwise burn every message it
  touched, permanently. That is not hypothetical: it is the 401 rung 10 hit on deploy day, and on
  this path it would have been unrecoverable.

**ORDER: the planner's notes doctrine lands BEFORE 10c — and the gap is a COIN FLIP, not a
deterministic miss (re-measured 2026-09-08 22:31Z).** First measurement, 2026-09-07 23:47Z: a
manager told her seat "route all future job/visit messages to Sam", the seat correctly called
`inform_team_lead`, the planner read it on the 00:00 tick and wrote NOTHING — the message flipped
to `read` and the instruction was permanently gone. **Second measurement, the SAME instruction
from the same seat 2026-09-08 22:21Z:** read at the 22:31 tick, and the planner wrote
`MEMORY.md` + `firm/routing.md` (the quote, the author, the date) — the first rows ever in
`planner_memory`. The only deploy between the two (`b71cc10`) touched `plannerPrompt.ts` for the
10b quiet-note wording ONLY; the "WHEN A SEAT TELLS YOU SOMETHING" section and the notes doctrine
are byte-identical across both events. So: same prompt, same message, opposite outcomes. That is
WORSE for planning than "never writes it down" — a 50% mechanism looks fixed on any good day.
The diagnosis stands: that section offers exactly two things to do with a message (quote it to
close a gap, or `observe` it) and notes are not on the list, so a standing instruction falls
between them and survives only when the model reaches for `write_memory` unprompted. Pinging
first would only lose it thirteen minutes sooner. The doctrine fix needs an eval that runs the
same message N times, not one live look.

**Rung 10b.3 — the conversation decides WHICH (user, 2026-09-09; immediately after 10b.2's live
verification).** Found by the user while Test A was in flight, from a question the e2e could not
have surfaced: *if the email had not been nulled, and the customer had several threads with the
firm, would we find the right one?* No.

**The two keys answer different questions and the code treats them as one.** `matchIdentity`
returns `{customerId}` from phone/email/address and `{customerId, jobId?}` from the thread — the
thread is the ONLY key that knows the job. But the order is phone → email → thread → address with
an immediate return on a unique hit, so the moment a person key matches, the thread is never
consulted. The door then guesses (`recordFieldNote.ts:512-516`): most recently created
non-terminal job. So a known customer with a quote outstanding AND a repair booked, replying about
the older one, has their message filed against the newer job — cited, on the right person, looking
entirely correct. If the reply carries a commitment, the ask attaches there too. The code half
knows: the comment above the thread key says *"filing a reply on the wrong job of the right
customer is a quieter error than it looks"* — describing this exact failure from the branch that
usually never runs.

**Second finding, same investigation: the outbound email capture is stamped with NOBODY.**
`send_email` records `{to, subject, sentVia, threadId}` (`src/mcp/server.ts:3899-3914`) — no
`customerId`, no `jobId`. So a thread WE started carries a capture that `findCustomersByThread`
finds and learns nothing from. This is why the thread key is narrow today: it only fires on a
conversation we already filed an inbound end of. Stamping it would make the thread identify anyone
on any thread the firm opened — probably the cheaper half of making the key earn its place.

**Severity, honestly: low frequency today, rising with usage, ZERO detectability, medium impact.**
Not a crisis; it does not create a wrong trip or a wrong promise by itself. It bites a firm doing
well enough to have customers with two live jobs.

**THIRD FINDING, MERGED IN HERE (user, 2026-09-09): the firm's own replies never reach the
record.** Every seat's trigger is `{ interval: 1, labelIds: 'INBOX', userId: 'me' }`
(`src/composio/pairing.ts:247`), so a manager replying from Gmail directly is never captured — not
filtered, not dropped, never delivered. A job's correspondence is one-sided; the planner cannot
tell whether anybody answered (so "nobody has replied to this customer" is a conclusion it is not
entitled to draw); and a promise typed by hand binds the firm invisibly — rung 10's own failure
class, through a door rung 10 does not watch. It also starves the extractor's thread context,
whose L1 fallback holds only inbound halves. The 2026-09-08 memory line "app-sent human replies
never arrive (INBOX-only)" recorded this as a TESTING footnote; that framing was wrong.
**USER RULING 2026-09-09: everything enters L1 GATED** — a human's own sent mail goes through the
work/personal classifier, because L1 has no delete. The 2026-07-04 exception making AGENT sends
classifier-free stands (audit matters most when the action was inappropriate); flagged to the user
as a narrowing, not an overturn. Adding `SENT` is NOT a config one-liner: the agent's own sends
would duplicate (`sent:<messageId>` vs `<messageId>` are different dedup keys, and L1 cannot be
rewritten), and `ensureTrigger` returns an existing trigger's id WITHOUT reconfiguring it, so
already-paired seats keep the INBOX-only trigger and the change ships inert unless triggers are
recreated.

**RPI: no research (`2026-09-09-who-may-write-l2.md` already maps this exact territory), a SHORT
plan, then implement** (user, 2026-09-09). Plan written:
`thoughts/shared/plans/2026-09-09-the-conversation-decides-which.md`. ~20 lines plus tests, but two decisions belong in the
plan rather than at the keyboard:
- **The cross-customer hazard.** If a person key matched customer A and the thread's job belongs
  to customer B, taking the thread's job files against the WRONG customer's work — worse than the
  bug being fixed. Rule: adopt the thread's job ONLY when it belongs to the already-matched
  customer. Obvious stated, easy to miss while typing.
- **RESOLVED — where `send_email` gets the customer/job: BOTH routes.** An optional `jobId` when
  the agent has one, falling back to resolving the customer from a unique recipient address. The
  argument alone is forgettable; address resolution alone is wrong exactly when the address is
  ambiguous. Together they cover each other and neither can fabricate — no unique answer, no stamp.

**Rung 13 — the roster reconciles with Composio (user, 2026-09-09; deliberately at the TAIL).**
Measured while building 10b.2: `manager-sam` IS paired in Composio (every inbound capture, drop
and extraction cost row today attributes to it) and our roster row records **nothing** — no
`composio_connected_account_id`, no `connected_email`, no `composio_trigger_id`. Two live
consequences, one root cause: the Composio thread fetch is gated on
`tech?.composio_connected_account_id` (`src/inbound/webhook.ts:310`) so it **has never fired in
the pilot**, and `isOwnMailbox`'s authoritative arm reads `connected_email` so only its
single-recipient heuristic runs.

**Why the sweep never fixes it.** `checkPairing` writes those columns in ONE place — a CAS guarded
on `state === 'invited'` (`src/composio/pairing.ts:207`) — and the lifecycle sweep only selects
`['invited','pending_verification']` rows with `link_issued_at` set (`src/jobs/lifecycle.ts:271`).
`manager-sam` is `active` with **`link_issued_at` NULL**, so our connect-link flow never ran for
it at all: that pairing was made OUTSIDE the app (dashboard, or a hand-set user id). The sweep is
a one-way ratchet — it can create a pairing record and offboarding can delete one, but nothing
ever compares what Composio holds against what the roster says for a LIVE row, so this drift is
silent and permanent.

**Size: ~250 lines, one phase, half a day.** Almost everything is reused: `compositeUserId` +
`connectedAccounts.list({userIds})` (tenant-scoped by construction, so cross-tenant adoption is
impossible for free), `ensureTrigger` (**already idempotent** — it lists active triggers before
creating, so no duplicate-trigger hazard), `casTechnician`, and the `GMAIL_GET_PROFILE` + billing
block (worth extracting, ~20 lines). New: `reconcilePairing(tech)` ~50 lines, a sweep extension
~15, tests ~150.

**Two decisions it needs before it is written:**
- **The gate.** NOT `link_issued_at` — that skips the one broken row while billing a
  `GMAIL_GET_PROFILE` probe every 15 minutes for `ben-shelton`, who is legitimately unpaired (link
  issued 2026-07-12, never completed). Use EVIDENCE: reconcile only refs that inbound captures /
  `dropped_audit` rows are actually attributed to. Zero cost when there is no drift, and it is the
  signal that exposed this.
- **Does adopting open a `pairing_verification` item?** The normal flow opens one so a manager
  confirms the mailbox; adopting a pairing that has worked for weeks and then nagging about it is
  noise, but skipping it records a mailbox nobody verified.

**It is more than repair.** With `link_issued_at` null, reconciliation is the ONLY way the app can
ever learn about a connection made in the Composio dashboard — which is how this one was made.
Scope care: it touches the same CAS/state machine carrying "never resurrect an offboarded pairing"
and "never re-open an item the manager dismissed", both with review findings attached — hence its
own plan, not a patch. **Interim (user, 2026-09-09): hand-write the row when convenient; nothing
blocks 10b.2, whose guard catches the live specimen either way.**

**THE BOARD IS REBUILT AFTER THE WHOLE SCHEDULE STORY, not per rung (user, 2026-09-06):** *"We'll be
rebuilding the board after we build the entire schedule rung."* So do NOT spend effort keeping
`web/board/` pretty through 9c/10/11 — keep it WORKING (it is the manager's hands) and let the
rebuild absorb the UX. This is also why 9b's Telegram-board manual check was closed as deferred
rather than run.

**Rung 9 SPLIT at plan review (2026-09-05):** the ruling that killed the window turned rung 9 into
two independent pieces. **9a — the rails come out** deletes the promise ladder and the stored
window and touches no table structure beyond dropping four columns; it is BUILT. **9b — the visit
is one object** is the L2 remodel (`assignments` and `visits` become one object with a
committed→placed→happened→reported lifecycle, absorbing multi-technician from the old rung 8), and
is BUILT as of 2026-09-05 — minus multi-technician, which was split out again as **9c** (see §9b).
Splitting them meant 9a could land and deploy without waiting on the table merge.

**The ladder was REWRITTEN 2026-09-03 (user) — see §8 below for why.** The original rung 8 ("a visit
is not a person", researched + planned 2026-09-02/03) is SUPERSEDED: it patched the reading layer of
a schema rung 9 replaces. Its research stands; its plan is marked superseded, not deleted.

**Deploy rulings (user, 2026-09-03), amending the 2026-08-29 no-deploy rule:** rung 8 and rung 9 MAY
deploy on their own — the no-deploy rule was about the PLANNER, and neither rung touches it.
Scheduling is live on the pilot; the remodel needs a real data migration, decided at rung 9's plan
review. Rungs 10 and 11 stay separate. **Do NOT check whether the freeze was ever used before
deleting it** (user): *"the freeze is not important, don't rely on test data because test suite does
not reflect the real world"* — the decision is on design, and pilot/test-suite usage is not evidence
of real-world usage either way (the same rule as "never size from the pilot box").
**Standing constraint on 6 and 7:** neither may assume one person per visit — see §8 below.

**Nothing reaches production until the whole order is done AND evaluated** (user ruling,
2026-08-29). Per-build production caution is OFF; per-build correctness gates stay ON. Evaluation is
an end phase, not a per-build step — an eval written against the current planner structure goes
stale the moment the structure changes, which is the whole point of the rebuild.

**Ruled out, so nobody re-proposes it:** planner-wake-on-event (user). Cadence stays a timer.

### 7. The record has authors — RESHAPED 2026-09-02 (user), not yet planned

Research: `thoughts/shared/research/2026-09-02-the-detectors-report.md` (the ground at `a6fabae`,
the 6.5 branch; its ADDENDUM records this reshape). **Not yet planned.**

**The finding that forced the reshape: build 7 as chartered was nearly all already shipped.**
"The detectors stop minting" is DONE — the sweep files no question, opens no nudge, writes no
review item and moves no row's state (`flagNudgeGap` deleted in build 6 Phase 1; `watchers.ts`
says so at :553-557). It writes observations, which IS the recording build 4 asked for. Build 6
gave the planner `dismiss`/`recover` at every stage. What actually remained was ONE feature —
the planner cannot open its own observation, `createObservation` has exactly one production
caller — plus a decision. Too thin for a build.

**USER RULINGS 2026-09-02** (verbatim ask: *"maybe we can have the seat agents and manager agents
look out for contradictions/gaps as they form too. So the detector isn't the only 'eye' that the
planner gets, that the seat agent can also provide observations proactively."*):
