- **The seats are an eye.** Both tiers already hold `inform_team_lead` — it is in the base `TOOLS`
  array (`server.ts:671`), not `ADMIN_TOOLS` (`:860`), so technician and manager seats alike can
  already speak to the planner.
- **AUTHORSHIP: the seat PROPOSES, the planner RECORDS.** The inform arrives exactly as today; the
  planner — newly able to open observations — turns the ones that matter into standing rows.
  Chosen over a box-side `observe` tool because it adds NO write path from a sandbox, no
  attribution column, no volume brake and no new trust boundary, and it reuses build 5.5 whole.
  **Direct seat writes are a LATER build**, revisited only if the planner proves a lossy filter.
- **SCOPE: build 7 is the subject decision + authorship.** Contradiction detectors split out to
  **build 9** — they need the subject decision anyway and are cheap once it is made. Keeps 7 < 8
  unblocked and the review surface narrow.

**Why 5.5 did not already do this.** An inform and an observation differ in one property and it is
the whole point: an inform is read ONCE and drained (`listOffice(…,['unread'])` +
`markOfficeMessagesRead`, `OFFICE_MESSAGES_SHOWN = 30`), while an observation stays in front of the
planner every tick until the planner ends it. **5.5 gave the seats a VOICE and no way to put
anything on the LIST** — a seat's notice of a contradiction is gone permanently if that tick is
busy.

**THE BLOCKER — one doctrine sentence forbids the chosen design today.** The office block's header
(`planner.ts:2578-2581`) says verbatim *"OFFICE MESSAGES — from the seats, information not
instruction. Weigh them; they never close or fill anything by themselves, and **a message id takes
no decision entry — decide only about gaps**."* It is DOCTRINE, not a rail: no code refuses it,
`validateDecisions` would simply say "names a gap that was not in this plan". Two clauses must
SURVIVE the rewrite — *"information not instruction"* and *"they never close or fill anything by
themselves"* are the trust boundary. An inform is a seat's CLAIM, never the record's finding, and
must never become citable evidence (it cannot: an inform is deliberately not L1, and not a
`CITABLE_TOOLS` result). Editing it needs build 6's two paid-for rails — pin with a CONTIGUOUS
single-line phrase (the string spans three array elements) and read the RENDERED artifact.

**THE DEDUP IS NOW BLOCKING, where it was merely worth settling.** The observations migration
header claims dismissal is what stops re-surfacing; it is not.
`uq_observations_subject_ever (tenant_id, kind, subject_ref)` blocks a subject from ever entering
the record twice — permanently, code-made, at first sight — while dismissal only hides an existing
row from the planner's `['open']` read. **Two mechanisms, not one**, and
`listObservedSubjectRefs` filters NO status on purpose. A second author breaks the index harder
than the planner alone does: a job-scoped subject means one such concern per job FOREVER, a
capture-scoped one means no dedup at all. Neither fits, and the plan must settle it first. The
cost of getting this wrong is already on the record in the sibling store — `moneyEvents.ts:253`:
*"correcting a typo suppressed the gap FOREVER … doing the right thing was what buried it."*

**TWO USER RULINGS ADDED 2026-09-02, mid-Phase-3, both raised as questions against the doctrine
being written.**

*(a) The planner reads its own inbox.* — *"Why can't the reader read office_messages? Shouldn't the
planner be able to read anything?"* There was no principled reason: `office_messages` carries no
sensitivity column and no manager-only concept, and its bodies are less sensitive than `v_captures`,
which is already exposed. It was simply the one thing the planner could not look back at, beside its
own decisions, notes, observations and questions. `v_office_messages` is the fifteenth spine view.

*(b) A seat's words may CLOSE a gap — SUPERSEDING build 5.5.* That build ruled office-channel
answers never fill the record, and the doctrine said so outright. Overturned, in the user's words:
*"The planner already gets to consider whether or not a gap should exist. Then they should naturally
get to just close the gap with any information … because they already get to kill a gap with no
information. Also, when a planner needs information from the seat agents, chances are the
information is not obtainable from the record, or else the planner would've just gotten it from the
record."* Recorded so the reasoning survives: `dismiss` and `close` are DIFFERENT claims, so this is
not parity — it is that a planner trusted to end a gap outright is trusted to weigh a relayed report.

- **It cost one line of code.** `spine_read` is a CITABLE tool, so exposing the view made seat words
  quotable by itself; the shown bodies are additionally pushed into the haystack so the planner
  need not look up what is already on its screen. **Desk pairs are excluded** — they are answers
  given in the planner's own name, and quoting those is the self-citation the rail exists to stop.
- **The metric does not widen.** A citation from a lookup stamps no capture id (build 6 Phase 4), so
  `mv_gap_outcomes.closed_by_technician` is unmoved. Widening what may be CITED never widens what
  may be COUNTED.

**AND A RULING I HAD ALREADY VIOLATED, caught by the same message.** §6a entry 4 says a
planner-opened observation is *"a CAPABILITY, not a doctrine push: nothing encourages it (the
job-notepad posture)"*. Phase 3's first draft pushed in three places — *"if one is worth keeping,
observe it"*, *"say so while you can see it"*, *"you should when you have seen something those two
checks cannot"*. The push came from scarcity: I was describing a message that drains and cannot be
recovered, and urgency followed from it. Ruling (a) removed the scarcity, which is why the two
rulings arrived together and why the fix reads naturally rather than strained. All three passages
now end the way the notepad does: **Nothing tells you to make one.**

**Two things nothing in the design brakes, recorded rather than solved.**
- **Volume.** The gap list is the ONE unbounded block in the planner's prompt (every other block
  has a shown-count). Detectors are sparse because they are SQL with a narrow predicate —
  *"choosing what they fire on IS the attention upweighting"*. A model-authored eye carries no such
  weighting, and doctrine is the only lever the chosen design leaves.
- **The quiet-firm skip becomes materially more reachable.** `runPlanner` returns at
  `planner.ts:3327` when a firm has no open gaps AND no unread messages, BEFORE the dismissed sets
  are read — so a recover-only tick is unreachable. Build 7 makes dismissal the normal way to
  curate, which makes that state ordinary rather than exotic.

**A finding worth carrying into any build that touches the planner's reads.**
`listObservationsByStatus` PAGES past PostgREST's 1000-row cap and says why; its twin
`listNudgesByStatus` (`nudges.ts:114-128`) does NOT — a single unpaged `.select()`. Both sets are
unbounded since build 5 deleted the clocks, and that reader serves both the planner's gap read and
the sweep's alarm pass. One fails loudly at scale, the other silently. Not sized against the pilot,
per [[never-size-from-the-pilot-box]] — the SHAPE is the finding.

**Three corrections to earlier entries, made in the research doc rather than by superseding them**
(each was true when written): the "15 staged tokens" in §6a entry 6.5 is **13**, counted from the
code (5 `if` blocks + 8 in `STAGED_ACTION_KINDS`); the **4 dead-end review kinds are not written by
the detector sweep** at all (the inbound webhook and the calls worker own them), so they do not
leave "when detectors stop minting"; and entry 6's `query_spine` residue names a tool this identity
cannot reach — the surface is `spine_read`.

### 6. Intent verbs + the doctrine — BUILT 2026-09-02

Nine verbs became **seven**, and the question every phase asked was one question: when the planner
asks to do something, should code say no because a REAL thing breaks, or only because it does not
match our story of how a gap goes? Plan:
`thoughts/shared/plans/2026-09-01-intent-verbs-and-the-doctrine.md`. Five commits, `7e80496`
→ `ff34fd1`.

```
                  NOTICED          PENDING/QUEUED/ASKED
BEFORE  send      yes              no        (world-protecting: a second send is a no-op)
        hold      yes              NO        ← our story
        dismiss   yes              NO        ← our story
        close     NO  ← our story  yes
        recover   yes              NO        ← our story
        escalate  no               yes       ← a STAGE in a story; deleted
        leave     yes              yes       ← changed nothing; deleted
AFTER   everything works everywhere except `send`, which stays pending/noticed-only.
```

**`escalate` DELETED (Phase 1).** The most lifecycle-shaped verb in the vocabulary; build 6.5
replaces reaching a human with one generalized `send`, so reworking it would be thrown away. NO
MIGRATION — the CHECK stays a superset, historic rows read, and `escalate` moved from
`PLANNER_JUDGMENT_ACTIONS` into `attentionActions` so last week's handovers do not become this
week's neglect. The count-only manager push went with it: its ONLY trigger was an applied escalate,
so `PlannerSummary.pushSkipped` is gone too. `flagNudgeGap` deleted; **`review_items` keeps every
item ever filed** and every reader is unchanged.

**`leave` DELETED (Phase 2)**, and the reason it could go is the honest one: it was the only verb
that moved no part of the record, and the `unmentioned` tick mark already records silence for every
gap in the view. It was paying a verb for what code gave away free.

**`hold` reaches every open stage and gained a RELEASE (Phase 2).** Both an omitted key and an
explicit `null` release (providers differ on whether null-valued keys survive); a
present-but-unusable value is still refused loudly. A hold decision always carries the key, null
included — the first draft released by the ABSENCE of one, a coupling across two files no type
would catch. **A release on a gap that is not held is REFUSED**, or it is a silent no-op recorded
as applied.

**`dismiss`/`recover` reach every open stage (Phase 3), migration
`20260902120000_nudges_dismissed.sql`.** Not optional once escalate went: an asked gap nobody will
answer, whose record will never fill, had NO ENDING AT ALL. `'dismissed'` is a STATUS, not a flag —
it is absent from `OPEN_NUDGE_STATUSES`, so it vanishes from the planner's read, the delivery host
pull, the alarm pass and every guarded write **without any of them changing**. `prior_status`
records the stage it came from; a NULL prior is refused rather than guessed, because restoring an
`asked` row to `pending` invites a second send `uq_nudges_subject_ever` forbids outright.

**ONE TRANSLATION, deliberately.** `close` on an observation writes the observation's dismissal
carrying the citation. Everywhere else this design refuses rather than remaps; here there is no
judgment to remap — on a stage where nobody was asked, "the hole is filled" and "this no longer
needs attention" are the same claim about the record, the close rails do not soften, and only the
target table differs.

**The planner may cite what it looked up (Phase 4).** The rail's SHAPE is unchanged — a quote must
appear verbatim in text the model was shown — and only "shown" widens, to what a `spine_read`
returned this tick. Needed an `onResult` hook in `toolLoop.ts`: the executor returns the RAW value
while the model sees `stringifyResult`'s truncated copy, so checking against the raw one would
accept words cut off before the model ever read them.
- **A looked-up citation stamps NO capture id.** That column IS
  `mv_gap_outcomes.closed_by_technician`; widening what may be CITED must never widen what may be
  COUNTED.
- **THE PLANNER COULD CITE ITSELF**, found by live probe: `write_job_note` → `read_job_note` →
  close on basis `evidence` was ACCEPTED, laundering its own sentence into `nudges.resolution`
  under a basis meaning "a person's own words". Closed with `CITABLE_TOOLS` (`spine_read`,
  `get_customer_context`) plus a `call.ok` guard — a refused `submit_plan`'s error text echoes the
  model's own rejected quote.
- **ACCEPTED RESIDUE:** `query_spine` needs no FROM clause, so `select 'words I made up'` returns
  quotable text. No allowlist closes it and no shape check would (`select 'x' from v_jobs limit 1`
  defeats a require-a-view rule). Bounded by: no capture id stamped, every query written to
  `query_audit` with its SQL, and it takes SQL whose only purpose is to fabricate.
- **ORDERING:** the terminal-tool early return is INSIDE the per-call loop, so `[spine_read,
  submit_plan]` WORKS and only `[submit_plan, spine_read]` fails. The plan said the opposite and the
  first doctrine repeated it.

**Stuck became visible (Phase 5).** A queued gap renders a DELIVERY line. The ledgers live on the
group LEAD — only its counters are ever incremented — so members borrow them, and a member whose
lead has ended is LEADERLESS and frozen forever while its own counters read 0/0.
- **The regression this phase introduced and review caught:** the line first said "still being
  attempted", and the doctrine called such a gap one to leave alone. False in three reachable
  states — leaderless, inactive technician, and a box not pulling at all where `serve_count` stays
  0 forever. The phase whose purpose is *stuck becomes visible* had deleted the one line covering
  the commonest stuck row. It now prints AGE + counters and asserts nothing.

**Known blind spots, recorded rather than fixed.**
- `mv_gap_outcomes` lists four terminal statuses, so a **dismissed** ask falls OUTSIDE it entirely
  rather than landing in a wrong bucket. How a dismissed question should be counted is the
  evaluation phase's question.
- An **unbounded hold** on an asked row silences `asksUnattended` — the only operator-visible watch
  on an asked gap — for as long as the date says. Capping the verb would be code overruling the
  pacing judgment build 5 handed the planner.
- A **frozen delivery tells nobody** — counted, logged, never pushed. Now
  `docs/observability-gaps.md` entry 1.
- `runPlanner` skips a firm with no open gaps BEFORE reading the dismissed sets, so a
  **recover-only tick is unreachable**. Pre-existing.

**Eval damage, counted honestly.** 24 orphaned ACTION expectations (10 `action:'escalate'`, 14
`actionNot:'escalate'` — there is NO `leave` expectation; the one `action:'leave'` is a `history:`
SEED, the same seed-vs-expectation trap the corpus header warns about for `extend`). Plus 24 claim
clauses WEAKENED rather than orphaned: `plannerEvalGraders2.ts` grades `mustNotClaim`/`mustName`
over `[handoff_note, reason]`, and `handoff_note` is now never written, so they fire against
diagnostic copy instead of the manager-facing copy they were authored against. Four new `build6`
scenarios were added — and the first three had to be rewritten because they **passed if the planner
did nothing** (silence ⇒ no decisions ⇒ an empty claim haystack).

**Two process lessons, both paid for.**
1. **Read the RENDERED artifact, not the edit script.** Two Phase 4 doctrine fixes were reported
   done, committed as done, and had never landed — an edit script died on a failed assertion before
   writing the file. Caught only by rendering `PLANNER_SYSTEM` and reading it. Both are now pinned
   bidirectionally (`toContain` AND `not.toContain`).
2. **Pin prompt text with a CONTIGUOUS single-line phrase.** `PLANNER_SYSTEM` is a joined array; a
   pin spanning a line break can never match.

**A verification-agent hazard, fixed here.** Ten integration suites opened with
`from('tenants').select('id').limit(1).single()` — no ORDER BY — while meaning "the seed tenant",
whose timezone some of them hardcode. The local stack accumulates a tenant per integration run
(1000+), so it reliably picked a test-created UTC tenant instead, and `recordFieldNote.test.ts`
failed only after 5pm Pacific when UTC and LA dates diverge. Fixed in all ten (`e8efdd4`).

### 1b. The planner's execution envelope — BUILT 2026-08-29

Build 1's unfinished business, and a LIVE production failure mode rather than a design nicety. Full
detail: `thoughts/shared/research/2026-08-29-the-planner-runs-on-a-clock-it-cannot-see.md`.

`MAX_TOOL_LOOP_BUDGET_MS = 90_000` bounds **one firm's entire planning conversation** — every model
call and every tool call, not one call and not one tick. Exceeding it throws before the plan is
read, so a plan the model already submitted is **discarded whole**; the tick records
`unmentioned`/`refused` for every gap and applies nothing. Because the bound is deterministic wall
clock, a firm whose work outgrows it fails *identically every tick* — and since the planner is what
closes gaps, the queue grows and the failure becomes more certain. A ratchet, not a blip.

**User ruling (2026-08-29):** *"Planner model calls occur 30 minutes at a time, so it's ok if it
does something truly long horizon. I would not try to put a maximum number over the planner's head
to constrain it. Though I would try to keep it within 15 minutes or so for now. Then we can see if
the planner hits that."* → the envelope is a SAFETY BOUND, not a design constraint.

Two things whoever builds it must not miss:

- **The turn ceiling binds before the clock.** `MAX_TOOL_LOOP_TURNS = 8`, and the planner overrides
  neither ceiling. Raising the time budget alone buys ~112s per turn instead of ~11s — it does not
  buy long-horizon work. That needs the turn ceiling.
- **A 15-minute tick eats the cadence behind it.** Render runs at most one lifecycle cron at a time
  and **DELAYS** the next firing until the active run finishes — it does not drop it
  (https://render.com/docs/cronjobs, verified 2026-08-29; an earlier version of this entry and the
  comment at `src/jobs/lifecycle.ts:653` both said "skips", which is wrong). The effect on the
  planner is the same either way: a delayed firing lands on an off-grid minute and
  `plannerCadenceDue` will not call it due. Render stops a run only after 12 HOURS, so the platform
  is never the binding constraint. Firms are planned sequentially. One firm at full budget is
  safe (14:00 → 14:15, the 14:30 planner slot survives); TWO firms at full budget run through the
  next `:30` slot and miss it. The delayed watcher sweep is immaterial by comparison — its own
  deadlines are 18h and 24h.

**Do not size this against pilot latency figures collected before 2026-08-29T17:16Z** — they are
all Opus 5, whatever the date suggests (see [[planner-model-is-glm-53]]: deploying the OpenRouter
code was NOT the switch; the env var was, twelve hours later).

**MEASURED, GLM 5.3, first two real pilot ticks (2026-08-29):** 17:30:48Z → **16.2 s**, 18:00:37Z →
**12.2 s**; both 2 turns, 2 tool calls, 1 gap, `planned` + 1 applied. **Per model call: 5-9 s.**
Attribution is by `cost_events.provider='openrouter'` / `sku='glm-5.3-*'` inside the tick window,
never by date. Both ticks spent their one tool call on `recall_decisions` — the planner reading its
own prior decisions back.

**BUILT 2026-08-29** — `thoughts/shared/plans/2026-08-29-the-planner-execution-envelope.md`. The
measurement settles the shape: at ~8 s/turn the 8-turn ceiling stops the loop after ~64 s, so the
90 s clock **has never once fired**, and raising it alone would change nothing. User's calls: clock
→ 15 min, **no turn ceiling at all** for the planner (an explicit `maxTurns: null` opt-out; the
constant survives as the default for other callers), runaways **measured, not policed**, and the
model is **not** told its remaining time until build 6 rewrites doctrine anyway.

**What shipped.** `MAX_TOOL_LOOP_BUDGET_MS` 90s → **900s**, still hard-clamped (no caller may raise
it). `MAX_TOOL_LOOP_TURNS` stays 8 as the **default**; `maxTurns: null` is a new per-caller opt-out
and the planner is its only user. The eval clamps itself to **120s** (`EVAL_LOOP_BUDGET_MS`,
`plannerSim.ts`). The ratio is what matters — 900/120 = **7.5×** — because the absolute figure
depends on ticks, not scenarios: the corpus is 62 scenarios (66 until build 3 retired the four
`lessons` ones, 2026-08-30) but `runArc` runs up to `MAX_ARC_TICKS = 12` per scenario, each with its
own full budget, so a worst case is 62 × 12 × the per-tick clock (~25h at 120s, ~186h at 900s),
not 62 ×. An earlier draft of this entry quoted the
one-tick figures (~2.2h / ~16.5h) and was low by up to 12×. `npm run ticks` now prints `turns p50/max · duration p50/max` per firm, which is the
instrument for deciding whether a ceiling comes back.

**Two rails the final review added, both consequences of removing the turn ceiling:**
- **`MIN_TURN_BUDGET_MS = 15_000`** — the loop must not START a turn the clock cannot pay for.
  Without it, the NORMAL way an opted-out caller ends is not a budget throw at all: some turn begins
  with a few hundred ms left, the model call aborts, and the loop reports *"the provider did not
  respond"* — a plain `Error`, so the caller books a CAPACITY exhaustion as a provider OUTAGE, which
  is the one distinction `ToolLoopLimitError` exists to make. Never pre-empts the first turn.
- **`MAX_TOOL_CALL_MS = 90_000`** — one tool call's ceiling, independent of the loop's. The old 90s
  loop budget was implicitly this too; deriving it from the loop budget alone would have handed a
  single hung spine read the full fifteen minutes.

**And the instrument was blind to its own subject.** A tick that died on the clock was recorded with
`ms: 0` (`idlePlan`), so the duration distribution was computed over successes only and its max was
capped at the longest tick that FINISHED — *"a max drifting toward the clock"* could never fire.
`idlePlan` now takes real elapsed loop time on the error path, the report prints failed-tick
durations on their own line, and `limitHits` is printed at all (it never was).

**Consequences to hold on to:**
- `limitHits.turns` is now structurally unreachable for the planner. The key stays (asserted by
  `lifecycle.test.ts`, rides into `lifecycle_runs.summary`); a `turns` hit appearing there means
  somebody restored the ceiling.
- A runaway is no longer distinguishable from a slow tick until the clock throws. Deliberate — the
  instrument is the distribution, not the counter. **Runaways are measured, not policed** (user
  choice): the two rules considered and NOT built were skip-the-next-tick and shrink-the-work.
- **Context exhaustion is now the de-facto outer limit.** ~128 turns × up to 8 tool results ×
  `MAX_TOOL_RESULT_CHARS` can exceed GLM 5.3's 1,310,720-token context
  (https://openrouter.ai/z-ai/glm-5.3). It lands in `tenantErrors`, not `limitHits` — a different
  signature. Model-dependent: a 128k-context model hits it far sooner.
- **The model is NOT told its remaining time** (user choice) — build 6 rewrites doctrine anyway.

**THE PLANNER GOT A SYSTEMIC DETECTOR** (user request, same day). It was the only lifecycle step
without one: `runPlanner` catches per tenant, so it returns normally however badly a firm did, and a
firm burning the whole clock every half hour left every `lifecycle_runs` row reading **`ok: true`** —
green forever with nothing working. `ok` is what becomes `process.exit(1)`, which is what Render
shows as a failed run and what `npm run ticks` groups under Failures, so a step that never sets it is
a step nobody hears. Now: **any loop-limit hit** turns the tick red (deterministic — the clock is
wall-clock and the input barely changes, so it repeats identically and never self-heals; one firm is
enough, or a single runaway hides behind nine healthy ones), **or** every firm erroring, which is the
watcher sweep's existing test. A lone provider blip stays green on purpose. The report names a limit
hit with its own diagnosis rather than the generic "every attempted row/tenant failed", which would
send the reader to the provider status page instead of to the queue that outgrew the budget.

**Deliberately NOT built (user choice, 2026-08-29):** an end-to-end rehearsal of the alarm, and a
clamp-down-only `PLANNER_BUDGET_MS` for inducing a real budget throw on a live tick. The alarm has
unit coverage (mutation-checked) but **has never fired against a real runaway** — no runaway has ever
been observed. Weigh that against [[manager-push-is-dead]], where an unexercised path was silently
dead for 30 days.

**BASELINE at the moment of the build — pilot last 24h, ATTRIBUTED BY PROVIDER** (`cost_events`
inside each run's `[started_at, finished_at]`, never by date):

| model | planned ticks | turns p50 / max | duration p50 / max |
|---|---|---|---|
| GLM 5.3 (`openrouter`) | 16 | 2 / **2** | 12.2s / **19.3s** |
| Opus 5 (`anthropic`) | 7 | 3 / **4** | 16.4s / **34.3s** |

GLM's worst tick is **19.3s against 900s — 47× of headroom** — and its turn count has never left 2.
Compare against the GLM row when deciding whether a turn ceiling returns.

**The instrument is model-blind, and it caught us out immediately.** `npm run ticks` reads
`lifecycle_runs` only; it has no provider column, so its distribution line mixes every model in the
window and the SLOWER one owns the max. The first draft of this entry recorded the blind line
(22 ticks, max 4 turns / 34.3s) as GLM's baseline — both of those maxima are Opus 5 — while quoting
the never-attribute-by-date rule three paragraphs above. Same error, one screen apart. Attribute
before quoting.

**One claim in the research doc is WRONG and the plan corrects it in place:** "reading `outcome` in
the catch would convert total loss into partial application." `submit_plan` is the loop's TERMINAL
tool (`toolLoop.ts:581`), so a successful submission returns the loop and cannot then hit a limit;
the only path that records a submission and keeps running is the wholly-refused one
(`planner.ts:1930`), where `decisions` is empty by definition. **There is nothing to salvage.** What
a limit throw destroys is the model's reasoning, which only ever existed in the message array — no
catch-block change can reach it.

### 3. Planner memory — reconcile against the spine BEFORE you plan — BUILT 2026-08-30

**USER DIRECTION 2026-08-29:** *"when the planners tick, they should first reorganize/recompose
themselves to fit with the updated database. Then they can continue working."*

The direction arrived while ruling out the obvious alternative. Asked whether a tick could "pick up"
the previous tick's work the way a long-running coding agent does — one conversation, auto-compacted
when it fills — the answer is that the planner's ticks are **not one task**: thirty minutes pass, a
technician replies, a gap closes, evidence lands. A persisted transcript carries forward assertions
that were true at 17:30 and false at 18:00, and compaction compresses the stale facts along with the
fresh ones. (Two mechanical problems too: Render's FS is ephemeral so the transcript would live in
the DB, and cross-tick caching is intermittent at a 30-min cadence — measured 2026-08-30, §4's
OpenRouter entry — so it cannot be relied on to make a growing transcript cheap either.)

**Reconcile-first is the answer to that, and it is the same relationship L3 already has to L1/L2:**
the spine is authoritative, memory is an interpretation layer over it, and **on any conflict the
spine wins.** A tick therefore opens by re-reading the firm's current state, correcting or retiring
what its memory now gets wrong, and only then plans.

**Why this matters more than it looks.** The memory design's own stated risk is that *"a planner
reading and writing its own memory every 30 min is a loop with no external correction"* — the ACE
context-collapse hazard `lessons.ts` cites — and the only answer on the table was the Reflector as a
periodic AUDITOR. **Reconciling against the database makes the database the corrector, every tick,
rather than a reviewer catching drift weekly.** The Reflector-as-auditor role survives; it stops
being the sole defence.

**Build 1b is effectively a prerequisite for this shape.** Reconcile-then-plan is two jobs in one
conversation. Under the old 8-turn ceiling, turns spent tidying memory came straight out of turns
spent planning — three or four each. With the turn ceiling gone and a 15-minute clock, both fit.

**HOW IT LANDED (2026-08-30, plan `thoughts/shared/plans/2026-08-30-the-planners-memory.md`).**
Three tables — `planner_memory` (one row per file per firm, path-keyed, `MEMORY.md` structurally
reserved by the CHECK's lowercase charset), `planner_memory_versions` (last ten per path), and
`planner_runs` (ONE row per firm, upserted, caller-supplied instant). Two tools, `read_memory` and
`write_memory`, the FIRST WRITE VERB on an identity that was read/ask only — it passes the dispatch
gate's rule because it is neither irreversible (versioned) nor externally visible, and
`src/teamLead/identity.ts` records that reasoning beside the allowlist. What is novel and has no
precedent: it is the first model call that mutates durable state from INSIDE the tool loop, with no
post-loop validation stage the way `applyDecision` has.

**The three open questions above, answered:**
- **Doctrine, not a code-enforced phase** (user). Enforcing it needs either a second model call per
  firm per tick or a prerequisite-tool mechanism the loop does not have.
- **Churn: no brake** (user — *"I'm fine with nothing stopping the 48 rewrites a day"*). Bounded on
  disk instead, by ten versions per path rather than by write frequency.
- **Crowding out: watched, not prevented.** Build 1b's turns/duration distribution (`npm run ticks`)
  is the instrument.

**FOUR RAILS DIED WITH `planner_lessons`, deliberately** — the evidence check on every proposal, the
`[8-hex]` citation stamp, two-strike retirement, and the date rail. **Reconcile-first is what
replaces all four**, which is why its paragraph in `PLANNER_SYSTEM` is load-bearing rather than
advisory and is pinned by a test. The date rail specifically was dropped on a user ruling
(2026-08-30, overturning the 2026-08-28 "code enforces the date rail" direction): it was built for a
300-char sentence where refusing on one bad date costs one rule, and on a 4,000-char document one
stray date would refuse the whole write — and a to-do file is exactly the file that carries dates.
Consequence recorded rather than mitigated: a memory can go stale and only reconcile-first catches it.

**THE HOLE, NAMED AND LEFT UNBUILT.** Reconcile-first only catches beliefs the record can
CONTRADICT. *"The manager prefers blunt messages"* is unfalsifiable by any spine query and can sit
wrong forever. The Reflector-as-auditor was the recorded future answer; it was never built, and the
Reflector itself is deleted (it imported from `lessons.ts` and its only output was lesson rows).

**Also lost, and owed:** nothing measures whether a memory ever changed a decision. The `[8-hex]`
stamp was the only mechanism, and the eval's four `lessons` scenarios retired with it — 4 scenarios
removed, no replacement, logged in `src/eval/plannerCorpus.ts`. The evaluation phase at the end of
the build order owns re-covering it. `npm run memory:planner` (read-only, replaces
`npm run lessons:planner`) prints what each firm has written and whether the index still mentions it
— the surface for watching whether a to-do bin emerges on its own. Nothing seeds one and the
doctrine is deliberately neutral about it (user): watch BOTH failure modes — no to-dos at all, and
memory that is all task chatter and no understanding.

**RECONCILE-FIRST HAS TWO BOUNDS, added 2026-08-30 on a user challenge, and they are the ONLY
brake that exists.** Asked what "reconcile" actually instructs, the honest answer was that the
paragraph said *what* to check against (this tick's gaps) and *when* to change (only on a
contradiction), but never *how much to read* — which a literal reader turns into "open every file
every tick". A file is ~1,000 tokens against a 6,080-token baseline, so ten files roughly triples
the tick's fresh input; within-tick caching makes the re-send across turns cheap, so the real cost
is ATTENTION, not money. The deeper tension the challenge exposed: **reconcile-first is both the
corrector and the churn source** — the same sentence that keeps memory honest invites rewriting it
48 times a day, which is the ACE context-collapse hazard `lessons.ts` cited. There is no code
brake (user ruling), so the prompt IS the mechanism:
- *"DO IT FROM THE INDEX, not by opening files … never as a routine sweep."* The index is already
  in the prompt and free to re-read; files are not.
- *"AND IF NOTHING CONTRADICTS, CHANGE NOTHING."* Leaving notes alone is the right outcome, not a
  skipped step.
Both are pinned by tests. **The instrument for whether it worked already exists:**
`npm run memory:planner` prints `updated_at` per file and the version counts ARE the churn signal.
One live tick (2026-08-30) read exactly one file — the orphan the footer named — and wrote nothing,
which is the desired behaviour, but n=1 says nothing about steady state.

**`gap_summaries` FOLLOWED THE REFLECTOR OUT (2026-08-30, plan
`thoughts/shared/plans/2026-08-30-retire-gap-summaries.md`, migration 20260831130000).** It was
written as the Reflector's reading material — *"the durable unit of planner experience"* — and once
build 3 deleted the Reflector it had NO production reader at all, while the lifecycle kept writing
one row per closed gap on every tick. `listRecentSummaries` and `countSummaries` ended with zero
callers; only the eval harness touched the rows. Retired with the sweep (`src/jobs/summarize.ts`),
lifecycle step 11, and `runArc`'s ⑤ epilogue.

**WHAT WAS GIVEN UP, named rather than glossed.** `manager_fate` — what the manager ACTUALLY did
with an escalation — was the only pre-computed answer to *"does escalating work at this firm"*, and
it was trustworthy ONLY because the sweep held a summary back up to 7 days until the review item
settled. That holdback is gone. `working_hours_open` was a STORED figure under the fixed 7-19
convention. **What survives is why the loss is affordable:** `review_items` / `v_review_items` still
carry `status`, `resolution` and `escalated_from_nudge`, and `nudge_decisions` still holds every
decision, is never pruned, and is readable per JOB — so escalation outcomes stay derivable live.
Only the rollup went. Two accessors died with it as collateral, both flagged by review rather than
noticed by a compiler: `findItemForNudge` (`src/db/reviewItems.ts`) and `CLOSED_NUDGE_STATUSES`,
which this plan had moved to `src/db/nudges.ts` for a caller the same change deleted.

**TWO THINGS THE BUILD DELIBERATELY DID NOT DO, named so nobody assumes they were missed:**
- **No cap on the NUMBER of memory files, or on total bytes per firm.** Per-file (4,000) and index
  (3,000) budgets are enforced; file count is not. A planner that mints one file per gap grows
  unboundedly, `renderMemoryIndex` scans them all every tick, and the only remedy is manual SQL —
  `npm run memory:planner` is read-only on purpose. The plan names *"memory that is all task
  chatter"* as a watched failure mode and gives the runtime no bound to hit. Decide at the
  evaluation phase, with the shape of real memory in front of you, rather than guessing a number now.
- **`write_memory` has NO mode gate**, so a `PLANNER_MODE=shadow` tick really does write
  `planner_memory` and really does stamp `planner_runs`. That is the point: shadow means "change
  nothing a person will see", and it has always written the planner's own records
  (`nudge_decisions`). Gating memory would make shadow useless for the one question it exists to
  answer here — what does this planner's memory look like after a week of ticks?

**`REFLECTOR_MODE` is dead code and must be deleted from the `lifecycle-heart` Render service**
(§ its entry below still records it as `shadow`). It is inert rather than harmful, but a live
variable pointing at deleted code is exactly what confuses the next reader. **DELETE IT AT DEPLOY
TIME, NOT NOW** — the pilot is still running the OLD build, where `REFLECTOR_MODE=shadow` is live
code, so removing the variable today would silently switch off a currently-running shadow reflector
in production. That conflicts with the standing ruling that the harness rebuild stays out of
production until the whole build order is evaluated.

### 4. A gap is not an ask — the observation record is planner-kept (direction 2026-08-31)

Research: `thoughts/shared/research/2026-08-30-a-gap-is-not-an-ask.md` (the ground at `a60af27`;
its addendum records this direction). **Not yet planned.**

**The finding that frames the build — a one-line contradiction carried since 2026-08-17.** The
nudges migration opens *"One row = one question the team-lead identity ever CONSIDERS asking a
technician"*; the planner's doctrine opens *"A gap is a HOLE IN THE RECORD, not an unanswered
message"* (`plannerPrompt.ts:34`). The prompt already says what build 4 wants; the data says the
opposite — `technician_id` at creation, statuses `pending/queued/asked`, an 18h delivery clock and
a 24h answer clock. Checked, not assumed: there is NO representation anywhere of "the system
noticed something" that is not a question to a person, and a gap past the per-tick cap is not
stored at all — the system's whole memory of noticing IS the ask it sent.

**USER DIRECTION 2026-08-31 — the observation record:**

- **The record grows without bound.** Every detector finding becomes a row; the per-tick cap's
  silent drop dies. (A "500 open observations" volume worry was raised and WITHDRAWN — it was
  sized from the pilot's seeded schedule, where nobody ever reports; the never-size-from-the-pilot
  rule's third instance. A real firm's loop is self-draining: gap opens → planner chases → report
  lands → gap closes. Steady state ≈ daily visits × time-to-report.)
- **The planner curates it.** It may DISMISS an observation it judges inconsequential — dismissal
  is what stops the detector re-reporting that subject — and RECOVER one later when stakes rise.
  Today's dismissal is permanent, code-made, and decided at first sight
  (`uq_nudges_subject_ever` + the `fresh` filter); it becomes planner-writable and revisable.
  **Same store, new owner.**
- **Detectors stay ours, and that is the point.** They are the planner's EYES: by choosing what
  they fire on (no report, no cost) we upweight what we think matters. Feeding the planner is
  deliberately forcing it to look. The planner then decides for itself what to act on, what to
  keep, what to drop.
- **The detector must not be the only way a gap opens.** The planner may read L1/L2 itself and
  open its OWN observation — and conduct an ask on it. A CAPABILITY, not a doctrine push: nothing
  encourages it (the job-notepad posture — "nothing tells you to write one").

**Consequences across the order** (the direction spans 4 and 7, and touches 5 and 6):

- Build 4 defines the OBJECT (observation ≠ ask); build 7 re-homes AUTHORSHIP (detectors report,
  the planner keeps the record). They meet at the dismissal store.
- Build 5: a planner-opened observation has no `NudgeKindSpec`, and today a kind with no spec is
  invisible to the whole sweep — never answered, escalated, or expired (`watchers.ts:487-497`).
  The sweep that stops deciding must also stop needing per-kind specs to decide with.
- Build 6: dismiss / recover / open-an-observation are intents the verb vocabulary must express.
- The dispatch gate: opening an observation is internal and reversible, so a planner-opened gap
  passed the then-rule ("nothing irreversible or externally visible") unchanged — a standing
  argument for the observation being its own record rather than a checkbox on the ask row. (That
  rule is retired as of rung 10b — see §the team-lead surface above — but the argument for the
  observation being its own record stands on its own.)

### 5. The sweep stops deciding — clocks become alarms (BUILT 2026-08-31)

Research: `thoughts/shared/research/2026-08-30-the-sweep-stops-deciding.md`. Plan:
`thoughts/shared/plans/2026-08-31-the-sweep-stops-deciding.md`. **Six user rulings, all final**
(2026-08-31): (1) the acting clocks are DELETED — alarms only, computed sweep-side so they
survive planner death; (2) moot becomes INFORMATION — the fact reaches the gap view as a THE
RECORD line and the planner ends the gap; (3) `escalate` performs its own handoff at apply time
(the send-collapse pattern); (4) the machinery give-ups (18h never-delivered, serve cap, attempt
cap) are demoted to OPERATOR alarms — the solo-operator ruling's "delete the handoff pretence"
applied; (5) item auto-resolve stays (the manager queue keeps self-cleaning — the sweep's ONE
remaining judgment); (6) all riders in.

**This AMENDS the 2026-08-22 ruling** ("moot, escalate, expire and the delivery caps are FACTS;
only `filled` is a judgment", §6 below): those facts stopped ACTING. The facts are still
computed — as information (the moot line) and as alarms — but nothing closes, escalates,
expires or hands anything to anyone except the planner.

**What the sweep is now:** detect → record observations (uncapped) → self-clean the manager's
queue (`itemResolved`, the slimmed `NudgeKindSpec`'s one judgment) → raise alarms. The alarm
pass counts, every five minutes and with no model: `asksUnattended` (asked ≥24h, no applied
planner judgment in the window — `leave` counts), `deliveryStuck` (pending/queued ≥18h, or
frozen at the serve/attempt caps), `observationsStale` (open ≥72h, untouched in the window —
`leave` does NOT count; build 4's counter re-homed from the planner). Thresholds inherit the
dead deadlines' numbers as WATCH values, deliberately un-re-derived. Never `ok=false`; the
surfaces are `npm run ticks` ("## Alarms", last-write-wins snapshot) and per-tenant stderr with
≤5 ids per class. A failed decisions read counts every candidate — fail toward alarming.

**Consequences to hold:**
- `extend` RETIRED with the clock (7 planner verbs). `escalate_after` is a dormant column;
  `not_before` was already enforcer-less — a hold is real because the planner honors its own
  note. Build 6 may reintroduce a pacing intent.
- `escalate` requires a surviving `handoffNote` (no fallback author — refused at apply
  otherwise, the send pattern) and works on pending/queued/asked (a stuck relic can be handed
  over deliberately); still refused on `noticed`. The count-only manager heads-up moved to the
  planner tick, item-opened-gated, same `BOARD_BOT_TOKEN` gate.
- A moot-authorised `close` (basis 'record' via the moot line) ends the gap **'cancelled'**,
  the status the sweep's moot wrote — `mv_gap_outcomes` keeps bucketing honestly. The
  record-basis chain is `recordFilled ?? moneyOnRecord ?? mootFact`.
- The planner sees EVERY kind now (its spec-registry filter died); a frozen queued row exits
  only through the planner. The tenant evidence/money read is clamped to
  `EVIDENCE_FLOOR_MAX_DAYS = 30` — gaps have no lifetime, so the oldest open ask no longer
  bounds it; evidence older than the window is reachable via spine_read but not citable.
- **`NUDGES_MODE` is RETIRED** — delete the env var from the lifecycle-heart Render service AT
  DEPLOY TIME (it joins `REFLECTOR_MODE` on that list; the harness rebuild is still
  not deployed). `ladderMute` now keys on WATCHERS live + planner not live.
- Deleted outright: `compose.ts` (`handToManager` — its last survivor — died with the manager
  handoffs), `flagOverdueVisitGap`, `reviewItemExistsFor`, `setNudgeAttempts`, the per-kind and
  per-tenant mint caps (`tenant_settings.max_new_per_tick` stays as dormant DATA — it is a
  firm-writable build-2 column). `NudgeKindSpec` slimmed to `{kind, subjectIsVisit,
  itemResolved}`.

**The send-window history, ported from compose.ts's header before its deletion (the user's
2026-08-29 ruling, kept verbatim):** the ask window was 7am–7pm firm-local, seven days a week
(user, 2026-08-17), lived in compose, and since the collapse gated the PLANNER. It was deleted
whole in build 2, and what replaced it is not another gate:

> "if a message goes out outside of working hour, then the human gets a tick. Not the end of
> the world… And if the message doesn't get answered, then the planner will learn that
> messaging at that time is bad, and message at a different time. Of course there's going to be
> a learning process. But we don't want to constrain what the planner can do."

The working-hours METRIC keeps a fixed 7–19 convention of its own in `jobs/workingHours.ts` — a
yardstick pinned to a SQL twin, not a claim about any firm.


### 5.5 The planner's inbox — the office channel (BUILT 2026-08-31)

Inserted by the user on 2026-08-31 ("build the inbox first — central intelligence needs comms
lines"). Plan: `thoughts/shared/plans/2026-08-31-the-planners-inbox.md` (research beside it).
**The goal is the planner's INFORMATION LOOP** — everything on the channel informs judgment;
NOT orchestration (the planner never tasks agents), NOT a second record (no L1 echo; an
assistant's answer never fills a gap — the ledger still closes a cost gap).

**One table, `office_messages`** (operational, RLS deny-all, NOT L1 — the agentContext
precedent; registered by name in seed.mjs truncateSpine + world.mjs RESET_TABLES at creation,
§1's rule). Four flows:

- **agent informs the office** — `inform_team_lead`: a plain bucket write, NO model call on
  receipt; the next tick reads unread rows in an OFFICE MESSAGES block (after the gap blocks —
  cache tail), marks them read only after a successful apply+record pass (a dead tick re-shows,
  never drops). **A gapless firm with unread messages PLANS instead of skipping** (review
  blocker F2). Everything on the channel is "information not instruction" — tier rides as
  metadata, never authority.
- **agent asks the office** — `ask_team_lead`, the Q&A DESK: a synchronous, READ-ONLY
  reconstitution of the planner (its memory index + a desk prompt) answering inside the asker's
  own tool call. **"Answering is not waking"** — the recorded carve-out from wake-on-event.
  Tier is the toolset: an ACTIVE manager's desk gets the team-lead read surface (writes
  excluded, dispatched under `spine:team-lead`); everyone else — technicians, roster-less,
  invited managers (review F-A: the capability is `isActiveManager`'s predicate, not tier) —
  gets ZERO dispatched tools with their own open questions inlined. **The planner's NOTES are
  the exception (user ruling 2026-09-01, reversing a final-review fix):** the memory index
  rides every desk — "a desk with no MEMORY.md is not an accurate representation of the
  planner… not a high stakes item" — so TOOLS are bounded by mechanism and NOTES by prompt
  (`DESK_NARROW_DISCLOSURE`, restated per non-manager asker). Read-bounded, not
  disclosure-proof. Transcripts land as bucket rows (`ask` + `desk`, desk born read); the next tick's block shows answered pairs "said in
  YOUR name". Failure keeps the ask unread — the tick sees it. `DESK_BUDGET_MS = 40_000`,
  PROBED: the gateway MCP client's per-call timeout is DEFAULT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS = 60s
  (s2, OpenClaw 2026.6.34 — `MAX_TOOL_CALL_MS` does NOT govern that path).
- **the office reaches an agent** — planner verbs `ask_agent`/`inform_agent` (gap-tied,
  `agentMessage` date-railed, addressee = the gap's technician_id — the FK the host pull's
  `technicians!inner` join needs; fourth amendment of `nudge_decisions_action_check`). Queued
  rows ride a second host-pull queue (`GET/POST /control/office`, nudgeQueue freeze doctrine)
  and `Box_Setup_Stuff/office-runner.sh` injects each as an agent turn via THE CLI DOOR:
  `openclaw agent --session-key main --message-file … --json`, **no --deliver** — probed live
  on s2: the turn runs via the gateway, lands in the Telegram chat's main session, and is
  recalled by later turns (decays on idle rotation — that IS the design). `gateway.hooks` is
  absent fleet-wide; the HTTP hook door was skipped entirely. Replies come back through
  `inform_team_lead(replyToMessageId)` — validated tenant-scoped or degraded to a plain inform
  (never a 23503) — and render attached to their gap in the next block.
- **seat-to-seat informs** — `inform_teammate` (user ruling: inform yes, ask NO — an answer
  path is the access-control surface the user declined). **TIER RULE: at least one endpoint
  must be a MANAGER**; tech→tech is refused with "put it on the record with a field note, or
  inform the office" (lateral flow routes through the hub: tech informs planner →
  `inform_agent`). Delivered turns are origin-honest: "NOTE FROM <name>'s assistant", never
  the office's voice.

Seats' doctrine: bootstrap-box.sh §0l "Messages to and from the office" (sync rule honoured).
plannerSim: world seed `officeMessages` + timeline step `office_inform` + `officeAfter`
read-back. Eval: tier-1 cases W14/W15/X09; tool surface 17/46/47 (**17/42/43 since rung 8** — the four solver tools left).

**Deploy notes (the rebuild is still NOT deployed):** migrations 20260901150000 +
20260901160000 BEFORE code; office-runner is a box rollout — s2 first, fleet only with the
whole-order deploy; a hosted reseed now clears the pilot's office_messages like its siblings.
Joins the 4–7 evaluation train. Deferred by ruling: re-ask (the once-ever index), escalate =
ask(manager) semantics (build 6 decision 2 — `nudge_id` on the bucket is its hook), unread-
aging alarms (the tick drains the bucket; tick-death is already alarmed).

**TRAP, hit 2026-09-02 while building 6.5 Phase 1: `npm run gen:types` reads the PILOT.** The
script is `npx supabase gen types typescript --linked`, and `--linked` is the hosted project
`daqapdlkgxfrhquacprv` — the deployed pilot's live DB, which is BEHIND main because deploys run no
migrations. Run it after adding a LOCAL migration and it does not add your table: it overwrites
`src/db/database.types.ts` with the pilot's schema (measured: 202 insertions, **374 deletions**,
and the `observations` table — on main since 2026-09-01, never migrated to the pilot — simply
disappears). Typecheck then fails somewhere unrelated. No guard fires, because it is a READ:
`testEnvGuard` protects the suite, not a codegen script. **Use
`npx supabase gen types typescript --local > src/db/database.types.ts`**, and check
`git diff --stat src/db/database.types.ts` is ADDITIVE — any large deletion count means the pilot's
schema just landed in the repo. The script itself is unchanged (flagged to the user, outside build
6.5's plan scope).

### 7. The record has authors — BUILT 2026-09-02

Plan: `thoughts/shared/plans/2026-09-02-the-record-has-authors.md`. Research:
`thoughts/shared/research/2026-09-02-the-detectors-report.md`. Four commits, `b25d735` → `d46ce40`.

**The charter was mostly already shipped, and the research is what established that.** "The
detectors stop minting" was done in build 4 (the sweep records observations and files nothing
else); "the planner curates" was done in build 6 (dismiss/recover at every stage). What was left
was AUTHORSHIP — `createObservation` had exactly one caller — plus one contradiction the
observations migration stated as a single mechanism and is really two.

**The two mechanisms, separated.** `uq_observations_subject_ever` compensates for the DETECTOR'S
BLINDNESS: it is stateless, runs every five minutes, and without a unique key would re-notice the
same visit forever. Dismissal is what hides a row from the PLANNER'S `['open']` read. The migration
header said the dismissal was what stopped re-surfacing; it never was. The index is now **partial on
`author='detector'`** — left whole it would have meant one planner observation per subject FOREVER,
the failure `moneyEvents.ts:253` already records on the sibling store.
- **A partial index is only usable when the query IMPLIES its predicate.** `listObservedSubjectRefs`
  had to learn `author='detector'` too, or the sweep's dedup read became a Seq Scan (measured
  33 → 770 at 20k rows) twice per tenant per five-minute tick, on a table with no delete path.

**`observe` is a VERB, not a tool** — a tool mutates durable state from inside the loop with no
post-loop validation stage (`identity.ts:25-30`). Six verbs now. It is the only one that CREATES a
gap rather than acting on one, so it names no gap id and takes the gap-less decision path — but
unlike a gap-less send its log row keys to `observation_id`, which is what makes it reachable from
per-gap recall.

**`subject_ref` is a fresh uuid for planner rows, and the reason is the OTHER table.**
`uq_nudges_subject_ever` is NOT partial, and a `send` on a noticed observation creates its nudge with
the observation's own kind and subject. With the job id as subject, two observes on one job would
collide the moment anything turned one into an ask, and the heal path would stamp the observation
`asked` though nobody was asked. Unreachable today only because `technicianId: null` makes
`countsAsTheAsk` always refuse — an incidental line, not a guarantee.

**FOUR USER RULINGS, three of them mid-implementation.**
1. **The seats are an eye, but the planner is the author** (2026-09-02). A box-side `observe` tool
   was the alternative: it would have needed a write path from a sandbox, an attribution column, a
   volume brake and a new trust boundary. Direct seat writes are a later build.
2. **The planner can read its own inbox** — `v_office_messages`. Raised as a question against the
   doctrine ("shouldn't the planner be able to read anything?"). There was no principled reason for
   the gap: no sensitivity column, bodies less sensitive than `v_captures`. It was simply the one
   thing the planner could not look back at, beside its own decisions, notes, observations and
   questions.
3. **A seat's words may CLOSE a gap — SUPERSEDING the 5.5 rule** that office-channel answers never
   fill the record. The user's argument: *"they already get to kill a gap with no information"*, and
   *"when a planner needs information from the seat agents, chances are the information is not
   obtainable from the record, or else the planner would've just gotten it from the record."* One
   asymmetry recorded so the reasoning survives: `dismiss` and `close` are different claims, so this
   is not parity — it is that a planner trusted to end a gap outright is trusted to weigh a
   technician's relayed report. **What does NOT widen: a citation stamps no capture id**, so
   `mv_gap_outcomes.closed_by_technician` is unmoved (build 6 Phase 4's rule).
4. **The doctrine STATES capabilities, it does not PUSH them** — and this caught a violation of a
   ruling already on the record. §6a entry 4 says a planner-opened observation is *"a CAPABILITY,
   not a doctrine push: nothing encourages it (the job-notepad posture)"*. The first draft pushed in
   three places. All three now end the way the notepad does: **Nothing tells you to make one.**

**THE FINAL REVIEW'S SHARPEST FINDING, and it is a pattern worth remembering.** `v_office_messages`
as first written exposed `to_agent` bodies — the planner's own send text — and `spine_read` is
CITABLE. That is the self-citation laundering build 6 Phase 4 found by live probe and shut, reopened
by a view added **in the same commit** that excluded desk pairs from `readText` for exactly that
reason. The view is now `direction = 'to_planner'` only. **Two guards elsewhere in a commit do not
protect a third door opened in it.**
- Also fixed: money authorised a record-basis close on a `planner_observation` (the first kind whose
  hole no code-computed fact can speak to — a deposit would have closed "the customer sounded ready
  to walk" on the record); and a jobless observation asserted a firm-wide negative from a job-scoped
  query that never ran.

**RECORDED, not fixed — the self-citation surface is wider than this build.** `dismiss_reason`
(v_observations) and `resolution` (v_nudges) are planner-written and already exposed to a CITABLE
tool. Closing that properly is evaluation-phase work; it is why `note` was NOT added to
`v_observations`, which leaves the doctrine's "find dismissed ones in v_observations" weakest for
exactly the rows this build creates.

**Two things the tests could not catch, both found by review.** Deleting the provenance map argument
passed all 2632 tests (every observe naming a message would have been silently refused in
production); deleting the context write passed too. The new tests drove `validateDecisions` and
`buildGapView` in isolation and neither end of the actual pipeline.

**Eval:** one `build7` scenario, and it needed a new expectation key. `action:` filters to SEEDED gap
keys and an observe creates its gap that tick; `gapLessAction:` filters on a null key and an
observe's row is keyed to the observation it made. Either would have graded the pre-build-7
behaviour and passed on it — so `anyAction` exists, and the 6.5 precedent (delete a scenario rather
than ship one that grades the wrong thing) is why.

**Scope deliberately NOT closed, flagged to the user and left by their choice:** §8 below still says
build 7 decides "one gap per assignment row vs one gap per visit". It does not — build 8 owns that,
and the user declined to amend the note.

### 6.5 One send verb — escalate dies (USER RULING 2026-09-01; BUILT 2026-09-02, all five phases)

**The ruling.** *"I think we should get rid of escalate and we should look into having a generalized
send verb where the planner can send a message to anyone, the agents, managers, technicians, etc.
And it doesn't have to be hardcoded to be related to the gap."* Slots between 6 and 7, hard dep
**6.5 < 7** — build 7 hands the planner authorship of the record and routes dead ends to humans by
judgment, so the human-routing verb must exist first. The gap between 6 and 6.5 (no way to reach a
manager) costs nothing: nothing is deployed and evaluation is an end phase (user, 2026-09-01).

**Why escalate dies rather than being reworked.** It is the most lifecycle-shaped verb in the
vocabulary — `WHEN TO GIVE UP` is literally a stage in a story about how a gap ends. The same day's
earlier ruling ("escalate = ask(manager)") is SUPERSEDED; reworking it would be discarded when the
generalized send lands, and deleting it is strictly less work. Build 6 Phase 1 is now the deletion.

**The structural fact 6.5 must solve (probed 2026-09-01).** Every planner verb hardcodes its
destination, and none lets the planner choose:

| verb | reaches | who chose |
|---|---|---|
| `send` | the gap's person, by text (verbatim, zero inference) | the RECORD (`view.nudge.technician_id`) |
| `ask_agent`/`inform_agent` | the gap's person's ASSISTANT | the RECORD (same field) |
| `escalate` | the firm's managers, via a queue | hardcoded |

So `escalate` was the only verb able to reach a human the gap does not name. **And every decision
must name a gap** — `required: ['nudgeId','action','confidence','reason']`
(`plannerPrompt.ts:409`), with `nudge_decisions` keyed by nudge/observation id. A gap-less message
has no slot in the decision shape: **that is 6.5's real work, not the verb.**

**What already exists in its favour:** `listManagersWithTelegram(tenantId)` + `notifyManagers`
(`src/calls/notify.ts:11`) already address managers as a ROLE and fan to Telegram;
`office_messages.nudge_id` is nullable with NO FK, so the bucket already accepts a gap-less message
(built that way in 5.5); and two delivery paths already exist — verbatim to a person's chat (the
nudge runner) and to their assistant (the office runner). The verb picks between them.

**Shape:** `send(to: <person | role>, as: <the person | their assistant>, message, about?: <gap>,
counts_as_the_ask?: <bool>)` — absorbing `send`, `escalate`, `ask_agent`, `inform_agent`.
`counts_as_the_ask` is not a delivery detail: today `send` marks the gap asked and enables
`answered_by_capture_id` attribution, while the 5.5 ruling says office-channel answers NEVER fill
the record. One verb must let the planner say which, which turns a property of the word into a
judgment.

**What is knowingly given up, and must be answered by 6.5's design:**
- **Durability.** A review item persists until a human settles it; a message decays. The user's
  answer is re-ask — the planner asks again — which makes the PLANNER the durability mechanism.
- **Observability.** `resolve`/`dismiss` is a measurable human response (it is what
  `mv_escalation_fates` counts). A message has no resolution state, and the safety budget moved
  from rails to evals.
- Seat-exempt people are unreachable either way (no chat, no assistant) — not a regression, but it
  means "nobody to ask" becomes "message a manager about it", never "file it".

**`review_items` empties itself** (user ruling 2026-09-01: *"realistically, it should contain the
confirmation sessions"*). Its 2 gap kinds stop being written when escalate dies (build 6); its 4
dead-end kinds and 2 reminders leave in build 7 when detectors stop minting; the 15 staged tokens
+ `pairing_verification` are the real job and stay. **Consequence to plan for:** once only staged
actions remain, `resolve_review` can no longer resolve anything (the `STAGED_ACTION_KINDS` guard
refuses it — staged actions confirm through their own tool), so it and `list_pending_reviews`
become "show me what is staged" / "cancel it" and should be renamed with the table. Cleanup after
build 7, not before.

**TWO RULINGS ADDED 2026-09-02 while planning 6.5 (user).**

*(a) The planner must actively know the firm's roster.* — *"If the planner is the central
intelligence of the firm, then it should know the roster of the team actively."* What exists today
is a per-gap `person: <display name>` line (`buildGapView`, `planner.ts`), so the planner knows the
people who currently have problems and nobody else, and knows them by a name it cannot address.
`v_team` has always been in its `spine_read` catalog (`server.ts:772`) — never a gate, only an
absence, because until 6.5 every verb's destination was decided by the record and a name was
unusable. 6.5 pushes a THE TEAM block and prints the REF beside the name on every gap,
for the reason the same function already prints a job id beside the customer name (`planner.ts:
125-128`: *"it had the words but not the key"*).

*(b) A gap whose person has LEFT must say so, and the planner adjusts.* — *"Especially if it was
working on a gap which belonged to someone who used to belong to the roster but not anymore. Then
the planner should actively adjust."* **Probed 2026-09-02, do not re-derive:** offboarding a seat
does NOTHING to that person's open gaps (no cancel, no reassign, no flag anywhere), and
`technicianName` is a SNAPSHOT stamped into `context` at detection time (`watchers.ts:609`,
`:675`), never refreshed. Consequences today: a departed person's gap shows their name with no
hint; a `queued` one freezes silently at the `isInactiveState` skip (`nudgeQueue.ts:131-133`); an
`asked` one waits forever and ages into `asksUnattended`. The planner cannot fix any of it because
the only person it can reach is the one on the gap. 6.5 makes it fixable (ask somebody else about
the same gap), so the departure becomes visible in the same build — the gap's person line reports
CURRENT standing resolved live against the roster by `technician_id`, never the snapshot.
**NOT by auto-cancelling on offboard:** build 5 removed the sweep's authority to end gaps, and a
trigger here would re-make that mistake. Tell the planner; let it judge.

*(c) A FULL ROSTER TRAIL, not just a departure date.* — *"The planner should probably have a
record of the firm's roster (with history) … so it doesn't have to go looking for record that
proves a person was offboarded."* User chose the trail over a single `state_changed_at` column.
New `roster_events` table: joined / left / came back, when, and who did it. **Probed 2026-09-02:**
every state change already funnels through ONE function (`setTechnicianState`,
`technicians.ts:321` — the only other write of `state` is the insert at `:169`), which is what
makes an app-side write safe (a DB trigger cannot see WHO made the change, and the actor is the
half a bare timestamp cannot give). `technicians.updated_at` is bumped by ANY edit and can never
answer "when did they leave" — so **the trail cannot be backfilled**: it starts empty, and a
departure predating it renders `date unknown`, never a guess. The roster block shows departures
only for people the tick's own gaps name; `v_roster_events` serves the rest.

Also confirmed by the user in the same exchange: NOT auto-cancelling a departed person's gaps is
correct — *"the planner should be flexible enough to handle this type of situation."*

**BUILT 2026-09-02, five phases.** Verbs 7 -> 5: `send`, `hold`, `close`, `dismiss`, `recover`.
`send(to?, as, message, about?, countsAsTheAsk?)` reaches anyone on the roster by either leg,
about a gap or about nothing.

- **1 · the firm's roster.** `roster_events` + a DB trigger; a THE TEAM block; every gap's person
  resolved live, so a departed person's gap says so.
- **2 · who you can reach.** `to` on the message verbs, resolved against the roster the planner
  was SHOWN (never a fresh read) and canonicalised before any write.
- **3 · a message need not be about a gap.** `nudge_decisions_subject_check` DROPPED — both ids
  null is a legal row meaning "a message to a person that no gap raised".
- **4 · one verb.** The collapse, plus the chat leg: `via='chat'` office rows ride the EXISTING
  nudge pull, so the whole leg is spine-side (`Box_Setup_Stuff` untouched — proven by replaying
  the runner's own jq extraction against a real two-item pull).
- **5 · seeing what you sent.** A WHAT YOU SENT block, because "ask again" is a message now and
  the gap's own row records only the FIRST ask.

**Three invariants worth not re-deriving.** A gap-less decision writes NO `job_id`
(`listDecisionsForJob` keys on it alone, and `recall_decisions` maps `gapId: nudge_id ??
observation_id` — a job-stamped gap-less row would reach the model with a null gap id). A gap-less
message may name NO DATES (the allowed-date list is computed per gap). And the outbound block
NEVER calls a chat message unanswered: `in_reply_to` comes only from an assistant replying, while
a person who gets a text answers their own assistant, which files a field note and no office row —
so absence means "nobody used this channel", not "nobody answered".

**Deliberately left, each with a reason:**
- **`as: 'board'`** — the spine-side manager push, the only leg reaching a SEATLESS manager. One-way,
  unrecorded, and its token was found unset on the pilot cron in Aug 2026. `as:` is the extension
  point, so it costs nothing to add later.
- **Several addressees on one message** — build 8 ("a crew has no lead"). `to` stays singular, and
  `office_messages.message_group_id` was added UNUSED so the identity exists when build 8 needs it
  (free now; a backfill over live rows later).
- **The quiet-firm skip** — a firm with no open gaps AND no unread messages is still skipped before
  planning, so the planner cannot originate a message at a silent firm. Un-skipping costs a model
  call per cadence tick per firm forever.
- **No `v_office_messages`** — `SPINE_VIEW_CATALOG` is shared with `query_spine`, so publishing it
  would hand every manager the planner's outbound bodies.
- **`B65-REACH-A-MANAGER`** — written and removed before shipping. `seedScenario` creates exactly
  ONE person, `seatExempt: true` ("deliberately UNREACHABLE"), and `ScenarioWorld` has no field for
  a second — so the scenario would have passed by grading the pre-6.5 behaviour. **The evaluation
  phase needs a world with a second, REACHABLE person before "reach somebody the gap does not
  name" can be measured at all.**

**A latent hole found and fixed on the way:** every grader filtered decisions to a gap key, so a
gap-less decision — the one class 6.5 added — was invisible to all of them. `ScenarioExpect`
gained `gapLessAction`.

**Deploy order (still NOT deployed, joins the 4-7 evaluation train):** migrations 20260903110000,
20260903120000, 20260903130000 BEFORE code. No box rollout — the chat leg needed no runner change.

Plan: `thoughts/shared/plans/2026-09-02-one-send-verb.md` (5 phases, complete).

Research: `thoughts/shared/research/2026-09-03-the-schedule-is-the-managers.md`.

Confirmations clause 5; the eval world's two solver tenants. **~8,500 lines net.**

**THE ONE LINE IN `bootstrap-box.sh` THAT MUST NOT BE DELETED WITH THE REST.** The §0h block was

append. Only the APPEND is gone. The delete half stays, because a box wired before rung 8 still

**Two things the code said that the research did not.** `fit.ts` carried `locked` as a displacement
