### 8. A visit is not a person — the multi-technician visit (USER RULING 2026-09-01) → SUPERSEDED BY THE SCHEDULING REMODEL (2026-09-03)

**READ THIS FIRST.** The rung below was researched (`thoughts/shared/research/2026-09-02-a-visit-is-not-a-person.md`)
and planned (`thoughts/shared/plans/2026-09-03-a-visit-is-not-a-person.md`, three phases, no
migration) — and then the user reframed the problem as a modeling failure rather than a bug: *"we
designed the scheduling features badly. We did not consider multiple technicians assigned to the
same job… we're really just trying to have the users fit to our reality of scheduling."* The plan is
SUPERSEDED. What replaces it is rungs 8–11 in the ladder above; the research stands and most of it is
rung 9's research.

**Two corrections to the text below, found by that research — ⚠ BOTH SUPERSEDED BY RUNG 9b
(2026-09-05), which is precisely the thing that fixed them. Do not carry them forward; they are
kept only so the reasoning that led to 9b stays legible:**
1. ~~`visits` is NOT dormant. `recordFieldNote:267` calls `createVisit` on every matched field note…
   What is true is narrower: `visits` is not the SCHEDULE. **The word "visit" names two unlinked
   tables** — `assignments` (the schedule) and `visits` (written per NOTE, with a `scheduled_at`
   column that nothing sets).~~ **There is now ONE table.** `assignments` was renamed to `visits`,
   the per-note table was dropped, and a note LINKS its trip through `visit_notes`.
2. ~~Reschedule is cancel + insert with a NEW id (`rescheduleAssignment`), so an assignment id can
   never name a visit across a move.~~ **A move is an UPDATE in place and the id survives it** —
   which is exactly the id this section's rung-8 text says does not exist.

**AMENDED 2026-09-05 (user) — the WINDOW dies too; no schema rail restricts the agent.** *"We're
getting rid of the window also. I want the manager agent to simply be an assistant that has access
to the schedule, who will initiate an edit of the schedule only when asked. The promise level of a
job shouldn't restrict the agent, nor should the window concept, especially because the lifecycle of
a job can be shortened and extended depending on how the humans act."*

Deleted by this ruling: `promise_level`, `promised_day`, `set_promise`,
`promised_day_when_promised`, and the single behaviour they powered — `promiseBroken` →
`requiresConfirmation` on `move_visit` (`src/schedule/booking.ts:722`); **and** the stored window
`assignments.earliest_date` / `latest_date` together with `assignment_placed_or_windowed`. **A visit
has a time or it does not**; unplaced means no time, and the manager's unscheduled list orders by
AGE (user choice, offered against a soft `target_date` sort key and a strip-enforcement-only
option). Nothing stored can go stale because nothing is stored.

**KEEP, despite the shared name:** `find_slots(earliestDate, latestDate)` is a SEARCH RANGE ("look
between these dates, default today+13"), not a commitment — it survives untouched. Deleting it
alongside the stored window is the obvious mistake here.

**What replaces the rails is DOCTRINE, and it is real scope for rung 9** — not just deletions. The
agent must look for promises **in the record** before it edits the schedule or makes a suggestion:
the calls, emails and notes in L1 (`v_captures`, bridged to the job via
`v_visits.source_capture_id` — since rung 9b, `v_visit_notes`). That is build 6's "cite what you looked up" applied to scheduling.
The agent is **reactive** — it edits only when asked and never initiates; the PLANNER recommending
is rung 11, a different thing.

**"Sometime next week" is a CONVERSATION, not a data structure (user, 2026-09-05).** Asked what
replaces `book_visit`'s `timeTbd` + date range, the user chose *book with no time at all*: *"If a
manager wants to book something sometime next week, they can ask the seat agent, who might ask the
planner for suggestions also because the planner has the firm's full workflow memory/context. This
way, the planner and seat agent are adaptive to different firms. And scheduling doesn't have to run
with our imaginations… we want to encourage (or perhaps even train our users through the seat
agents) behaviors that utilize AI."* The precedent the user named is the planner's gap-chasing,
which already trains users this way. Note that the seat agent asking the planner is ALREADY BUILT —
build 5.5/6.5's office channel (`office_messages`, direction `to_planner`, the
`ask_agent`/`inform_agent` verbs) — so this leans on existing plumbing.

**Confirmations re-gated, and a correction worth keeping (user, 2026-09-05).** The claim that the
promise ladder powered ONE live behaviour was WRONG, and plan review caught it:
`bookingRequiresConfirmation` (`src/schedule/booking.ts:217`) gates the two-step on `book_visit` AND
`unschedule_visit`, while `promiseBroken` gates only `move_visit`. Deleting the ladder would have
silently removed two confirmation gates and made the `pending_booking` / `pending_visit_cancel`
staged flows unreachable. Ruling: **book and move go one-shot; `unschedule_visit` keeps its two-step,
re-gated on the visit being PLACED** — a customer is expecting a placed visit, which is the
externally-visible test the confirmation doctrine already uses. `pending_booking` is deleted;
`pending_visit_cancel` survives.


**Why the ladder existed at all, found while researching this** (`src/schedule/booking.ts:570`):
`promised_day` was only ever a SHADOW COPY of the window, taken at the instant placement destroyed
it — because `assignment_placed_or_windowed` forbids a row being placed AND windowed at once. The
ladder was a workaround for that CHECK. Removing the CHECK removes the reason the ladder existed.

**The migration is unblocked** (user, 2026-09-05): *"I am not afraid of a real data migration
because we are operating in a test suite and nothing is in production right now."* Destructive is
permitted, consistent with the L1 waiver recorded above.

### 🟢 DEPLOYED 2026-09-08 — rung 10b, "the new caller" (live checks still owed)

Branch `feat/rung-10b-new-caller` (6 commits). Plan:
`thoughts/shared/plans/2026-09-08-rung-10b-the-new-caller.md`. What it changes, in one line each:

1. **The matcher learned two keys**: the sender's email address and the Gmail THREAD, in the order
   phone → email → thread → address, each unique-or-refuse. The thread key also names the JOB when
   the thread's captures point at exactly one. This is what would have caught the 2026-09-08 live
   specimen — a known customer's reschedule reply, parked as a stranger because it carried neither
   their phone nor their address.
2. **An unmatched caller is an ITEM, not a dead end.** `inbound_unmatched` (a third `PINGED_KINDS`
   entry — the list was written kind-blind and this proved it) replaces the `unlinked_call` review
   and its push, which no tool could ever act on. The kind and its existing rows are KEPT; only the
   writer is deleted, the `flagNudgeGap` precedent. The six open pilot rows stay for hand
   resolution.
3. **The planner may send a PAIR** (user, 2026-09-08): the text that reaches a manager's phone plus
   a quiet `as:"assistant"` note to that manager's assistant carrying the item id, so the answer
   comes back stapled to the right call. Rung 10's refusal of an assistant-leg send naming an
   inbound item is replaced by a marker rule — only the chat leg stamps `asked_at` — with the note
   bounded twice: it is dropped unless a chat send to the SAME manager about the SAME item was
   accepted in the same plan, and it is refused at apply unless that text's guarded stamp actually
   LANDED. Both bounds exist because the text is rate-limited to one a day and the note was not.
4. **`record_caller` — the planner's first write to the customer record.** It re-runs
   `recordFieldNote` for the capture the item names, so every row cites the call. **The identity's
   header rule "nothing irreversible or externally visible may dispatch for it" is RETIRED here**
   (see the team-lead surface entry above). Idempotent by reading `listL2CitingCapture` first, and
   by a DB index: `uq_customers_one_per_capture`. Customers only — `bookVisit` rolls a job shell
   back by flipping it to cancelled and the stamp STAYS, so the same index on jobs would refuse
   that legitimate retry, and the pilot already carries such a pair.
5. **The extractor reads the conversation**, fetched from Gmail through Composio (8s deadline,
   billed either way, skipped for a starter / an unpaired mailbox / a redelivery the door will
   no-op on) with our own captures as the fallback. Identity may come from earlier messages;
   `jobTitle`, `statusSignal`, `visitCommitted`, `touchesOperations`, `followUp` and `attributes`
   describe the LATEST message alone — and identity may never come from the firm's own signature
   block, because those fields feed the deterministic matcher.

**DEPLOYED 2026-09-08 20:02Z.** `Calls-worker` auto-deployed `b71cc10` at 19:59:54; the web service
and `lifecycle-heart` were triggered BY HAND and reported live at ~20:02 and 20:01:41.
`GET /health` on the web service answers `b71cc10`, which is the only check that reads the live
system rather than a written record.
- **The §4 estate table is WRONG about `lifecycle-heart`:** it says auto-deploy *yes*; the live API
  says **no**. Read the API, not the table. Auto-deploy is ON for `Calls-worker` and `Calls-purge`
  only; the web service, `lifecycle-heart` and `Board_Worker` are all hand-deployed.
- **`Board_Worker` was deliberately left BEHIND** — rung 10b does not touch the board, and the
  service is to be **retired entirely once the rungs are done** (user, 2026-09-08). Do not "fix"
  its staleness.

**The two migrations were applied 2026-09-08 20:05Z, and a `db push` trap came with them.** They
had NOT reached the pilot when the deploy went out — no indexes, and no rows in
`supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` either, so the earlier apply never touched this database at
all (a recorded-but-failed apply would have left the rows). Applied via the Supabase MCP's
`apply_migration`, which records its OWN timestamp rather than the file's, so the ledger and the
filenames now disagree:

| repo file | ledger row |
|---|---|
| `20260908110000_thread_id_index.sql` | `20260908200505 thread_id_index` |
| `20260908120000_one_customer_per_capture.sql` | `20260908200514 one_customer_per_capture` |

This is the `ledger versions ≠ filenames` trap in the deploy entry above, and the consequence is
narrow: `supabase migration list` shows both file versions as pending FOREVER, and a `db push`
(never do one) would try to replay them. Both are `create index if not exists`, so a replay is a
no-op — the drift is a reporting lie, not a hazard. Both indexes verified
`indisvalid` + `indisready` true after the apply, with zero duplicate customer citations
immediately before (the unique index aborts if any exist).

**Neither index is required for CORRECTNESS**, which is why the deploy was safe to ship ahead of
them: without the thread index the lookup scans 275 rows instead of seeking, and without the unique
index `record_caller`'s twin guard is the read alone, unlocked against a race. Both matter at real
volume; neither changes an answer.

**Deferred and NOT done**: every live check (all need the pilot deployed), the Phase 3 manual read
of the manager's text, and the seat wire pass that puts rule 7's relay line on the boxes (user-
deferred; `Box_Setup_Stuff/bootstrap-box.sh` and `docs/nemoclaw-tech-agent.md` carry it in the
repo). Also open: no behavioural eval pins the "latest message alone" guard, which is prompt-only.

### 🟡 BUILT (not deployed) 2026-09-09 — rung 10b.2, "the citing door on the seat"

Branch `feat/rung-10b2-citing-door-on-the-seat`. Plan
`thoughts/shared/plans/2026-09-08-the-citing-door-on-the-seat.md`, research
`thoughts/shared/research/2026-09-09-who-may-write-l2.md`.

**Phase 1 (`dcd7fce`) — the seat can cite the caller's own message.** `record_caller` is SHARED
with active manager badges via an explicit `SEAT_SHARED_TEAM_LEAD_TOOLS` exception list;
`TEAM_LEAD_ONLY_TOOL_NAMES` still derives from the array MINUS that list, so the ceiling stays
deny-by-default and a new team-lead tool is gated the day it is added. Technicians and inactive
badges are refused by a tier gate reusing the same `activeManager` predicate as the 26 admin
tools; the `spine:` path is unchanged. `MANAGER_TOOLS` pulls the schema OUT of `TEAM_LEAD_TOOLS`
(one schema, two catalogs) so wording cannot drift between readers. Writes name the acting badge
(`decided.actorRef`, defaulted to `TEAM_LEAD_REF`) instead of always the planner.

**Phase 2 — an omission stops counting as an answer.** `book_visit` requires `untimed: true`;
omitting date/startTime/endTime is REFUSED, and passing both is refused as a contradiction. And
`refuseNameAsAddress` rejects an address equal to the customer's own name, normalized past case,
padding, internal whitespace and surrounding punctuation — on BOTH minting doors.

**Three review findings worth keeping, because each was a fix that was wrong the first time:**
- **The own-mailbox guard's first version made things worse.** Testing every address on the `to`
  line turned "is the firm mailing itself" into "is the sender among the recipients", so a
  customer who kept herself on `To` (a contact form's copy-to-sender, a reply-all) would have lost
  her identity key and opened a second unmatched item. It is now conservative: yes only via the
  roster's `connected_email` (authoritative) or a `to` header with exactly ONE address. Residual,
  documented in the code: a customer who mails themselves and BCCs the firm. **Arm one is DEAD in
  the pilot** — see rung 13.
- **The address guard was added to the wrong door.** It lived in `resolutionFrom`
  (`record_field_note`), which rung 10b.2 had just told the seat to stop using — so it guarded the
  door nobody takes and was missing from `record_caller`, where the 2026-09-08 specimen would have
  reproduced verbatim. Now a shared `refuseNameAsAddress` on both.
- **An eval pin that compared a literal to itself.** `SEAT_SHARED_TEAM_LEAD_TOOL_NAMES` was
  hand-written next to its own assertion; it now scrapes `server.ts`, and the drift was verified
  by widening the real Set and watching it fail.

**Doctrine moved with the code** (the Box_Setup_Stuff sync rule): rule 7 now BRANCHES on what the
seat can see — hold `record_caller` → record it yourself and pass `openJob`/`bookVisit` in the
SAME call (`open_job`/`book_visit` cannot cite an unmatched-caller capture: `inboundAskFor` matches
on `context.customerId`, which that kind never has); do not hold it → relay with
`inform_team_lead`, the technician's fallback, which an earlier draft had deleted. Rule 5 and rule
7 both carry the new `untimed` contract.

**⚠ DEPLOY IS NOT JUST A RENDER PUSH.** The manager `tools/list` moves 42 → 43, gateways cache it
per process, and rule 5/7 wording is baked at `wire` time. So: deploy, then
`nemoclaw <seat> gateway restart` on every manager sandbox, then a box `wire` re-run — BEFORE
Phase 3's live tests. Skip it and the seat serves a cached 42-tool list with the old doctrine and
the 2026-09-08 specimen reproduces, reading as a code failure.

**Not deployed. Phase 3 (pilot cleanup + the chained e2e) is not run.**

### 🔴 LIVE E2E 2026-09-08 — rung 10b's ask PASSED, the answer FAILED, and the doctrine it produced (§10b-live)

Two live tests against the deployed pilot, both read back through `supabase-test` and the cost
ledger. Full trace in the session; the facts that matter:

**Test 1 — a known customer replies (thread key).** Reply on the water-heater thread, 22:06:11Z →
filed as a `visit_notes` link on Tom Becker's confirmed visit `06b4d9b0`, job `fe8f98b1`. Zero
observations, zero messages. Two things went right: it did not park, and the quoted history
containing OUR OWN "Like at around 2 pm or so" did NOT set `visitCommitted` (the "earlier messages
include the firm's own replies" rule held). Two things unproven: the quoted history also carried
`Tom Becker 555-0199`, and phone is tried before thread, so the phone key almost certainly won —
the thread key is NOT proved live. (It CAN work here: the original capture's `external_id`
`1a07e2f8962a4f06` equals the Gmail thread id — a thread's first message id IS the thread id — and
`findCustomersByThread` reads `external_id` as well as `metadata->>threadId` for exactly this.)

**Test 2 — a stranger emails.** "Ruth Bramley", no phone, no address, 22:04:07Z → `inbound_unmatched`
`a25b4906` at +6s, `pinged_at` +9s, `asked_at` +22s, chat delivered to manager-joy's phone at +67s,
quiet note to her assistant, **zero review items**. Extraction correct, `visitCommitted:false`, the
refusal names every key tried. The planner's tick: 3 model calls, 13,568 of 13,596 tokens cached.
**The first half of 10b works.**

**Then the answer came back through the wrong door.** Joy's assistant used `record_field_note`
(×3), not `record_caller` — because at the time `record_caller` was refused to every non-`spine:`
badge by `TEAM_LEAD_ONLY_TOOL_NAMES` and was not in `MANAGER_TOOLS`. (Rung 10b.2 changes exactly
that; this paragraph describes the state ON 2026-09-08, not today's code.) The query
audit shows it READ the right things first — Ruth's capture `3d18b1cd` by id, its own quiet note
with the item id, the visit it had just made — and had no tool that accepted a capture id. What
it wrote: capture `79776a0e` (her email RE-TYPED as a `technician_note`), customer `291921e1` with
**`address_line1 = "Ruth Bramley"`** (the confirm step DEMANDS an address to mint, and there was
none), job `212ccc4d`, visit `e83a233a` **completed, untimed, participant = Joy** (a
`technician_note` on a job with no live trip mints a receipt trip with the filer on it —
`attachNote.ts:116`), then 22 minutes later `book_visit` → visit `134a0332` **untimed and
unassigned** although the reply itself named Thursday 2026-09-10 (omitting the date IS the
untimed encoding; the user had typed "Book it unassigned" after the bot offered
"unassigned/untimed" as a pair). Nothing cites `3d18b1cd`; the item is still open and will be
re-asked. The manager later placed `134a0332` at Thu 10:00–11:00 by chat, and that flow (ask for
duration, `move_visit`, `set_job_duration`=60) worked correctly.

**Diagnosis, in one line:** nothing tied the answer to the question. The item's closing test is
"does anything cite this capture" (`mootFactFor`, author-blind, record-derived — deliberately, and
KEPT that way: had "I handled it" closed the item, the failure would have been invisible). Every
seat write was an assertion the system recorded without checking. The model READ the tool prose
(four buried `book_visit` instructions followed: `resolve_date` first, ask for duration, day
phrase verbatim, board link) and lost to the SCHEMA every time prose and schema disagreed. Fixes
must be structural. Tool prose totals 7,169 words across 50 tools; `book_visit` alone is 451.

**Other findings from the same run:**
- **The Composio thread fetch has never fired in the pilot.** The trigger delivers as
  `manager-sam` (every drop today attributes there); the only roster row with
  `composio_connected_account_id` is `tech-jane` (offboarded, `connected_email`
  hongyi23360228@gmail.com — a different mailbox). `tech?.composio_connected_account_id` is null
  → `fetched = null` → thread context always falls back to our own L1 view. No `composio` cost row
  since 2026-09-07 22:55Z. The Gmail leg, its 8s deadline and the redelivery pre-check are
  UNPROVEN live. Roster/Composio mismatch, not a 10b defect.
- **A known customer's QUESTION rings no bell.** `PINGED_KINDS` = new job / commitment /
  unmatched. "Like at 2pm or so?" on an existing job is none, so it sits in a visit note with
  nobody on the hook, and the planner (which wakes only on those kinds or the :00/:30 cadence)
  never specifically saw it. A fourth kind is a product decision, not yet taken.
- **The sender was the firm's OWN mailbox** (user sent from hhe23360228@gmail.com to itself) and
  the extractor recorded it as `customerEmail`. Nothing checks sender ≠ the watched `to` mailbox.
  If filed onto a customer, every future self-sent mail matches them by the email key.
- **The seat cannot ping the planner.** `inform_team_lead` is a bucket write read at the next
  tick (measured: 22:21 → `read` at 22:31). Only the three pinged kinds wake it early. That is
  rung 10c.

**THE DOCTRINE THAT CAME OUT (user, 2026-09-09) — see the 10d ladder row.** Technicians are
ground truth INTO L1; L2 is the manager's operating book; the planner is the assistant who
investigates before anything lands on L2. `recordFieldNote` performs SIX L2 writes from a
technician note — `createCustomer` (392), `createJob` (569), `createVisit` via `attachNote`
(116), `recordVisitReport` (709), `updateVisitStatus→completed` (742), `updateJobStatus` (781).
**Five move to the planner (10d). One is DELETED: `labor_hours`** — "the manager can technically
get the hours worked from the schedule already"; the column ACCUMULATES (`current + added`, two
notes restating "two hours" = four), has no check against the scheduled window, is called cost by
its own comment, and nothing downstream reads it yet. The door keeps: L1 append, deterministic
identity, and the LINKS (`job_captures`, `visit_notes`) — a link is how the planner finds the
evidence, not an assertion about the world. Creation-vs-reporting is the line, drawn by WHO may
assert, not by channel or tier (Joy is on a crew row; tier is the wrong lever). The technician
catalog (`TOOLS`, 17) has exactly ONE creator — `record_field_note`; every other creator
(`open_job`, `book_visit`, `add_technician_seat`, `record_money_event`, `add_time_off`,
`record_caller`) is manager- or planner-only. So the principle is already enforced everywhere
but one tool.

**Why the planner, not a manager, answers "did that trip happen?"** A manager wasn't there and can
only rubber-stamp — an ask a human can only say yes to devalues every other ask. The planner has
the scheduled window, the job, prior trips, and channels to ask the technician back. The
deterministic happened-flip guard (exists because an inbound "nobody showed up" also arrives after
the visit's start) is replaced by the planner's evidence discipline; that discipline becomes
load-bearing and is what 10d's eval must prove.

**Cleanup owed on the pilot before the re-run** (per-path deletes, never a truncate): visits
`e83a233a` + `134a0332` and participants, job `212ccc4d`, customer `291921e1`; retire `a25b4906`
as dismissed with a reason. The re-typed L1 capture `79776a0e` STAYS (append-only).

### ✅ DEPLOYED 2026-09-07 — rung 10, "inbound asks before it creates"

**Proven end to end on the pilot, 23:24–00:00 UTC, first clean run.** A real customer email
reached a manager's phone in 76 seconds and a booked visit cited it:

```
23:24:44  email captured in L1 (the FIRST real inbound email this system has ever taken —
          the only prior one, 2026-07-04, was a `connector:gmail` fixture)
23:24:46  inbound_commitment observation written — right customer, right job, right sender
23:24:49  ping loop CLAIMS it                                   +2.9s (promise was ~10s)
23:24:58  the DATE RAIL rejects the planner's first draft: 'question used a date it was
          not given: "Friday"' — the model rewrote it to convey the same thing without
          naming a day it could not ground
23:24:59  rewritten message queued to a manager
23:26:00  delivered to Joy's Telegram
   [Joy replies "Book it"; her agent asks duration + technician, then books]
23:42:06  visit 06b4d9b0 confirmed Fri 09-11 08:00-09:00, crew Hman,
          source_capture_id = the customer's email          ← amendment B, live
00:00:43  the planner CLOSES its own observation, unprompted:
          {"action":"close","basis":"record","mootClose":true,"confidence":"high",
           "citation":"a visit was created from this capture — it cites this call"}
```

**What the deploy cost, recorded because both cost real time.** (1) PR #210 squash-merged
BEFORE the gate rewrite and the `touchesOperations` reframe were pushed, so `main` briefly
carried the version that woke the planner for a thank-you after a paid invoice; #211 fixed it.
Check content, not ancestry, after a squash — `git merge-base --is-ancestor` says "not in main"
for every pre-squash commit and tells you nothing. (2) The first email died at the last hop on
`teamLeadPlanner: inference 401` — `Calls-worker` had a credential the endpoint rejected while
the web service's worked, proven by the extractor succeeding on the same email. A NEW HOST for
an existing code path needs its env checked; the ping loop's own guard passes on a key that is
merely PRESENT.

**Residue.** The first email's observation is stamped `pinged` and never `asked` (401 collateral)
— nothing retries a claimed row, by design. The `inbound_new_job` arm and the no-ask filter have
no live coverage: `Default Tenant` has no customer whose jobs are all terminal.

**The ruling that shaped it** (user, 2026-09-06 and 2026-09-07): *"Before creating the visit,
someone gets asked"*, then *"for new jobs, we also want the planner to ask the manager, so the
manager can know instead of being blind to the job being created."* This OVERTURNS "create, don't
stage" (see the §10 remodel paragraph, now marked superseded).

- **For a customer we know, inbound creates NOTHING.** Open job on file → the capture is pinned to
  it and what they said is kept on it. No open job, or the firm agreed to come out with no trip to
  carry it → an OBSERVATION (`inbound_new_job` / `inbound_commitment`), and a manager is asked.
- **The extractor gained one field, `visitCommitted`** — did the BUSINESS agree to send someone
  out. Never true because a caller asked. Zero extra model calls; it rides the existing extraction.
- **A. `job_captures`** — the twin of `visit_notes` one rung down. The link-less shape is closed: an
  inbound capture on a job with no live visit used to be unstamped and attached to nothing, so both
  evidence homes missed it and the job read silent. (`job_notes` was taken — that is the planner's
  notepad.) It also makes a redelivery a real duplicate instead of a repeat extractor call.
- **B. What a yes creates CITES the call.** `book_visit` and the new `open_job` resolve the
  customer's ONE open ask and stamp `source_capture_id`; two open asks cite nothing, because a
  wrong citation would let the planner close the other call.
- **C. `attributes.correspondence[]`** on the job — one entry per inbound capture, appended under
  CAS, never `laborHours`/`workPerformed` (the report and the bill stay technician-only). Folded on
  retraction everywhere a typed reader looks, named in the retraction preview, reserved against
  `set_fact`, bounded to the last three in the brief.
- **`open_job`** (manager tier). Until rung 10 there was NO way to open a job without also putting a
  visit on the calendar — `book_visit`'s customerId path minted a shell as a side effect. A caller
  who wants a quote needs the job on file and nobody sent out.
- **The focused pass.** A second loop in the **Calls-worker** (the only always-on process: the web
  service spins down, the heartbeat is a :00/:30 cron) claims an unpinged observation within ~10s
  and runs `runPlanner` scoped to that one item, no `planner_runs` marker, 180s budget. The
  heartbeat is the fallback — an unclaimed observation makes an off-cadence firm due (35-min window).
- **Two markers, `pinged_at` and `asked_at`.** Pinged = the planner was CALLED about it; asked = a
  text went out, stamped by the send under a guard so the focused pass and the heartbeat can never
  both text about one call. **The ask guard is a 24-HOUR WINDOW, not once-ever** — the plan
  contradicted itself here, and an absolute guard meant a manager who ignored the one text left the
  job never opened. It writes no `not_before`: a stored hold would print a raw ISO date into the gap
  block that the date rail then refuses to let the planner use, killing the re-ask.

**Traps and findings worth carrying:**
- **A "processed" marker must be the LAST thing written.** `captureHasLinkedL2` reads every link, so
  writing one before the ask would let any failure in between bury a promise permanently — the
  redelivery short-circuits as a duplicate. The write path's tail is ordered by what it costs to
  lose: ask → correspondence → link → status signal. The ask is itself a marker, so that ordering is
  a deliberate trade (a failed correspondence append costs that job its own copy of the words),
  recorded in the code and pinned by a test.
- **Nothing releases a `pinged_at`.** The ping loop therefore checks whether a pass can run AT ALL —
  mode, credentials, the spine-model rail — BEFORE it claims anything. A worker deployed before its
  env landed would otherwise mark every inbound call "the planner was called about this" while
  calling nobody, permanently, and setting the variable later would not recover them.
- **The two doors spell the sender differently.** The email webhook writes `from`, the call worker
  writes `fromNumber`. Reading only `from` left every CALL — the rung's headline channel — with no
  sender to show the planner.
- **`captureHasObservation` needed its own index.** It reads by `subject_ref` alone, which
  `uq_observations_subject_ever` cannot serve (tenant leads it, and its `author='detector'`
  predicate is not implied): a Seq Scan per inbound delivery on a table with no delete path.
  `idx_observations_subject_ref` fixes it; `EXPLAIN` confirms.
- **A gap-less `send` never reaches the ask marker.** In a focused pass the FIRST one is bound to
  the focus observation — only the first, because gap-less sends are deliberately not deduped so the
  planner can tell several managers one thing, and binding all of them would refuse every manager
  after the first. On the heartbeat it is not bound (a tick shows many items); the doctrine covers it.
- **The date rail reaches further than it looks.** The ask-marker line originally read "you texted a
  manager 2 hours ago" — a temporal phrase `TEMPORAL_RE` matches and `dateLabels` does not contain
  for these kinds, so a planner echoing it would have had its whole message dropped.
- **`ingestEmail` and `googleapis` are deleted.** The direct Gmail puller had no production caller;
  email arrives by Composio webhook, push not poll.
- **⚠ THE PHONE DOOR ONLY REACHES THIS RUNG ON A `new_job` INTENT.** `calls/worker.ts` passes
  `structure: false` for every other label, so the extractor never runs and no observation is
  raised — which makes `inbound_commitment` on EXISTING work nearly unreachable by phone, even
  though "we'll come out Tuesday to finish it" is the archetypal committed call. Email has no such
  gate. Widening which intents structure was deliberately out of scope (the 2026-08-06 router plan
  keeps the extractor off cancel/complaint calls); it is the obvious next thing to settle.
- **`findFoldImpact`'s superset invariant is now load-bearing in a second place.** It walked
  `visit_notes` because attachNote's step 3 was the ONLY writer of `visits.source_capture_id` and
  linked the same visit in the same call. `book_visit` cites a call now, and such a visit has no
  note link — so the preview silently stopped naming a real appointment the fold then dropped from
  every briefing. The scan reads the column directly as well. Any future writer of that column
  inherits the same trap.
- **THE EMAIL CHANNEL DROPS ITS THREAD ID — verified 2026-09-07, and it is time-sensitive.**
  Composio's `GMAIL_NEW_GMAIL_MESSAGE` payload carries `thread_id` (12 fields; `gmail.ts` keeps
  five). `GMAIL_FETCH_MESSAGE_BY_THREAD_ID` returns the whole thread in ~0.7 s under our own
  pairing — proven read-only on the pilot mailbox — and reads are already skipped by the L1 log
  sweep. The outbound side stores `metadata.threadId` on every assistant-sent email; only inbound
  is blind, and L1 is append-only, so every email landing today is permanently un-chainable.
  Replies a person sends from the Gmail app NEVER arrive (the trigger watches INBOX only).
  Record: `thoughts/shared/research/2026-09-07-email-thread-context-from-composio.md`.
  **CAPTURED since 2026-09-07 (PR #213, deployed `262a27a`), VERIFIED LIVE 2026-09-08:** inbound
  captures carry `threadId`, `to` and `labelIds` in metadata, present-only, under the outbound
  capture's key names (`gmail.ts` → `webhook.ts`). The first live reply landed 80 s after it was
  sent with `threadId` equal to the original email's `external_id` — and was then PARKED UNMATCHED
  (0 observations, 0 links) although it was the same sender, the same thread, a job with a booked
  visit, and the customer's exact phone string sat in the quoted history. The customer has no
  email on file, so email matching would not have saved it either: the thread id is the only key
  that links that reply to its customer. Nothing reads the new keys yet; the 10b plan makes thread
  membership an identity key beside email (a reply in a matched thread is not a new caller).

### ✅ BUILT **AND DEPLOYED** 2026-09-06 — rung 9c, "a visit carries a crew" (merged as `bd50ab1`, PR #208)

**DEPLOYED to the pilot 2026-09-06, 20:11–20:20Z.** Order run, and it is the 9a/9b idiom:
additive migration → fork sync → deploy → drop migration → seat restarts → live E2E.

| # | step | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | preflight | no technician held two overlapping confirmed timed visits, so the backfill could not trip the relocated guard; `assignments` relic already gone; `btree_gist` present; only `v_schedule` + `v_cancelled_visits` depended on the column |
| 1 | `20260907100000` (additive) | **390 participants from 390 visits**, 0 desynced, 0 cross-tenant, 0 left at the `proposed` default (the fill trigger ran on every row) |
| 1b | PostgREST probes | `visits?select=id,technicians(ref)` → **200**. This is the one that had to be checked: a composite PK would have answered 300 `PGRST201` and broken the still-running old code for the whole window |
| 2 | fork sync + 5 deploys | all five `live` on `bd50ab1`; web `/health` confirmed it |
| 3 | `20260907100100` (the drop) | `visits.technician_id` gone, only `no_confirmed_overlap_participant` left, 3 triggers, `technician_refs` on both views, **0 grants leaked to anon/authenticated**, 3 to `spine_reader` |
| 4 | seat gateways | s2 canary first, then s3 s5 s6 s7 s8 |
| 5 | live E2E through s2 | "Both Ben and Joy are going" → **ONE visit, crew of two**, 0 desynced. `v_schedule` returns one row with `{ben-shelton, manager-joy}`; `v_visit_participants` returns two |
| 6 | the guard, provoked live | a rolled-back probe tried to put Ben on an overlapping trip: **23P01**, refused, zero residue |
| 7 | `lifecycle-heart` | five consecutive ticks `ok: true`, watchers `live`, all alarms 0 |

- **TRAP — `nemoclaw <s> recover` REWRITES THE GLOBAL INFERENCE ROUTE (2026-09-06, hit live during
  the 9c seat pass).** s3's gateway did not come back from `gateway restart`, so `nemoclaw s3
  recover` was run. It recovered the gateway AND said: *"gateway inference route
  (openrouter-prod/@preset/trades-seats) differs from the recorded route for sandbox 's3'
  (nvidia-prod/nvidia/nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b). Aligning the gateway to nvidia-prod…"* — and it
  aligned the **whole fleet's** route, not s3's: `openshell inference get -g nemoclaw` then read
  `nvidia-prod / nemotron-3-super-120b-a12b`. The per-sandbox "recorded route" is stale (pre-2026-09-05)
  and `recover` treats it as truth. **The fix it prints is WRONG** — `nemoclaw inference set
  --provider openrouter-prod` is rejected with *"Unsupported provider 'openrouter-prod'"*; the real
  command is the `openshell` one:
  `openshell inference set -g nemoclaw --provider openrouter-prod --model @preset/trades-seats`.
  **What actually saved it: `nemoclaw-inference-medic.timer` re-asserted the route within one
  5-minute interval** (config version bumped 13219 → 13220), so the blast radius was ~6 minutes.
  Check `openshell inference get -g nemoclaw` after ANY `recover`, and do not reach for `recover`
  when `gateway restart` already reported health passed.

- **TRAP — `nemoclaw <s> status` LIES about the gateway right after a restart.** s3 and s5 both
  reported `OpenClaw: not running` / *"The sandbox is alive but the OpenClaw gateway process is not
  running"* while the gateway was up, had registered its NemoClaw plugin, and was actively polling
  `api.telegram.org/getUpdates` in `nemoclaw <s> logs`. s3 then answered a full agent turn
  correctly. **The authoritative signals are the restart's own `✓ Gateway restarted; health passed`
  and an actual `nemoclaw <s> agent --agent main -m "…"` turn** — never `status` alone. Believing
  `status` is what triggered the needless `recover` above, and therefore the route regression.

**THE WINDOW WAS EMPTY, and only by luck of ordering.** `Calls-worker` and `Calls-purge`
auto-deploy, so the fork sync put them on 9c code at 20:11:59 — migration 1 landed at 20:11:58,
one second earlier. Zero captures, visits or schedule events were written in the gap. **The
durable rule: apply the additive migration BEFORE the fork sync, never after** — the sync is the
deploy for the two auto-deploy services, and nobody controls when their build finishes.

- **THE BOARD SENDS A CREW, NOT A PERSON — found by the final review, and it was a data-loss
  bug** (2026-09-06). `web/board/board.js` was not in the 9c plan's file list and the diff never
  touched `web/`, but every board write verb now means **REPLACE THE CREW**. The team view emits
  ONE STOP PER PERSON, so a two-person trip draws two otherwise identical cards; dragging Ben's
  card into Sam's lane sent `technicianRef: sam` and **deleted Joy from a trip she was still going
  on** — silently, with no history table to recover from, and Joy got a "moved off your schedule"
  push. Three more paths did the same: the detail sheet's Technician pencil (which printed
  `technicianName`, the FIRST of the crew, so the sheet never admitted Joy was there), and the
  tray's Assign on an **untimed crewed** visit — the tray deliberately holds those
  (`listUnassignedVisitsWithRefs`' `|| starts_at === null` arm), so "unassigned tray" is not the
  same set as "nobody on it". Fixed by expressing every gesture as crew ARITHMETIC against a new
  `data-crew` attribute: a lane drop swaps only the dragged person, an Unassigned-lane drop takes
  only that person off (parking the visit only when nobody is left), and both the sheet and the
  tray now PRINT the whole crew and name who a replace removes. **The standing rule this leaves:
  a schema change that alters what a write verb MEANS has to list `web/` in its file footprint,
  even while the board is "just kept working" pending its rebuild** — `web/` has no test harness
  at all, so nothing else will catch it.

- **A visit carries a CREW, and a crew has no lead** — `visits.technician_id` is GONE. Who is on a trip lives in `visit_participants`
(one row per person), and two technicians on one job at one time is ONE visit with two rows there
— not two visits nothing joins. A crew has **no lead**: everybody on it is equal, a note from
either of them closes the trip, and an unreported crew visit raises ONE gap.

- **The double-booking guard MOVED with the person** — it needs one row that knows both who and
  when, and an exclusion constraint cannot reach a second table. The guard is
`EXCLUDE USING gist (technician_id WITH =, tstzrange(starts_at, ends_at) WITH &&)
WHERE status='confirmed' AND starts_at IS NOT NULL`. It works ONLY because the person and the
times sit on the SAME ROW, and a Postgres exclusion constraint cannot reach a second table. So
moving the person off `visits` meant moving the guard with them: each participant row carries a
DERIVED copy of the trip's `starts_at`/`ends_at`/`status`, filled by a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE`
trigger and kept in step by an `AFTER` trigger on `visits`. **Probed, not assumed** (local stack,
2026-09-06): an exclusion raised from inside an AFTER trigger reaches PostgREST as
`{"code":"23P01"}`, the parent UPDATE rolls back whole, and a non-overlapping move syncs every
copy — so `isOverlapViolation` and rung 9b's "nothing changed" argument survive untouched.

**Five traps this rung found, each measured:**

- **A composite PK on a link table makes PostgREST synthesise a junction** — HTTP 300 PGRST201 on
  every `technicians` embed. Use a surrogate key on any two-FK link table. With
   `PRIMARY KEY (visit_id, technician_id)`, PostgREST reads `visit_participants` as a junction and
   invents a many-to-many `visits ↔ technicians` that collides with `visits_technician_id_fkey` —
   every select embedding `technicians` from `visits` answers **HTTP 300 `PGRST201`**. That would
   have broken the still-running old code for the whole deploy window. A surrogate `id` with
   `UNIQUE (visit_id, technician_id)` gives the same guarantee and no junction (probed: 200).
   Pinned by a schema test. **Do not tidy it back into a composite key.**
- **A bare `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` swallows an exclusion constraint** — always name the conflict
  target. It arbitrates over EVERY
   usable constraint, exclusion constraints included — so the relocated guard was silently OFF on
   the trigger's own insert path. Always name the conflict target.
- **Moving a filter from SQL into JS meets PostgREST's `max_rows`** — page the reader, or the
  1000-row cap truncates before the filter runs. "Nobody is on it" is an
   anti-join PostgREST cannot express, so four readers now fetch and filter in JS — and the
   1000-row cap truncates BEFORE the filter runs, silently. All four are paged.
- **A derived column needs its trigger on UPDATE too, and a `FOR SHARE` on the parent** — or the
  copy drifts from its source and the guard checks a lie. Without
   the first, a hand-written UPDATE desynchronises a participant from its trip; without the second,
   a participant inserted concurrently with a move commits carrying the pre-move times. Either way
   the guard ends up checking a time nobody is going.
- **Order is load-bearing in two different places** — leavers off before the times move, and the
  person reconciled before the times inside the trigger. App-side: `moveVisitRow` removes leavers
   BEFORE the UPDATE, or "Joy is out, Ben covers it" is refused because Joy is busy at the target.
   Trigger-side (while the bridge existed): reconcile the person BEFORE syncing the times, in ONE
   function — two triggers fire alphabetically and the wrong order refuses the same move.

- **Deploy shape: an additive migration BEFORE the code, the drop AFTER** (the 9b pair, reused),
  and the second window is NOT free — see the plan. `20260907100000` is additive and goes BEFORE the code —
the old column, its guard and its index all survive, and a bridge arm in the trigger materialises
`technician_id` as participant #1 so the old code keeps working. `20260907100100` goes AFTER all
five services are live: it rebuilds `v_schedule`/`v_cancelled_visits` with `technician_refs
text[]` / `technician_names text[]` (**one row per TRIP** — one row per participant would make
`count(*)` count people again), adds `v_visit_participants` for per-person questions, drops the
bridge arm, the guard, the index and the column.

---

### ✅ BUILT **AND DEPLOYED** 2026-09-05/06 — rung 9b, "the visit is one object" (merged `c7d69f8`, PR #206)

**Deploy 03:34–03:56Z. Evidence, not tool success lines.** Migration → all five Render services →
seats → tick → compat-view drop. **Pilot schema md5 now EQUALS local:
`8fe55faf8d9e040fe0e2836136a250d1`, 643 columns.**

| check | evidence |
|---|---|
| migration | 467 schedule rows carried, 0 lost; all 62 `completed` rows stamped by the backfill, 0 unstamped; `raw_captures.visit_id` non-null on 0 rows so the FK re-point validated |
| the bridge | `assignments` relkind `v`, 467 rows; **all three embed shapes 200** incl. the REVERSE `jobs?select=…,assignments(id,status)` |
| five services | web · lifecycle-heart · Board_Worker · Calls-worker · Calls-purge all `live` on `c7d69f8` |
| **one trip, one id** | booked untimed → moved → moved → cancelled = **4 `schedule_events` on ONE `assignment_id`**, `created_at` never changed, `updated_at` bumped each time |
| the stages | `v_schedule.stage` read `committed`, then `placed`, then `reported` on the right rows |
| a note attaches | technician note on a same-day booked visit → 1 `visit_notes` row, status `completed`, `completed_at` 20:48 FIRM-LOCAL, stage `reported`, **no second visit minted** |
| confirmations | unschedule of a PLACED visit staged `pending_visit_cancel` carrying `visitId`; confirm resolved it, visit `cancelled`, job still `open`, 0 open tokens left |
| **the backlog did NOT re-flag** | open `overdue_visit_gap` 100 → 98, **0 review_items created since deploy**; `lifecycle-heart` ran clean at 03:50Z |
| exposure | `v_schedule`, `v_cancelled_visits`, `v_visit_notes`, `visit_notes` all anon=false, authenticated=false |

**Three things the deploy taught that the plan did not know:**

1. **A gateway restart is the whole seat "wire" when only tool SCHEMAS changed.** Seats run off the
   gateway's cached `tools/list`, so `nemoclaw <s> gateway restart` is what makes `visitId` visible.
   A full `wire` was NOT needed and would have been wrong: it pushes doctrine from whichever
   `bootstrap-box.sh` copy is on the box, and **all six copies were stale against the repo**
   (the drift trap, again — md5 every time). 9b changed no doctrine text, which is why this was safe.
   s2's restart reported `health did not pass before timeout`; `doctor` said healthy and the seat
   then booked and moved for real. **Treat that message as a slow start, not a failure** — verify
   functionally before acting on it.
2. **⚠ STALE CONFIRMED VISITS DEFEAT THE NOTE-ATTACH RULE'S STEP 2, on real data.** A note attaches
   to "the job's ONE live visit" only when there is exactly one. The pilot's Marcus Ellery job
   carried a `confirmed` visit from 2026-08-23 that nobody ever completed, so the job had TWO live
   visits and a note filed against it fell through to step 3 and **minted a new `completed` visit**.
   The code did exactly what it was specified to do; the specification assumed jobs do not
   accumulate long-past `confirmed` rows, and they do. Consequence to carry into rung 9c/10: either
   step 2 should ignore visits whose day is long past, or something must sweep stale `confirmed`
   visits.
   **⚠ Do NOT size this from the pilot** ([[never-size-from-the-pilot-box]], and I nearly did):
   step 2 is defeated only when a job has MORE THAN ONE live visit, which is **3 of 300** open jobs
   there — the one I hit was one of the three, which made it read as common. What the pilot shows is
   the SHAPE, not the rate: 281 of those jobs carry a `confirmed` visit more than three days past
   that nobody ever completed, and that is an artifact of seeded data nobody closes out, not a
   forecast of a real firm. The mechanism is worth fixing; its frequency is unknown and this
   environment cannot tell you.
3. **`visits` inherited `assignments`' anon/authenticated SELECT on the pilot** — the rename carries
   grants. This is the pre-existing recorded posture (`20260824120000` was never applied to the
   pilot, so RLS is off on these tables) and NOT something this rung introduced, but it is a real
   difference from local, where the same table is anon=false. The `v_*` views fail closed either
   way. Flagged for an owner decision, not changed unilaterally.

**Deploy-window note, for the record:** `Calls-worker` and `Calls-purge` auto-deploy, so the fork
sync put them on new code ~1 minute BEFORE the migration landed. The pilot had taken 0 captures in
3 days, so nothing hit it. On a busy pilot, apply the migration BEFORE syncing the fork.

#### The build itself (`68bacf9`, `0dd4a0b`, `be54d85`, `a26e498`, `618d6ef`)

**What was wrong.** TWO tables both meant "visit" and nothing joined them. `assignments` was the
schedule — every `*_visit` tool wrote it, the board read it, the GiST exclusion guarded it.
`visits` minted one row PER FIELD NOTE and nothing ever scheduled from it, so a technician filing
twice about one trip produced two rows and a crew of two produced two more. Invisible, because
nothing displayed that table. The moment `visits` BECAME the schedule, those rows would have shown
on the board as work nobody did — which is why the note-attach rule (below) is part of this rung
and not a later one.

**The shape now.** ONE row per trip, keeping the name `visits`, with a lifecycle DERIVED from the
row rather than stored in a ladder: `committed` (confirmed, untimed) → `placed` (confirmed, timed)
→ `happened` (completed) → `reported` (a `visit_notes` row exists), plus `cancelled`. The
`visit_status` enum is unchanged; `stage` is a `case` in `v_schedule` and `stageOf()` in
`src/db/visits.ts`. **Status is tested FIRST** — a `visit_notes` row only splits a `completed`
visit into `reported` vs `happened`; a note linked to a still-`confirmed` visit leaves it `placed`.
So `where stage='reported'` is NOT "trips with a note on file". `proposed` is still written by
nothing — a later cleanup.

**Survivor = `assignments`, RENAMED.** Settled by probes on the local stack, not by preference:
`raw_captures.visit_id → visits ON DELETE SET NULL` is implemented as an UPDATE and therefore
TRIPS `raise_append_only`, so the 288 event-linked schedule rows could be neither deleted nor
moved. `DROP TABLE … CASCADE` does not trip it; `RENAME` keeps the FK and fires nothing. So the
schedule table was renamed and the 20 per-note rows were dropped with CASCADE (their notes stay in
L1). **No trigger was disabled at any point.** The user had waived the pilot's data
(2026-09-05: *"all of the data that exist in the pilot right now is test and seeded anyways"*).

| Phase | What landed | Migration |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `assignments`→`visits`, old `visits` dropped, `visit_notes`, merged columns, four views rebuilt | `20260906140000` |
| 2 | a move is an UPDATE in place — the visit's id outlives every move/park/attach | none |
| 3 | a note ATTACHES to the trip it reports (`resolveVisitForNote`) | none |
| 4 | doctrine, docs, deploy | `20260906140100` drops the compat view |

**Gates at the end of phase 3:** typecheck / lint / build clean, **2556 passed, 12 skipped** (up
from 2544 by exactly the 12 tests added; none deleted — the rung-9a mass-edit lesson applied).

**The note-attach rule, in order** (`src/writePath/attachNote.ts`): (1) the job's visit PLACED on
the note's own firm-local day, held by this technician or by nobody, nearest start winning a tie;
(2) else the job's ONE live visit, whatever day it sits on — the write-it-up-next-morning case;
(3) else CREATE one, already happened. **ATTACH IS CHANNEL-BLIND; CREATE AND REPORT ARE NOT.** An
inbound email answering a question about a booked visit is evidence about that trip and now links
to it — before this it linked nothing and was invisible to the planner's evidence read and the
watcher's activity probe. Only `technician_voice|technician_photo|technician_note` may mint a
visit or flip one to `completed`. **This is a rule about the NOTE DOOR, not about people** —
managers create visits all day through `book_visit` (admin-gated, untouched), and a manager filing
a field note still creates one. ⚠ **The "INTERIM" note that stood here was wrong about what came
next.** It said rung 10 would let inbound create a COMMITTED visit; rung 10 instead made inbound
create NOTHING and ASK (user, 2026-09-06/07). The channel gate stayed exactly as this paragraph
describes it — see §10.

**Traps and findings worth carrying:**
- **`ON DELETE SET NULL` is an UPDATE.** Against an append-only trigger it fails at DELETE time,
  not at DDL time — so the constraint looks fine until the day something tries to delete.
- **Naive-local vs real UTC, again.** Visit times are firm wall clock stamped as if UTC;
  `raw_captures.captured_at` is a real instant. Comparing them raw makes an 08:00 note look later
  than a 09:00 visit in `America/Los_Angeles`. Converted ONCE, in `noteLocalOf()`.
  **And do not compare those strings with `<`** — `2026-09-08T09:00:00+00` vs
  `…T09:00:00.000Z` is decided by `+` vs `.`, not by time. `Date.parse` both sides.
- **A once-ever index plus stable ids = a detector that fires once, forever.**
  `uq_observations_subject_ever` is unique per `(tenant, kind, subject_ref)` **and is PARTIAL —
  `where author = 'detector'`** (`20260904110000_observations_author.sql`), so it constrains the
  detectors and not the planner's own rows. Ids used to change
  on every move, which accidentally re-armed the overdue detector. With ids surviving moves the
  subject became `${visitId}@${starts_at}`, and the dedup probe asks BOTH spellings — without
  that, the entire existing backlog would have been re-flagged on the first tick.
- **A liveness filter can be bypassed by an id rename.** `boardNotify` resolved the notify target
  from the visit row; once a move no longer created a successor row, the PREVIOUS holder had to be
  looked up separately — and the first cut skipped the liveness check, which would have pushed
  schedule details to an offboarded ex-employee's phone. An existing test caught it.
- **A compat view bridges the deploy.** A plain `select … from visits` view named `assignments` is
  auto-updatable, so the five Render services keep working between the migration and the deploy;
  dropped by the second migration once all five are live. ⚠ **The load-bearing half — that
  PostgREST resolves EMBEDS through the view** (the running code does
  `from('assignments').select('*, jobs(…), technicians(…)')`) — cannot be covered by a test,
  because `20260906140100` drops the view in the same `db reset`, so no test ever sees the bridge.
  **PROVEN by hand instead (2026-09-05):** the view was created on a scratch stack and PostgREST
  probed directly — the forward embed, the `!inner` variant, `schedule_events → assignments`, and
  the REVERSE `jobs?select=…,assignments(id,status)` (`UNSCHEDULED_JOBS_SELECT`, hit on every board
  load, and the direction most likely to fail) all return 200; `information_schema.views` reports
  `is_insertable_into=YES, is_updatable=YES`; and the NEW code's shapes also return 200 with the
  view present, so view-and-table coexistence raises no PGRST201 ambiguity. **The lesson is the
  method, not the result:** a claim that no test can reach is not "probably fine", it is unverified
  until someone builds the situation and looks.
- **`schedule_events.assignment_id` KEEPS ITS NAME this rung.** That table is append-only and a
  column has no bridge, so renaming it would 400 every schedule write in the deploy window. The TS
  input is `visitId`, mapped onto the column. Code-first rename later.
- **The completed-stamp CHECK was dropped.** `visits_completed_has_stamp` blocked the legal status
  walk (the `cancel_job` cascade, the eval seeders); `completed_at` is optional detail, not an
  invariant.

**Deferred, deliberately:** **9c multi-technician** (a participant table; `technician_id` stays the
single nullable lead) — this rung counts a crew of two as ONE visit but does not name the second
person. **A capture on a job with NO live visit still links nothing** — a test documents it rather
than leaving it implicit. ✅ CLOSED by rung 10, but not the way this line predicted: the capture
is pinned to the JOB (`job_captures`), not given a visit of its own.

- **RECORDED, NOT FIXED — a crew edit is three HTTP round trips and is NOT atomic.**
  `moveVisitRow`, `setVisitParticipants` and `attachVisitParticipants` all do DELETE leavers →
  UPDATE `visits` → UPSERT joiners as separate PostgREST calls, because the DB cannot do it in one
  (the "APP ORDER PIN" test proves leaver-first is required, or the exclusion refuses a legitimate
  reassign-and-move). The leaver DELETE commits on its own; compensation is `restoreLeavers()`,
  which is in-process and best-effort. A dyno recycle between calls leaves a real person off a trip
  that did not move, with no event appended and nothing to reconcile it. This is a genuine
  regression from 9b's single UPDATE. Fix shape when it matters: a `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC that does
  the whole crew edit in one transaction.

- **RECORDED, NOT FIXED — the tray became an unbounded full-table read.** The "nobody is on it"
  test moved from SQL to JS (PostgREST has no anti-join), so `listUnassignedVisitsWithRefs`,
  `listUnassignedVisits` and `listIdenticalUnassignedVisits` narrow only on time — and
  `readLooseBucket` passes **the epoch**. Every board load now pages every confirmed visit the
  tenant has ever had, with a 4-level embed, and discards nearly all of it. Invisible at the
  pilot's 470 visits; it grows linearly forever on the hot path the board-latency doctrine exists
  to protect.

### ✅ BUILT **AND DEPLOYED** 2026-09-05 — rung 9a, "the rails come out" (merged as `e89cf74`, PR #204)

**Deploy done 06:38–06:53Z, in the order below. Evidence, not tool success lines:** the CHECK drop
first; then all FIVE Render services verified live on `e89cf74`; then the column drop; then the six
seats. Post-migration `lifecycle-heart` tick returned `"watchers":{"mode":"live","tenants":1,
"gapsFound":2,"tenantErrors":0}, "ok":true`. Pilot schema md5 now equals local:
`c2986e774ef6a837330a35ef665858a8`, 646 columns. Both views read as `spine_reader` (164 / 94 rows)
with `anon` and `authenticated` false — which also CLOSED a pre-existing exposure: on the pilot
`v_cancelled_visits` had carried full Data-API grants to anon/authenticated, and the drop+recreate
+revoke removed them.

**Two deploy-path facts the plan did not know, both worth carrying:**
1. **Render deploys from a FORK.** `Yu-C1/TradesDatabase` is a fork of `hongyi42/TradesDatabase`,
   and every service points at *its* main. Merging the PR does nothing until the fork is synced.
2. **There are FIVE services, and three have `autoDeploy: no`** — `TradesDatabase` (web),
   `lifecycle-heart` (cron), `Board_Worker` (worker). Only `Calls-worker` and `Calls-purge`
   auto-deploy. At the moment of the sync, `lifecycle-heart` was 2 commits behind and
   `Board_Worker` **nine days** behind; both had to be deployed by hand. All five reach
   `db/assignments` (verified by walking the built import graph), so all five gate the column drop.
   Their quiet drift is a standing hazard independent of this rung.

**Box note:** `nemoclaw` is only on the LOGIN-shell PATH (`/home/ubuntu/.local/bin`), so a wire pass
over non-interactive ssh must use `bash -lc` or it dies at `nemoclaw: command not found`. The fleet
is now **s2, s3, s5, s6, s7, s8** — not the s1–s6 older notes record.

**Manual checks run through the seats, 2026-09-05** (the plan's three Phase-3 manual items, agent-driven,
maker ≠ checker, DB read-back via `supabase-test`): s2 (manager-sam, 17:36–17:45Z, from the fleet-to-OpenRouter
session) and s8 (manager-joy, 19:40–19:43Z), both on the GATEWAY path (no `[agent/embedded]`), model
`meta/muse-spark-1.3`. **Book with no time** → `book_visit {jobId}` in ONE step; row `confirmed / starts_at NULL /
technician_id NULL` — exactly the tray reader's predicate. **Move** → `moved` in one step, untimed→Tue AND
Tue→Wed, zero `review_items` minted, each move = cancel+insert with `created_at` carried. **Unschedule a PLACED
visit** → `needs_confirmation` + an open `pending_visit_cancel` row; "Yes" → `unscheduled`, item `resolved`
("unschedule confirmed by manager-joy"), row `cancelled`, job stays `open`, tray rows for the job 0. Verification
job: customer Marcus Ellery / job `9b014ad9…` — reuse it. Recipe = `scripts/spine-eval/run-gateway.mjs`'s turn line
(`nemoclaw <s> exec --no-tty -- bash -lc 'openclaw agent --agent main --session-id <fresh> -m …'`, no `--local`,
no `--deliver`, fresh session id so the manager's Telegram context is untouched); 15–30 s per turn after the
first. s2 was carrying a date-eval at the time, so s8 was used — any manager seat of the tenant works. A staged
confirmation that is never answered stays `open` and VISIBLE in `list_review_items` (no sweeper); the clean way out is
the app path — a late "yes" hits the 15-minute expiry, which dismisses it as `confirmation expired` (proven on s2, 19:46Z).


Three phases, all landed and gated (typecheck / lint / 2545 tests / build, and `supabase db reset`
proving both migrations apply from scratch). Plan:
`thoughts/shared/plans/2026-09-05-the-rails-come-out.md`.

| Phase | What went | Migration |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `promise_level`, `promised_day`, `set_promise`, and the confirmations they gated | none (code only) |
| 2 | the stored window `earliest_date`/`latest_date`, `timeTbd`, the per-day board strip | `20260906110000` drops `assignment_placed_or_windowed` |
| 3 | the four columns, `promised_day_when_promised`, the `promise_level` enum, both views rebuilt | `20260906120000` |

**Confirmations, re-cut:** `book_visit` and `move_visit` are unconditionally ONE-SHOT;
`unschedule_visit` stages only when the visit is PLACED; `cancel_job` unchanged (always stages).
`pending_booking` and `pending_visit_move` review kinds are deleted — nothing mints them.

**DEPLOY ORDER — the two migrations are OPPOSITES, and each has exactly one wrong order:**

```
20260906110000  (drops a CHECK)     BEFORE the deploy, or with it
20260906120000  (drops the columns) AFTER the deploy
BOTH Render services (web AND lifecycle-heart cron) on the new commit before 120000
```

`110000` is often described as "safe either way" because dropping a constraint only widens what
the table accepts — true for the code already running, FALSE for the code arriving:
`createAssignment` writes an untimed visit as all-NULL, which `assignment_placed_or_windowed`
rejects with 23514, so applying it late breaks every untimed booking. `120000` is the reverse of
this repo's usual rule, because pilot code still running WRITES `promise_level` / `earliest_date`
on every insert. The cron service matters as much as the web one: `src/jobs/lifecycle.ts` imports
the spine tool surface, and the deployed `src/db/context.ts` / `src/schedule/boardRead.ts` SELECT
all four columns — those reads 400 the moment the columns vanish.

Before deploying, dismiss any open `pending_booking` / `pending_visit_move` review items
(15-minute expiry, so the window is small). **And re-run the box doctrine (`wire`) on all six
seats** — until that happens their AGENTS.md still teaches promise-gated staging and `timeTbd`
booking against a server where `set_promise` no longer exists and book/move never stage.

**Traps this rung produced, worth carrying:**
- **A green suite proves nothing when you mass-edit tests.** Deleting a test that asserts on a
  removed feature is correct; deleting one that merely *set up* the feature while asserting on
  something that survives is silent coverage loss. Classify every test before touching it —
  delete / invert / strip-the-fixture — and account for the count. It cost a real coverage loss
  in `toolCases.ts` before the discipline was adopted.
- **A test can pin the bug.** The tray reader's own test asserted `rows.every(technician_id ===
  null)`, which is exactly why an assigned untimed visit going invisible was invisible to the
  suite. When a fix breaks a test, check which of the two is wrong.
- **Reachability is not a compile error.** Untimed visits sit on no day, so every surface that
  showed a visit had to be re-checked by hand: two holes were found this way (recall mode
  collected untimed rows and never returned them; windowed mode resolved their customer to null
  because their jobs were absent from the jobs fetch).
- ⚠ **RETIRED by rung 9b (2026-09-05): the `created_at` carry.** It read: *"`created_at` now means
  the VISIT's age, not the row's — carried onto the successor of every move/park/attach, because
  those are cancel-plus-insert and an oldest-first queue would otherwise mean
  least-recently-touched."* The hack existed only because a move CANCELLED the row and inserted a
  successor. Rung 9b makes a move an UPDATE in place, so there is no successor to carry anything
  onto and `created_at` is the visit's age because it is the row's age. The `updated_at` tiebreak
  it forced on `listAssignmentsForJob` went with it. **The lesson survives the mechanism:** when a
  column's meaning has to be hand-propagated to keep a query honest, the shape underneath is
  usually wrong — fix the shape, not the propagation.
- `find_slots(earliestDate, latestDate)` SURVIVES — same words, different thing: a search range,
  never a commitment. Do not delete it while deleting the window.


**The remodel (user's model, 2026-09-03, ⚠ SUPERSEDED on its central point — see below):** a call →
the extractor judges the job and, if the firm committed to a visit, creates it UNPLACED (the
accepted carve-out: never on a day, never with a person; a wrong inference is an entry in the
unscheduled list the manager can drop — create, don't stage). **AMENDED 2026-09-05 (user): EMAIL
inbound is in rung 10 too** — *"it's just an extra input
path similar to phone calls anyways."* The rung is INBOUND-shaped, not phone-shaped: a call and an
email are the same event (correspondence in which the firm may commit to coming out), and they
already share one write path (`recordFieldNote`, reached from `src/inbound/webhook.ts` and the call
worker). Web form is the same shape and should be settled with it.

**⚠ "CREATE, DON'T STAGE" WAS OVERTURNED BEFORE IT SHIPPED — user, 2026-09-06 and 2026-09-07:**
*"Before creating the visit, someone gets asked"*, and then *"for new jobs, we also want the planner
to ask the manager, so the manager can know instead of being blind to the job being created."* The
argument that a wrong inference is merely a droppable row did not survive the widening: a job
appearing on the record that no human was told about is not droppable if nobody knows to drop it.
What shipped is ASK-FIRST — see the rung 10 record below. Rung 9b's technician-channel gate is
therefore NOT interim after all: inbound still creates no visit, and now creates no job either.
ONLY the manager touches the schedule, by hand or through their agent, which asks
(duration, time, who), has Google Maps tools, and uses its own judgment about the route optimizer.
The planner reads the schedule + unplaced visits, RECOMMENDS, learns how this firm schedules, and
passes that down to the seats. The promise ladder collapses into the visit's own window. The solver
ritual (`run_freeze` / `run_resolve` / `undo_run` / `set_visit_locks` + `locked`, ~2,400 lines, ~4.4 KB
of per-turn doctrine) is deleted — `locked` is read only by the solver and the board display, so
pinning exists purely to defend against our own auto-scheduler. `find_slots` is NOT the solver and
stays.

**The structural consequence:** the visit becomes ONE object. Which table survives is a choice on
MERIT, not force: `raw_captures.visit_id → visits ON DELETE SET NULL` + the append-only trigger is
what makes `visits` rows undeletable, and the user WAIVED that for this one pre-production migration
(2026-09-03: *"nothing is in production yet… even deleting L1 data is possible"* — the pilot counts
as a test surface, Ben/Joy/Judy rows included). Likely shape, to be settled by rung 9's research:
`assignments`' structure (real FK, both window shapes, lifecycle enum, the exclusion constraint) +
`visits`' name, L3 `attributes` slot and happened-stage fields, the old `visits` rows folded in as
"happened", one L1 repoint under the waiver. Open: a note with NO scheduled visit (walk-in,
emergency) must still land — attach-when-one-exists AND create-as-happened both survive, and
"attach to WHICH visit" is a new matching judgment.
