reading material and outlived it by nothing]**; **planner_lessons + the Curator** (evidence-cited, token-budget
bounded `LESSONS_BLOCK_BUDGET=2000` chars, two-strike retirement, 60-day stale expiry,
weekday-yes/dates-no rail); **the Reflector** (one call/firm/day max, gates: 2am+, marker,
≥15 summaries, ≥5 new since last SUCCESS — failure markers are ok=false and never advance the
watermark; REFLECTOR_MODE=shadow LIVE on lifecycle-heart, gate-skip verified in the tick JSON);
**the closed loop** (LESSONS block in the planner user message, lesson-id stamping in `reason`
for measurability, escalation self-check over v_review_items, live/shadow usage split in the
report; `npm run lessons:planner` = the operator console whose interface is deliberately the
future adversarial judge's).

**Review-round lessons worth keeping:** the first `late` metric definition was structurally
dead (all branches false by construction) — a paired metric that cannot fire un-pairs its twin;
an append-only summary must HOLD BACK until late-arriving facts (manager fates) land or it
poisons the corpus forever; failure markers must not advance freshness watermarks; everything
crossing DB→prompt flattens at RENDER time when rows already exist; and test fixtures mixing a
fixed `NOW` with DB `now()` can make two gates alibi each other — pin each gate alone.

**Standing follow-ups:** read the first real shadow proposals with the user when the pilot
corpus passes 15 (activation is deliberately human until the ratified adversarial-judge
mechanism is built); the queue-triage family (above) is unmoved; ~~still unverified: whether
`ANTHROPIC_MODEL` on lifecycle-heart actually names Opus — the code default is
`claude-sonnet-4-6`~~ — **VERIFIED 2026-08-24: the service carries `ANTHROPIC_MODEL=claude-opus-5`
and `MODEL_PROVIDER=anthropic`. The planner is on Opus 5.**
Next on the ladder per standing direction: the agentic eval at scale, then collapsing
investigate/plan/compose into one call. Rung-2 items are PULL-only today (no proactive manager ping — the
no-unearned-pings doctrine); if managers should be NOTIFIED of escalations, that is pacing-work
scope too.

- **The agentic planner eval EXISTS and has its first baseline (2026-08-24).** 65 scenarios
  (8 families, every one provenance-cited) run the planner AS AN AGENT: seeded local worlds,
  production-faithful ticks (real sweep → real planner → the schemas production hands the loop —
  at the time of this baseline just spine_read + recall_decisions; build 3 added read_memory and
  write_memory — through the real MCP dispatch),
  multi-tick arcs with a simulated clock, deterministic graders + a blind Haiku judge.
  First baseline, CORRECTED by the transcript-aware re-judge (docs/agentic-eval-baseline-2026-08.md,
  docs/agentic-eval-rejudge-2026-08.md): **Opus 63/65 (97%) vs GLM 58/65 (89%)** — the
  Haiku-judged 54 vs 42-44 was dominated by judge artifacts (see the judge-blindness trap
  below); truthfulness is NOT the gap (false-claims 10/10 both arms re-judged). Real
  separations: GLM burns deadline option-value (escalates ahead of in-hours deadlines with
  no new signal: CM-09/MG-06/WD-02), skips or loses the handoff note (5/34 note-less
  escalates; one note date-railed away), and had one tool-loop death graded "passed"
  vacuously (CM-02). Opus's two real fails: the CM-03 self-check read miss (both models) and
  WD-05's token 30-minute extend at the 48h ceiling. Harness traps paid for and encoded as tests: the typed
  layer's wall-clock stamps corrupt sim arcs (snapshot-diff fix-up), the sweep eats
  stale-deadline gaps before the planner sees them (corpus rail: due > EVERY tick),
  money_events is append-only (no per-scenario delete), sim runs must not straddle a DST
  transition (driver refuses), and a planner-applied escalate hands off on the NEXT tick
  (grade actions over all ticks). Run: scripts/agentic-eval/run.mjs; grade:
  analyze.mjs [--judge] [--compare]. This corpus is the regression suite and the yardstick
  the one-call collapse must beat — against the RE-JUDGED numbers, after the judge fix.

- **THE ONE-CALL COLLAPSE LANDED (2026-08-24, plan thoughts/shared/plans/2026-08-24-one-call-collapse.md).**
  The planner is now the ONLY model call in the nudge pipeline. Its `send` writes the entire
  technician-facing message and queues it verbatim in one guarded write (`markNudgeQueued`,
  pending-only) — compose's sweep, its per-kind prompts, its context readers and its
  message-assembly templates are all deleted, and with them the code-printed "(re: customer —
  job, visit on DATE)" frame. What the planner writes IS what the technician reads.
  Consequences to hold:
  * **A send with no surviving question is REFUSED**, never silently applied — the date-vocabulary
    rail (`cleanQuestionDraft`) still screens the text, and a dropped draft now costs the SEND,
    not just the draft. The prompt's YOUR MEMORY doctrine carries the one carve-out: this is the
    single refusal the planner should retry, rewritten.
  * **The ladder is MUTE when the planner is not live** (or has no credentials) while
    NUDGES_MODE=live. Reported as `ladderMute` + a loud log on every tick, never `ok=false`
    (a rollback is a legitimate choice). The floor is the 18h never-asked sweep — which itself
    only runs when WATCHERS_MODE=live, so the warning says which case it is.
  * **Ask latency moved from ~5 min to ≤~25 min** for a brand-new gap: compose ran every tick,
    the planner runs on the half hour. Bounded far below NUDGE_DELIVERY_DEADLINE_HOURS (18h).
  * **`teamLeadCompose` disappearing from cost_events is EXPECTED**, not breakage — there is no
    compose call left to bill. Only `teamLeadPlanner` remains on the ladder's ledger line.
  * **Thin-row handling changed owner:** compose used to hand a display-thin pending row to the
    manager within 5 minutes. Now such a row is asked with the gap view's placeholder copy or
    waits for the 18h sweep; a row too tampered even for `spec.handoff` stays frozen with loud
    logs (the pre-existing frozen-row doctrine). Detector-written rows are never thin.
  * **THE ANCHOR IS LOAD-BEARING, not decoration.** Code appends "(re: customer — job)" to
    every queued question (`anchorLine`, planner.ts). Standing box doctrine — AGENTS.md
    "Questions and items from the office", on all six seats — files a technician's reply with
    record_field_note "for the job the (re: …) line names" and ASKS rather than guessing
    otherwise. The final review caught the collapse deleting its only producer: a bare
    question makes "yeah, all done" unfilable, the answer never lands, and the gap escalates
    claiming nobody responded. Never ship a delivered question without it; a row that cannot
    name itself ships bare rather than fabricating one.
  * **Two known gaps, accepted (2026-08-24) rather than discovered later:** (a) there is no
    verb for "this should not be asked, give it to a person" on a PENDING row — compose's
    `ask:false` decline path died with it, so such a row waits for the 18h expiry sweep
    (`escalate`/`leave` are rail-refused on pending, `close` needs evidence); (b) the ladder
    lost its "how many questions actually went out" counter (`ComposeSummary.composed`) —
    `byAction.send` counts INTENT before apply, and the draftless-send refusal that now
    silences a gap has no distinguishable counter. Both are candidates for the next pass.
  * **Eval comparability:** on any future corpus rerun, a rail-dropped question now fails its
    scenario's send expectation instead of falling back to compose. The 63/65 (Opus) baseline
    stays comparable; the GLM rollback arm's ~6 rail hits per run degrade by design.
  * The eval's judge model is now `EVAL_JUDGE_MODEL ?? 'claude-opus-5'` (user ruling: a frontier
    model grades; no Haiku patch), and `--judge` prints the resolved model before spending.
  Live-proven on the local stack 2026-08-24: a seeded pending gap → real Opus planner tick →
  row `queued` with `question` byte-identical to the decision's `question_draft`, read back
  through psql (a second client).

- **TRAP — a note-only judge fails verified claims (the agentic-eval re-judge, 2026-08-24).**
  The eval's Haiku judge grades escalation notes against the gap block ONLY — it never sees
  the model's tool calls. Result in the first baseline: ~8 verdicts flagged "the job is
  still in_progress" as unsupported when the model had READ v_jobs and seen exactly that,
  and ~7 treated factual silence reporting ("has not responded") as blame though it is
  deliberately absent from the firm's blame vocabulary. 27 of 34 judged failures across both
  arms were phantom; the flagship "GLM over-claims" specimen (SMOKE-1: "no visit has been
  logged") was DB-verified truth. Rule: any LLM judge grading claims must be handed the same
  evidence the graded model had (transcript or verified-fact digest), and blame standards
  must be pinned to the firm's vocabulary list, not the judge's instinct. Also caught:
  substring claim checks can self-negate ("hasn't answered" contains "answered" — FC-08),
  and a scenario can fail both arms because it was mis-authored, not because the models
  erred (MG-07). Full verdicts: docs/agentic-eval-rejudge-2026-08.md.

### The live-state re-verification pass, and what had drifted (2026-08-24)

Section 4 had been stamped `2026-07-22` for a month while the system changed under it. Re-reading
it against reality took three cheap reads, and this is the recipe for the next time — **do this
before trusting any "current state" claim, and re-stamp the heading when you do.**

1. **The pilot database** (read-only; `supabase-test` MCP is the LIVE pilot): tenant list with
   per-tenant `customers`/`jobs`/`assignments` counts; `technicians` (state + `sandbox_ref` +
   telegram present/null); `sandbox_pool`; `min/max(starts_at)` on confirmed assignments; exact
   `count(*)` + `max(created_at)` on the brain tables (`nudges`, `nudge_decisions`,
   `review_items`, `money_events`, and — once build 3 and the retirement are deployed —
   `planner_memory` / `planner_runs`). **`gap_summaries`, `planner_lessons` and `reflector_runs`
   were dropped 2026-08-30 and still exist on the PILOT, which has not been migrated: their
   presence there is the deploy-lag signal, not live machinery.**
   Note the estimate trap: `list_tables` returns pg_class ESTIMATES — count exactly.
   The `v_*` views need `spine.tenant_id` set, or every query errors on the missing GUC.
2. **Render**: service list with type, `autoDeploy`, and the LIVE deploy's commit — plus env-var
   NAMES per service (`GET /v1/services/{id}/env-vars`; the MCP server has no env read tool).
   Never print values; `MODEL_PROVIDER` / `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` are the two non-secret exceptions and
   they are the two that decide which model runs.
3. **The repo**: the served tool count (pinned by a test), the view catalog, and
   `activeProvider()` — the routing question is always "which env does THIS service carry".

- **What had drifted, in order of how much it would have misled someone (2026-08-24).**
  - **The model map.** The file said all spine model calls were GLM-5.2 via DeepInfra. DeepInfra
    was retired 2026-08-19 and the calls now split BY SERVICE — Opus 5 on the cron, Fireworks GLM
    in the web service — because `MODEL_PROVIDER` is set on one and not the other.
  - **The tenant list** named two tenants; the pilot has four (the spine-eval world was seeded into
    it on 2026-08-12), and the lifecycle cron sweeps all of them.
  - **`sandbox_pool`** was described as the pool; it is a four-row claim/retire ledger with no row
    for s2/s3/s7/s8, and the eval tenant's `manager-sam` has quietly claimed the free blank s6.
  - **The demo schedule** had expired — a demo run today shows an empty calendar.
  - **Two Render services** are behind main with autoDeploy off (one by 27 days), and the file's
    own Feature-status log still described box rollouts as the thing that goes stale.
  - **Two open questions were already answered** and nobody had gone back to close them: the
    planner IS on Opus 5, and its tool loop is no longer uncached.

The lesson is the one already written at the top of §4 in a different form: **a status board with
no re-verification date is a liability, because it reads exactly like a fresh one.** A build log
never lies about being old; a "current state" section does.

### The ask shape — one message may cover several gaps, and the answer keeps its link (2026-08-25)

Built on `feat/the-ask-shape`, on top of the one-call collapse (PR #179). Four phases, all landed
in the branch; the six-seat doctrine refresh is the only step left and it is user-gated.
Plan: `thoughts/shared/plans/2026-08-25-the-ask-shape.md`.

**What changed, in one line each.** ① A gap closed on a quote now records WHICH capture answered
it, derived at close time from the citation the planner already had to prove. ② `nudges` gained
`ask_group_id`, so several gaps can ride out as ONE message. ③ The planner names the group itself
(`nudgeIds`) — code enforces coherence, never grouping policy. ④ A recorded field note hands the
seat agent back `openQuestions`, so it can follow up while the technician is still on the phone.

**The facts worth keeping:**

- **`ask_group_id` holds the LEAD gap's own id, not a fresh uuid.** The lead is then
  self-identifying (`id = ask_group_id`), so `sessionId: nudge-<lead>`, the box's sent-marker and
  the `(re: …)` anchor all name the gap the planner actually chose. Two earlier drafts disagreed
  about who the lead was ("the `nudgeId` field" vs "earliest-created") — they diverge the moment
  the planner does not pick the oldest gap.
- **Every member row stores the FULL grouped message in `question`.** Not a pointer, not a null.
  `nudgeQueue.ts`'s `if (!row.question) continue` tamper-freeze is the only thing between a
  malformed row and an infinite re-serve loop, and it stays untouched. The consequence is that
  anything reading `question` across a group must dedupe by TEXT.
- **The attribution stamp is an audit link, not a gate.** `attributeAnswerToGap` still declines to
  stamp when 2+ gaps are asked — code cannot tell which question a sentence answered. Batching
  lands squarely in that case, which is exactly why ① exists: the close-derived stamp comes from
  the quote the planner cited, so a batched ask does not go dark.
- **The derived stamp requires a capture a badge-holder SPOKE** — a channel test
  (`technician_note|technician_voice|technician_photo`) AND a non-null ref. The ref alone is
  INERT as a guard: the phone worker stamps the sentinel `'phone-line'` and mail ingest stamps the
  MAILBOX OWNER's ref on a customer's reply, so both foreign shapes carry one.
- **`openQuestions` is deliberately UNFILTERED and can include what the person just said.**
  Attribution declines at 2+ asked, so no "which one did they just close" signal exists. Worse,
  `setNudgeAnsweredBy` does not change `status` and the watcher's activity auto-close needs
  `soleGapOnJob`, so **the list does not shrink as gaps are answered** — the same entries return on
  every subsequent note for that job. §0k judges against the whole conversation, not the last
  message. Reporting what is open is honest; guessing which one closed is not.
- **The follow-up loop has two halves and both had to be written down.** ASK without RECORD leaves
  the gap `asked` and the office re-asks the technician what they just said — §0k clause 1b.
  And the reply to a follow-up is a bare fragment ("about two hundred") that `matchIdentity` cannot
  resolve, so 1b must name the mechanism: `confirmed: {customerId}` with NO `confirmId` (the
  dispatch reads `confirmed` before the confirmId branch and one-shot commits). Without it the
  assistant asks the technician WHICH customer, seconds after recording one for them.
- **④b's zero-inference rule governs the ASK LEG ONLY** (user correction). The seat agent already
  reasons freely once a person replies, so a response-leg follow-up contradicts nothing.
  **Waking the planner on the answer event is RULED OUT** (user, 2026-08-25): a planner-written
  sentence dropping into a live conversation is two voices in one chat, and the planner holds no
  technician write tools if it stayed in the session.

**Traps this build hit, in the order they bit:**

1. **A two-line `## ` heading in the §0k block would have corrupted all six seats.** The block is
   installed by a delete-then-append `awk` that deletes from `/^## Questions and items from the
   office/` to the NEXT `^## ` line — so a second heading line orphans into every AGENTS.md on
   every refresh, and `extractDoctrine` reads the section as empty. Caught by
   `src/eval/doctrine.test.ts`, not by review. **Keep §0k headings on ONE line.**
2. **`doctrine.test.ts` pins SECTIONS, not CLAUSES.** Deleting a clause from §0k failed no test
   until this build added a body assertion. The block is the only instruction the six seats have.
3. **`vitest.config.ts:11` sets `fileParallelism: false`.** Do not reject a test approach on
   "it would poison concurrent files" — nothing runs concurrently. The real objection to a
   `REVOKE`-based failure injection is durability: a restore skipped by a crash breaks the local
   stack for every later file and the next run. `vi.mock` in its OWN file is the clean route, and
   it reaches `src/mcp/server.ts` through the `export * from './nudges.js'` barrel.
4. **Pre-existing, still live:** if `docker exec … grep -q` at `bootstrap-box.sh:1018` fails for any
   reason OTHER than the block's absence (a transient docker error), the delete is skipped and the
   block DOUBLE-APPENDS. No repo test can see it — `doctrine.ts` reads the script, not the seat.
   Audit with `grep -c '^## Questions and items' AGENTS.md` after any fleet refresh.
5. **Known efficiency cost, accepted:** `commitNote` now issues `listAskedNudgesForTechnicianJob`
   twice per recorded note with identical arguments, on the hottest technician write path. Halving
   it means having `attributeAnswerToGap` return what it read, which its `Promise<void>` pin rules
   out.

**FLEET REFRESH DONE 2026-08-25** — all six seats (s2, s3, s5, s6, s7, s8) carry §0k with clauses
1a/1b, verified byte-identical to the canonical block (normalised section md5 `9ee678c4e62c`,
40 lines, all six MATCH). s2 rolled first as canary; the audit ran across all six rather than only
s2, per the rule that s2 being freshest is how drift hid last time. The `diff` against each seat's
in-container `AGENTS.md.prev-20260825` backup shows ONLY the §0k block changed and the section
count unchanged. No restarts, no config writes: `doorman.service` and all six
`nemoclaw-agent-sN.service` stayed running with unchanged container uptimes, `nudge-runner.timer`
and `badge-pull.timer` still waiting. Applied as the §0k step alone, NOT `wire` — wire also
re-applies the egress policy, nine other blocks, the guard plugin and heartbeat/tool-trim config
writes, which is disproportionate for a doctrine-text change (tool count unchanged at 14) and
config writes have downed the gateway before.

**THE COPY-DRIFT TRAP HAD RE-OPENED, in the worst possible copy (found + fixed 2026-08-25).**
`/usr/local/sbin/bootstrap-box.sh` — the copy `recycle-sandbox.sh` actually runs
(`BOOTSTRAP="${RECYCLER_BOOTSTRAP:-/usr/local/sbin/bootstrap-box.sh}"`, line 40) — was still at
**2026-08-15** (`973afe2f…`, commit `c834fbd`), which predates §0k entirely. **Any sandbox
recycled between 2026-08-23 and today would have come back with NO office-questions doctrine at
all** — the seat would see office questions in its channel history with no standing rule to record
the answers, which is the exact failure §0k was written to prevent. Nothing detects this: the
recycler reports a wired blank as healthy, and `doctrine.ts` reads the repo script, never a seat.
SIX copies now live on the box (memory previously said five). Current state after this pass:

| path | before | after | note |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/usr/local/sbin/bootstrap-box.sh` | 2026-08-15 | **repo** | what the recycler runs — was the live defect |
| `~/Box_Setup_Stuff/bootstrap-box.sh` | 2026-08-23 (`d4b0061`) | **repo** | the tree `wire` needs; why seats had the 08-23 block |
| `~/bootstrap-box.sh` | 2026-08-15 | **repo** | the path a human types |
| `~/bss-repo/`, `~/stage-deepinfra-retire/` | 2026-08-15 | untouched | unreferenced by any unit |
| `~/deploy-recycler/` | older than 40 commits | untouched | unreferenced by any unit |

Each updated path kept a `.prev-20260825` backup. **Add to the standing discipline: after changing
any AGENTS.md block, md5 `/usr/local/sbin/bootstrap-box.sh` too — updating the seats without
updating the recycler's copy means the next recycle silently reverts the fleet.**

**Two artifacts of this pass worth knowing.** (1) The seats' old §0k was the 2026-08-23 text, which
lacked the one-call-collapse paragraph ("the office's planner now writes those questions itself…")
— so that paragraph reached the fleet for the first time today, a month-old change nobody had
noticed was undelivered. (2) The delete-then-append idiom leaks ONE BLANK LINE per refresh: the
appended heredoc begins with a blank line, and the `awk` deletes from `^## Questions…` onward,
never the blank line before it. Cosmetic in markdown and pre-existing, but it accumulates — do not
mistake it for corruption after N refreshes.

**LIVE E2E COMPLETE 2026-08-25/26 — the whole ladder ran end to end on a real Telegram chat (s5).**
Five seeded two-gap jobs, five grouped asks, three follow-ups, one correct escalation.

* **Batching works.** Every pair shared one `ask_group_id`, one `question`, one `asked_at`, and
  read as a single natural question — not stapled parts.
* **The follow-up leg works, including the half that fails silently.** The technician answered
  only the visit half; `record_field_note` returned `openQuestions`; the agent asked for the cost
  in its own words; **the answer was recorded and attributed**. Without §0k clause 1b that last
  step is invisible: a perfect-looking chat and a gap still sitting at `asked`.
* **Both attribution paths fired, each in its designed case.** The rooftop-AC job closed TWO gaps
  with TWO DIFFERENT captures — only the Phase-1 close-derived path can do that, since record-time
  attribution declines at 2+ asked. The freezer job's cost gap was stamped at record time, one
  second after its sibling closed left exactly one open.
* **Escalation fired correctly** when a follow-up went unanswered overnight.
* **The planner's group-aware pacing is the result worth keeping.** Unprompted it extended ONCE to
  a working hour ("the default escalation would fire first thing in the morning, so move it to
  mid-morning"), then held every tick after, refusing to re-extend, and reasoned that the cost gap
  "rides on the same single message Ben already has and a cost figure does not decay, so hold the
  shared deadline and escalate it together with the cooling gap." That is doctrine, not code.

**FIVE traps this E2E surfaced, all of which cost real time:**

1. **A nudge delivered to the wrong Telegram id reports SUCCESS at every layer.** Ben's roster row
   carried an id the owner did not monitor; Telegram accepted every message (ids incremented, so a
   real chat existed), the runner exited 0, and `asked_at` was stamped. Nothing anywhere errors.
   **The seat's `allowFrom` must be fixed with it** — `dmPolicy` is `allowlist`, so a roster-only
   fix delivers the question and then silently ignores the reply. Both layers:
   `channels.telegram.accounts.default.allowFrom` in the container AND
   `messaging.plan.channels[].inputs[]` (`inputId: allowedIds`) in `~/.nemoclaw/sandboxes.json`.
2. **The gateway HOT-RELOADS config** (`[reload] config change detected` in `/tmp/gateway.log`).
   Process start time is NOT evidence a config change has not landed — a restart is usually
   unnecessary, and `nemoclaw <s> gateway restart` blocks for over two minutes when it is.
3. **`vitest.config.ts` sets `fileParallelism: false`.** Do not reject a test approach because it
   would "poison concurrent files" — nothing runs concurrently.
4. **RETIRED 2026-08-29 — there is no ask window.** This entry said it was real and enforced at
   `planner.ts:1580` via `insideAskWindow`, skipping the model call (`skippedWindow` in the tick
   summary). All three identifiers are deleted. The planner chooses when a message goes out; what
   gates the model CALL is now a per-firm cadence (`plannerCadenceDue`, `off_cadence` in the tick
   summary). The grep advice generalises and is worth keeping: grep the actual export, not a
   remembered name.
5. **Deliberate seeding hits real constraints, which is the system working.** An exclusion
   constraint (`no_confirmed_overlap`) refused a double-booked assignment for the same technician.

**PERMANENT COST, accepted:** five test customers (Marlow Residence, Ashgrove Bakery, Colvin
Motors, Halverson Dental, Pinehurst Grill) now carry stamped L1 captures and are therefore
**undeletable** on the pilot. `ben-shelton` is now roster state `active` (1 of 5 technician seats)
with `telegram_user_id` pointed at the owner's own account — deliberate, so one human receives both
the manager and technician chats.

**KNOWN DRIFT, deferred by the user:** only s5 carries the #182 copy fixes; s2/s3/s6/s7/s8 are one
version behind on §0k, to be reconciled in a fleet-wide restart. Separately, s3's sandbox allowlist
still holds Ben's OLD id — fix before that seat is reassigned.
Changing §0k also changes `doctrineFingerprint`, so tier-2 eval runs from 2026-08-25 are no longer
directly comparable to earlier ones — nothing pins the hash, so nothing breaks silently, but a
comparison across that date is measuring two different prompts.

### The OpenRouter switch — the spine only (2026-08-28)

**The spine moved to OpenRouter on `z-ai/glm-5.3`; the FLEET did not.** Seat agents stay on
Fireworks / `glm-5p2-fast` until the deferred seat plan lands *(it landed 2026-09-05 — see
"The OpenRouter switch — the fleet" below; both halves now share one key)*. `docs/inference-recovery-runbook.md`
now opens with that split — it covers the fleet only, and the first person to follow it during a
spine outage would otherwise drive the wrong system.

**Why OpenRouter:** failover stops being code we maintain. One model id resolves to ~10 upstream
providers (`z-ai/glm-5.3`; Opus 5 has 9) and OpenRouter falls through on any error. Before this,
`src/model/` resolved ONE endpoint with no retry and a provider outage became `tenantErrors` while
the deterministic constants took over.

**NO `provider` block is sent, and that is the cost decision.** OpenRouter sticky-routes a model to
one upstream for ~10 min to keep its cache warm, and its docs state sticky routing is disabled by a
manual provider order. MEASURED 2026-08-28: 5888/5892 input tokens cached on the second call,
$0.00805 → $0.00155 — **5.2x**, on the ~90% of planner input that is stable doctrine prefix.
Throughput-sorting would have bought marginal tps and paid that 5x for it.
**`INFERENCE_CACHE_KEY` is now INERT** (`prompt_cache_key` is OpenAI-specific; OpenRouter ignores
it). Caching needs no key here.
**CAUTION reading a first call:** a cold sticky anchor always reports `cached_tokens: 0`. An
instrumented probe showed 32/5892 and read as "caching is broken"; three controlled repeats showed
99%. Never judge caching off call one.

**CROSS-TICK CACHING IS INTERMITTENT — measured, 2026-08-30, and it corrects the assumption above.**
The ~10-min sticky window implies a 30-min cadence gets no cross-tick reuse. Over the planner's first
17 GLM 5.3 ticks it got plenty, and then none:

| window | turn-1 input | cached | ticks |
|---|---|---|---|
| 17:31 (first call ever) | 6,529 fresh | 0 | 1 |
| 18:00 – 22:00 | 451 fresh | **6,080 (93%)** | 10 |
| 22:30 – 01:30 | 6,531 fresh | **0** | 7 |

Identical prompt, identical total (451 + 6,080 = 6,531) — only the hit changed. Plan for neither
"reliable" nor "absent". WITHIN a tick it is flawless: every turn 2 of all 17 ticks cached exactly
6,528.

**THE CACHED PREFIX IS THE SYSTEM PROMPT + TOOL SCHEMAS (6,080 tokens).** The user message (~451
tokens for one gap) is the variable tail — so **prompt ORDER decides what can cache.** Anything
appended AFTER the gap blocks can never join a cross-tick hit, because the gaps change every tick.
`buildPlannerUserMessage` appends the lessons block last, which was free while it was always empty
and is not free for anything real. Read this before deciding where build 3's memory index goes.

**Measured cost:** a warm 2-turn tick is **0.74¢**, a cold one **1.6¢** — 18–38¢/day per firm at the
pilot's ~24 planned ticks. Also settles a tokenisation question: the repo's 4-chars/token proxy
over-estimated this prompt by ~6% (6,452 estimated vs 6,080 measured for system + schemas).

**`reasoning_effort: 'none'` IS GONE FROM THE TYPE.** GLM 5.3 rejects it —
`400 "Reasoning is mandatory for this endpoint and cannot be disabled."` `minimal`, `low` and
`medium` all succeed (`minimal` returns 0 reasoning tokens, so it IS the replacement). Removing
`'none'` from the union rather than mapping it silently forced every caller to choose at compile
time: the four forced-JSON callers took `minimal`, the planner went `low` → `medium`. Default is
now `medium` and is always sent — an omitted field takes the provider's default, which is nobody's
decision.

**Gemini can never serve the spine** (user ruling): `INFERENCE_MODEL` feeds the planner AND all four
forced-tool callers, so one typo would put a seat model on the planner. Enforced twice — per-request
in `resolveOpenAiEndpoint`, and at the lifecycle's planner step where it becomes a `stepError`. The
second is the point: a per-request throw lands in `runPlanner`'s per-tenant catch and presents as
`tenantErrors`, a config typo wearing a provider outage's clothes.

**Rate ceiling:** OpenRouter applies a per-model `new-account-rpm` cap, ~20 req/min observed. It is
NOT the documented $10 lifetime-credit threshold — the account had **$35 purchased** and still hit
it. 17 rpm sustained 196 consecutive calls cleanly. The planner at twice-an-hour-per-firm is nowhere
near it; bursty callers are the exposure, and OpenRouter **429s rather than queues**.

**Cost:** `openrouter` SKUs at list price, applied to the pilot by hand — Render deploys no
migrations, and the monthly rollup INNER-joins `cost_rates`, so an unpriced SKU makes its rows
VANISH from the rollup rather than show as zero (the 2026-08-06 NVIDIA failure). The cache-write row
is seeded at **$0** for the same reason: OpenRouter publishes no `input_cache_write` for this model.

**Rollback stays one variable:** `MODEL_PROVIDER=anthropic` on both Render services. The Anthropic
SDK path was kept whole — it is the only bypass if OpenRouter ITSELF is down, which is the failure
mode routing everything through one aggregator introduces.

### resolve_date is a CONVENTION, not a crutch (measured 2026-08-28)

`scripts/eval-date-reliability.mjs`, `z-ai/glm-5.3`, n=196, zero API errors, zero truncation:

| condition | score |
|---|---|
| **A** bare prompt, no tool, no guard | **81/98 (82.7%)** |
| **D** bare prompt + `resolve_date` ONLY (no grounding, no guard) | **98/98 (100%)** |

**All 17 bare-prompt misses are disagreements about what an ambiguous English phrase MEANS** — 8×
"next Monday" (GLM answers the coming Monday, the corpus wants the one after), 5× "this Friday" when
today IS Friday, 4× "two weeks from this Monday" (+7 vs +14). **49/49 on every unambiguous
template**, month-end / year-end / leap-adjacent included.

So the tool is not compensating for weak arithmetic. The prompt's *"You are UNRELIABLE at date
arithmetic"* is measurably false, and **49/49 on unambiguous templates is the finding that stands.**

**CORRECTED 2026-08-30 — the rest of this entry was wrong, and in the opposite direction.** It said
the tool "imposes a convention on phrasing that has no single right answer" and warned that deleting
it "starts booking people for the wrong Monday." Both claims fail on inspection of the corpus:

**1. The corpus's `next <weekday>` truth is indefensible on six of its seven anchors.** The rule is
unconditional — `truth = comingOccurrence + 7` (`scripts/eval-date-reliability.mjs:61`), whatever
today is:

| today | corpus says "next Monday" is | days out |
|---|---|---|
| Wednesday | 2026-07-27 | **12** |
| Friday | 2026-07-27 | **10** |
| Sunday | 2026-07-27 | **8** |
| Thursday | 2027-01-11 | **11** |
| Monday | 2026-11-09 | 7 ✓ |

Said on a Wednesday, "next Monday" does not mean twelve days out; it means the Monday five days
away. Only the today-is-Monday anchor is defensible. **GLM was right on those cases and the corpus
marked it wrong.** Of the 17 "misses", the `next <weekday>` class is corpus error; the "this Friday
when today IS Friday" and "two weeks from this Monday" classes remain genuine ambiguity.

**2. Condition D's 100% is circular.** The eval's ground truth and the eval's tool are the same
expression — `addDays(comingOcc(r, wd), 7)` at `:61` versus `addDays(comingOcc(refDate, a.weekday),
w * 7)` at `:106`. D cannot score below 100% as long as the model calls the tool with
`weeksAhead: 1`. It measured *"did the model call the tool"*, never *"was the answer right"*. The
tool agreeing with itself was recorded as the tool being correct.

**3. Production is probably fine, and the eval scored a contract production does not use.** The real
`resolve_date` (`src/mcp/server.ts:372-375`) carries a clause the trimmed eval copy dropped
entirely: *"'next <weekday>' is genuinely ambiguous… resolve BOTH weeksAhead 0 and 1… ask which they
mean before booking or moving anything."* That is the correct handling, and it covers exactly the
class the eval called a model failure.

**What to do with this:** keep the tool (the ambiguity clause is the reason, not the arithmetic), fix
the corpus before quoting any `next <weekday>` number again, and never read a condition-D score as
evidence about correctness while truth and tool share an implementation. **The planner is
unaffected** — it never parses a phrase like this; it copies pre-rendered labels
(see §"date rail proves copying").

**TWO HARNESS TRAPS that manufacture false model verdicts**, both now fixed in the script: an
unpaced pool loses ~95% of calls to the rate cap and prints `0.0%`; and `max_tokens: 300` with
reasoning unset lets the thinking trace eat the answer — the same artifact that once produced a
false "GLM weaker than Haiku" verdict here. An 11% score was reported off both before the user
pushed back. **Read errors and truncation BEFORE reading a date-eval number.**

### The OpenRouter switch — the fleet (2026-09-05)

**Route:** `openrouter-prod / @preset/trades-seats` — an OpenRouter **preset** serving
`meta/muse-spark-1.3` (standard tier; multimodal is the reason) and falling through, per request
with no memory, to `~google/gemini-flash-latest`. Fireworks ran out of credits on 2026-09-04 and
the fleet was inference-dead until this landed. Plan (with every measurement):
`thoughts/shared/plans/2026-09-05-fleet-to-openrouter-muse-spark.md`; research:
`thoughts/shared/research/2026-09-05-fleet-to-openrouter-gemini-3-8-flash.md`.

**Why a preset.** The gateway rewrites every request's `model` to its one configured string and
openclaw has no extra-body hook, so a `models[]` fallback chain cannot be sent by the seats
(NVIDIA/OpenShell#2039). A preset is invoked *as a model id*, which the gateway can carry, and
OpenRouter applies the chain server-side. **The medic therefore never reroutes** — the 08-12
ruling's intent holds — and the second lane is verified daily by a direct probe. Verified live
before the flip: the medic's exact payload with `models:[muse, ~gemini]` fell through to Gemini
(the primary was 403-gated at the time) and passed the tool-call gate.

**Measured, 2026-09-05 (unit costs and one-turn shapes — NOT volumes):** Muse: reasoning
mandatory, medium 160–374 tokens, peaked 478/512 → `max_tokens` 1024 in the probe, 8192 on the
seats; implicit caching engaged 1,137/1,264 on a repeat; probe ≈ $0.0014 cached / $0.0033 not.
Gemini: 570 tokens for the same payload (tokenizer ≈ 2.2x more efficient than Muse's), reasoning
mandatory and NOT controllable (`none` still burned 124), probe ≈ $0.0011. (The "never caches on
this path" claim recorded here on 2026-09-05 morning was WRONG — measured that evening, Gemini
caches implicitly through the gateway's plain requests, session-stickily; see the Gemini caching
entry below.) Muse honours `reasoning_effort`
(minimal 12–90). `~google/gemini-flash-latest` resolves to `google/gemini-3.8-flash` and
retargets on release. Only Meta hosts Muse, only Google hosts Gemini.

**Medic v6:** balance check every tick for FREE (`/api/v1/key` + `/api/v1/credits`, both 200 on
this key though `/credits` is documented management-key-only; floor $2; sources named in the log
so a half-missing watch is visible; NO BALANCE WATCH said loudly); route asserted with
`--no-verify`; the v4 tool-call probe **hourly** (user: "stretch the medic to 60 minutes"),
stamped only on a pass; `served=` logged; fallback probed daily; state in `StateDirectory=`
(RuntimeDirectory is wiped when a oneshot stops); `EnvironmentFile=-`; `TimeoutStartSec=300`.
≈ $2/month vs ≈ $28 had the 5-minute cadence stayed on Muse.

**Seats:** honest model id, `input:["text","image"]`, `contextWindow` 262144 on manager seats
(s2 s3 s6 s7 s8 — five of six) and 131072 on s5, `maxTokens` 8192,
`compat.supportsReasoningEffort` so `thinkingDefault:"adaptive"` → medium and a per-message level
reaches the wire (`/think <level>` on the chat path; `--thinking <level>` on the `nemoclaw … agent`
CLI, which passes `/think` through as plain text). Written by `bootstrap-box.sh wire <s>` with
`NEMOCLAW_CTX_WINDOW`. **Two traps found live (2026-09-05):** openclaw's config `compat` is a
STRICT object — `supportsParallelToolCalls` is not a field, and the s2 canary crash-looped on
`compat: Invalid input` until it was dropped; and the preset must NOT carry `reasoning.effort` —
with the seats sending `reasoning_effort`, OpenRouter's shallow merge kept both keys and returned
`400 … both provided with conflicting values` for every non-medium level (preset v2 removes it).

**The coupling to know:** the fleet now shares the PLANNER's key (user ruling: test suite). A
balance-out stops both in the same minute; the medic's first gate exists for that. Seat spend is
account-level (never itemised in `cost_events`; the rollup nets the spine out of the invoice);
seat requests arrive at OpenRouter untitled (the gateway strips headers) — "untitled = the
fleet". `docs/inference-recovery-runbook.md` is rewritten for this shape.

### Muse Spark latency, double texting, and the photo path (2026-09-05)

**Where a seat turn's time goes (measured on s2, Telegram text turn):** Telegram→gateway 1–3s ·
gateway prep→first model call 1.6s (s7 0.8s) · Muse TTFT ~3s · thinking ~4s (avg 407 reasoning
tokens at ~100 tok/s) · answer ~1.5s · spine tool call via doorman→Render 0.3–0.7s ·
**+3–5s for every additional model call** · reply delivery 2.1–2.7s (preview + edit). ≈13–15s for
a one-tool turn, ≈8s with no tool; model-to-model mean 7.9s, p90 13s over 21 date-eval turns.
The date-eval's 23s/question includes ~11–13s of `nemoclaw … agent` CLI boot (two plugin loads
and a failed pairing handshake) that a Telegram user never pays. Ruled out with measurements:
the egress proxy (0.48s to `api.telegram.org`), tool-schema count (40 schemas ≈ +0s — the old
51s grammar penalty does not exist on this route), prompt size (33k tokens ≈ +1s TTFT), MCP
handshakes (pooled per session, 10-minute idle TTL). Muse's reasoning is MANDATORY
(`reasoning_effort:"none"` → 400): open-ended prose burns 3,465–4,519 reasoning tokens for a
600-token answer and the effort knob barely moves it, while grounded seat turns think 83 (low)
to 727 (high) tokens — effort works where the prompt is specific. Prompt caching: the FIRST call of a fresh session hits 99.4% of the 37.9k-token prefix, but over the whole 82-question run (252 generations, 3.07 calls/question, summed from OpenRouter's own generation records) only **67.7%** of prompt tokens were cached — later calls in a turn carry tool results the cache does not cover — and the run billed **$5.33**: median **$0.064 per question**, $0.021 per call, reasoning 73% of completion tokens. Cache reads ARE billed at the $0.15/M rate (verified: an identical second call's prompt cost fell $0.0345 → $0.0042). Unit costs only — never multiply by this suite's volume.

**Double texting:** OpenClaw's inbound debouncer `messages.inbound.debounceMs` defaults to
**0 = off**, and no seat ever carried a `messages` block. Set to **2000 ms** on all six seats
(user, 2026-09-05 — raised to 5000 for one wire pass that evening, then returned to 2000; the
reasoning is what matters).

**The debounce window does NOT have to cover a person's typing pause, because `steer` does.**
`messages.queue.mode` defaults to `steer` (500 ms, we set nothing): a message that arrives while a
run is ACTIVE is injected into that run after the current tool calls and before the next model
call — one turn, one reply (https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/queue). So the labour splits three
ways: msg 2 before turn 1 starts → the debounce merges it; msg 2 during turn 1 → steer injects it;
msg 2 after turn 1 ends → two replies, which is correct. Only the first sliver needs a window, so
the window stays small. **The cost is per MESSAGE, not per pair** — `scheduleFlush`
(`inbound-debounce.js:126`) resets the timer on every new item, so the window is added to every
inbound text before its turn starts, on top of a turn already measured at ~8–15 s. Owner ruling:
a 5 s wait before every reply is too high a price for a case steer already handles.

**Why the first live test looked like a failure (and was not).** "Hi" then "What is joy's schedule
again" arrived **11.1 s apart** (gateway log 03:27:45.820 → 03:27:56.963) and turn 1 had already
**ENDED at 03:27:54.014** — 2.9 s before the second message landed. There was no overlap to steer
into and no window short of ~12 s would have merged them. The cake pair was the same shape (turn 1
ended 18:00:29.3, msg 2 read 18:00:32.3). Telegram stamps both messages of a pair to the same
MINUTE, which is what made the screenshot look like a bug: **always read the gateway log's
milliseconds, not the chat clock.** STILL UNPROVEN: steer has never been observed firing on this
stack — both natural tests missed the window. The test is to ask a seat something slow (a
multi-tool schedule question) and send a second message while it is still working. Its documented
limit (https://docs.openclaw.ai/concepts/messages): text only — a photo flushes immediately. The queue's default
`steer` mode could not merge the mango-cake pair either: the Telegram handler ran turn 1 inline,
so the follow-up text was not even read until turn 1's reply had gone out (20s after it was sent).

**Photo-then-question costs 44s:** the turn the photo arrives on sends it as native vision input
(40k input tokens, 7.4s, correct). The NEXT turn sees it only as `MediaPath` metadata, so the
agent ran three `exec` probes hunting for the file (~16s) and then a 20.8s `image` tool call — a
second vision pass. Fix is a doctrine line ("a prior photo's `MediaPath` is already in the
transcript — pass it straight to `image`") plus captioning the photo; no config fixes it. A photo
turn also pays ~6s before the agent starts that a text turn does not (11.6s vs 1.6s pre-model),
unexplained at `info` logging — a `logging.level: trace` turn on s2 is the next step.

**s2 is uniquely heavy:** 2,453 sessions, a 14.7 MB `sessions.json`, 204 MB of transcripts (s7:
35 sessions, 235 KB) — every eval question, verifier run and lever check leaves one behind, and
s2's per-turn prep is 2.3–2.6s against s7's 0.8s. Prune `dateval-*`/`verify-*` sessions (never the
live Telegram session) and make the eval harness clean up. Other levers, none applied: skip
`resolve_date` on plain date questions if the eval shows Muse is right in-head (~4–5s), thinking
`low` vs adaptive (~2.5–3s/call, against the medium-default doctrine — measure quality first),
`channels.telegram.streaming: "off"` (~1s, no flicker). Harness defect, unfixed:
`scripts/provider-trial/provider-bench.mjs` counts `delta.reasoning_content` but OpenRouter
streams `delta.reasoning`, so it reports a reasoning model's whole thinking phase as TTFT and a
fake tok/s ("33.7s / 4,498 tok/s" for Muse).

**Gemini 3.8 Flash caching, measured 2026-09-05 (the research doc's "needs explicit breakpoints"
line is superseded — see its amendment):** implicit caching WORKS through the seats' plain
OpenAI-completions requests (fleet eval: call 2 of every session read 28,002/32,260 tokens; direct
probe $0.0207 → $0.0041), but it is **session-sticky** — the first call of every fresh session
missed, because `prompt_cache_key` (= session id) pins a Google replica; Muse's cache is
prefix-global (99.4% on fresh sessions). Explicit `cache_control` beats it (99.98% cached, read
$0.0021/33k, a fresh WRITE billed only $0.0041 — cache-read price + storage, not the docs formula)
but is unreachable from openclaw 2026.6.34 by config: it emits breakpoints only for
`openrouter` + `anthropic/*`, and `compat.cacheControlFormat` is rejected by the strict schema —
a payload-patch plugin would be the route. Unit costs from the eval's first four questions:
Gemini ≈ $0.031/question (call 1 uncached $0.025 + call 2 cached $0.005; reasoning ~300 tokens)
vs Muse's measured $0.064 — cheaper despite the worse cache, on $0.75/M input and short thinking.

### Gemini 3.8 Flash becomes the primary; Muse Spark 1.3 the fallback (2026-09-05, evening)

**User decision, after both models ran the same 82-question date eval on the same Saturday:**
preset `trades-seats` v3 = `["google/gemini-3.8-flash","meta/muse-spark-1.3"]` (both pinned — the
eval gate, the medic's `served_of` and the response's `model` field all compare exact strings, so
the floating `~google/gemini-flash-latest` alias is retired); medic v7 swaps `PRIMARY_MODEL` /
`FALLBACK_MODEL` and nothing else; `bootstrap-box.sh wire` writes the honest id
`google/gemini-3.8-flash` (all six reasoning-effort levels accepted by both lanes, probed);
`scripts/date-eval/plan.mjs` defaults to it.

**The evidence, side by side** (runs `date-eval-runs/2026-09-05-{muse,gemini}`, costs summed from
OpenRouter's generation records, latency from its per-call `latency`/`generation_time`):

| | Muse Spark 1.3 | Gemini 3.8 Flash |
|---|---|---|
| substantive score | 81/82 (D08: read "a week from tomorrow" as the week) | 80/82 (T04/T05: named both readings but leaned and did not ask) |
| weekday↔date contradictions reaching text | 0 | 0 — the guard had nothing to catch on either |
| `resolve_date` bypassed | 0/78 | 0/78 |
| median agent turn (in-sandbox) | 14.1s | 10.2s |
| per call: before first answer token / total | 2.62s / 3.7s | 1.65s / 2.0s |
| reasoning tokens per call (median, p90) | 264, 604 — thinks every call | 0, 474 — thinks on ~⅓ of calls |
| billed | $5.33 = $0.064/question, $0.021/call, 3.07 calls | $3.40 = $0.038/question, $0.012/call, 3.57 calls |
| cache share (whole run) | 67.7% (prefix-global; fresh sessions hit 99.4%) | 61.4% (session-sticky; every fresh session's first call misses) |

**What decided it:** the biggest latency factor is the fixed cost *per model call* before the first
answer token (prefill + mandatory thinking) × ~3 calls a turn — Gemini's is ~1s shorter and it skips
thinking on mechanical calls; that alone is the 11.7s vs 7.9s model-time gap. Accuracy was equal
within noise. Accepted trade-off: Gemini leans on ambiguous dates (T04/T05) where doctrine says ask —
watch it on Telegram. For reference, GLM 5.2 on Fireworks ran ~8s a turn (08-15, ×5), the NVIDIA
94/94 baseline 14.1s; the CLI harness adds ~15s to every run and is not the variable.

**Rollout (done 2026-09-05 20:27–20:45Z):** medic v7 installed (md5 = repo) → preset v3 published
(`POST /api/v1/presets/trades-seats/chat/completions`; a new version is designated on POST) → probe
forced (`rm` the two stamps in `/var/lib/nemoclaw-inference-medic/`): `served=google/gemini-3.8-flash`,
no warning, `fallback lane verified — meta/muse-spark-1.3` → `wire s2` (model entry + the 2000 ms inbound
debounce), canary turn's two generations both `google/gemini-3.8-flash-20260902`/Google → s3 s6 s7 s8
(262144) and s5 (131072). Six-seat check: id, tier, `debounceMs 2000`, gateway listening/ready, Telegram
polling, zero `invalid input`. The bootstrap copy that ran is `/home/ubuntu/Box_Setup_Stuff/bootstrap-box.sh`
(synced from the repo first — md5 `2e80e4b4…`; the other five copies on the box are older vintages).
